# SP6b — Cave biomes (Design)

Date: 2026-11-12
Status: **Approved (2026-11-12)**: the user chose the recommendation on all three Points and on the CI budget (below). Revised after the technical review, the fidelity review and the builder check (`.superpowers/sp6b/review-technical.md`, `review-fidelity.md`, `builder-check.md`; what changed and why: `.superpowers/sp6b/revise-notes.md`). Size: **XL** (about 55 plan tasks; SP6a was planned at 51 and closed at Task 58, SP5c was L at 37). ## Points for the user

The brief leaves every technical and UX choice to this spec (D1-D36, each with its alternative; any of them can be overturned at review). Three choices are the user's: one trades scope against time, one accepts a cost in cave air for the look of the bottom of every cave, and one decides whether SP6b chases a refill target the user set. Each Point says which sections an answer changes.

**The user's answers (2026-11-12, by multiple choice, in Spanish):** Point 1 "XL completa (Recomendado)"; Point 2 "Suelo de roca (Recomendado)" (F1); Point 3 "A SP12, re-medir (Recomendado)". The CI budget of D30 and §13 was also put to the user, because SP6a's ruling R23 deferred the reduction to SP6b and the 3 min 30 s target is relaxed: "5:30, luego 6:00 (Recomendado)" (the work item ≤ 5 min 30 s, Stage 2 joining it if Stage 1 misses; SP6b's exit ≤ 6 min 0 s). The spec's text below already states these choices.

1. **SP6b's size.** Counted as plan tasks: the first commit (1); the CI work item (R23, before any generator change; 4); the seven new blocks (1); the picker, its halo read and its parameters (2); the `caveBiome` condition, the scan's run biomes and the `strata` rule kind (3); the fast path's extensions with their fuzzing (2); T's biome writes, the masked readers and the probes (2); the bottom floor (2); rule 4 in the built-in trees (1); the generator checkpoint (D25, 1), which runs once rule 4, B3's shares per window and C7's bottom parts exist and before B3's border, abyss-rock and grid parts and the `caves-biomes` job; the migration of saved worlds (D29, 1); the metrics B3, its border and abyss-rock parts, the bottom parts and `cave_heavy`'s C1 (4); U2 over the new leaves (1); goldens and the tuning (2); the X-ray by biome, the terrain passes drawing nothing under the X-ray and the strip's overlap (4); cave culling with its checkpoint, the raycast reference, R3 and its pop-in part (7); the map layer, the slice field and the Caves tab with its biome finder (5); the bench rows and two Chrome legs (3); the Firefox run (1); master pins (2); smoke, review links and screenshots (2); the exit and the checks (3). About 55 tasks: **XL**, beyond the master's L (2-3 weeks, master :1792). **Recommendation:** build it as specified and say XL in master §10's SP6b header (§15 item 1). **Alternatives** (either or both):
   - **(a) Drop the additions beyond the brief and the master** (about 2 tasks fewer): the Caves tab's biome finder ("Nearest lush cave", "Nearest dripstone cave", "Nearest abyss", D11), its "Show cave biomes on the map" and "Cave biome parameters" buttons, and the Cross-section's Voxels mode coloured by biome (D10's second half). Neither the brief nor the master's SP6b entry asks for them; the review links (D14), the X-ray by biome and `cave_heavy` find the biomes without them. They are the first and third items of the cut line (§17), which the controller may cut anyway if time runs short.
   - **(b) Move cave culling and R3 (with the raycast reference) to SP11** (Graphics scale-up; about 7 tasks fewer). Culling is invisible, and on the Iris Xe in Chrome the GPU is not the bottleneck at the surface (stream `gpuMs` p50 0.88 ms, p95 2.20 ms against a 4.165 ms frame; recon-render §0.6); its gain is underground and on Firefox. It changes the master's minimum exit for SP6b, which names R3 (:1936).

   With both, about 46 tasks. No alternative reaches L without also moving something the brief puts in SP6b (the X-ray by biome, the `cave_heavy` preset, the bottom floor the user handed here).

   **What the choice changes:** (a): D10, D11, §9.2's last item, §9.3's Find row and two buttons, §12.8's smoke items, §17 items 1 and 3; (b): §8, D13, D22-D24, §12.4, the exit table (§18), §15 items 1, 2 and 17. Both: the size in the header.

2. **The bottom of the world in caves (D6): do you accept the rock floor's cost?** The user: "a menudo se nota demasiado el límite de altura del mundo por debajo y se expone un suelo plano de bedrock en las cuevas amplias". The brief asks that the world's bottom read as natural rock, not a plane (the brief, "The flat bedrock floor"). **Recommendation: a rock floor (F1).** Every cave family and carver closes above a rolling rock floor with mounds and gullies, so wide caves end on deepslate (in the abyss on ash over dark abyss rock), and the bedrock is never seen from a cave. In a wide cavern the floor's air starts at about y −51 in the gullies and up to about y −36 on the mounds, about 17 blocks above the bedrock on average, and its height changes by about 6-8 blocks over 20-50 blocks (§5.2; D25's checkpoint measures it and takes a screenshot before the rest is built on it). **Its cost:** the cave air of the bottom 10-25 blocks goes, about 10-14 % of all cave air and about a third of the abyss's air (**estimate** from recon-gen §1.6 and §2; D25 measures it), so the deepest caves end higher and the abyss, already the rarest biome, gets thinner (B3's 0.5 % per window still applies; §1.6). **Alternatives, which keep the cave air but leave the world's limit visible:** a noisy bedrock layer (F2): caves still reach the bottom, where the bedrock becomes a bumpy black surface 1-8 blocks high instead of a plane; or both (F3): F2's bumpy bedrock under F1's floor, seen only by whoever digs down, with F1's cost.

   **What the choice changes:** D6, §5, §12.3's bottom parts, D25's checkpoint, the SP7 hand-over on lava below y −54 (§16), §15 item 15.

3. **The X-ray refill's 6 s target (ruling R26, D35).** At SP6a's exit a cave Apply with the X-ray on refilled RD 12 in 8,233-8,404 ms on the Iris Xe in Chrome and 8,250 ms in Firefox (the SP6a spec's Exit evidence, :1022, :1188), recorded and not gated; the user moved the 6 s target to "SP6b/SP12". The refill is not short of workers: each T job runs about 2.4 times slower while the page renders (recon-render §6). The only lever likely to reach 6 s is a frame cap while the world regenerates (120 fps on a 240 Hz display) with the per-frame upload budget scaled to the frame interval; its gain is unmeasured. **Recommendation:** hand the 6 s to SP12 (whose entry already says "if SP6b does not reach it", master :2006), take the free lever that SP6b needs anyway (the terrain passes draw nothing while the X-ray is on, D24), and re-measure `xrayApplyRefillMs` in SP6b's Chrome legs. **Alternative:** SP6b runs a measured spike (count the refill's rows in Node, then one L14 run with the cap at 120 and the scaled budget) and gates 6 s if the spike reaches it with `xrayP95` and `xrayMainP95` inside their gates (about 3 more tasks); what the user would see is 120 fps instead of 240 during the few seconds of a refill, with the X-ray's frame gates unchanged.

   **What the choice changes:** D35, §12.6's L14 parts, §16 (SP12), §15 item 5, the size (+3 tasks).

## Goal

Underground, caves now differ by where they lie. Some caves have moss blocks lining their floors and ceilings, with patches of clay on the floor: **lush caves**, under humid ground at moderate depth. Others are lined with warm brown dripstone blocks on floor and ceiling: **dripstone caves**, under ground far inland at moderate depth. Deep under mountains lies **the abyss**: all of its rock is very dark stone, layered with dark shale, cracked stone and thin faintly violet veins, and its floors are grey ash. Nothing lights it but the faint light floor of SP6a, so it is really dark (night vision shows it). Elsewhere caves stay stone and deepslate. Where one cave biome meets another or plain cave, the change is wavy and speckled, never a straight line or a grid. Wide caves no longer end on the flat black bedrock plane: the deepest caves close on a rolling rock floor of mounds and gullies well above it (Point 2). C switches the X-ray between colouring caves by type (the default) and by cave biome; the strip's legend no longer covers the F3 counts. The map gains a cave-biome layer at a chosen depth, the Density tab a "Cave biome" slice, and the Caves tab a "Colour by" choice and a biome finder. The `cave_heavy` preset appears in New world and in the map, with more and larger caves, to find everything fast. Underneath: a cave-biome picker runs per block from the column's climate, its surface estimate and two border noises; T writes each cave voxel's biome into the `cave` byte's high nibble and a per-quart summary into aux B; a new surface rule (rule 4) skins cave floors and ceilings from it; cave culling hides sections that no straight line of sight from the camera can reach. Measured by B3 (biome shares per 8 × 8 km, borders), R3 (0 wrongly culled; ≥ 50 % culled underground), C1's `cave_heavy` part recorded, C1-C9, L2, S1, DT1, DT2, P1 and two Chrome legs. CI first becomes faster (R23). The generated world changes: `GENERATOR_VERSION` 8 → 9 at the one push that changes generated output, and the goldens are re-recorded (D28).

## Non-goals

- **Water, aquifers and lava in caves:** SP7 (the user: "Me parece bien que lleguen en SP7 y SP8"). The abyss gets lava in SP7, not water (§16).
- **Cave decoration and the abyss's crystals and gothic touch:** SP8b (vines, pointed dripstone, glow lichen, the exclusive mineral, the faintly glowing crystals or gems). SP6b's biomes are recognised by their blocks, not their decoration (the brief, guess confirmed).
- **Ores:** SP8b. **Cave and abyss sounds** (the abyss silent): SP8c.
- **Textures** for the new blocks: SP8a; SP6b's blocks are flat colours (the brief).
- **Styling** of the X-ray's biome colours, the legend and the Caves tab: colours are chosen only so biomes can be told apart (the user leaves styling until every SP is done, quoted in `2026-11-01-sp5c-selection-tool-design.md:31`).
- **The full F3 readout, the probe of every stage output and a "freeze culling" toggle:** SP10 (§16).
- **The final cave tuning and `cave_heavy`'s finalisation:** SP12 (master :2004, :2009); SP6b sets `cave_heavy`'s overlay and records its C1.

## What was agreed earlier, and what is only proposed

From the brief, which records the user's words and the items the user saw in a playback and did not correct ("guess, confirmed").
1. **The scope** (agreed at the SP6 split: "Sí, partámoslo en SP6a y SP6b"): the 3D cave-biome picker (lush, dripstone, abyss) with cave floor and ceiling rules, the abyss palette, the X-ray coloured by cave biome, the `cave_heavy` preset; with master §10's additions (the map cave-biome layer, cave culling with R3, B3, C1's `cave_heavy` recorded, C1-C9, L2, S1, DT1, DT2 re-asserted, P1 re-measured, `GENERATOR_VERSION` bumped, new states in the clipboard).
2. **Variety and characteristic blocks** (the user): "Los biomas darían variedad y bloques característicos a las diferentes cuevas."
3. **The abyss** (the user, master D14): "Oscuro y silencioso, decorado por elementos con un ligero toque gótico (SP8b) y puede ser iluminado un poco por gemas o cristales exclusivos de este bioma, y la propia lava que también emite luz".
4. **Blocks in SP6b, decoration later** (guess, confirmed): the abyss palette (very dark stone with faint veins, cracked variants, shale strata, ash floors; flat colours until SP8a's textures), lush blocks (moss, clay) and dripstone blocks; **the three zones are recognised by their blocks, not their decoration**. Hand-overs: SP8b, the crystals or gems and the gothic touch; SP8c, the abyss silent (little sound, not unsettling music); SP7, no water in the abyss but lava may appear and light it a little. The abyss is really dark from SP6b, with SP6a's light floor 0.02.
5. **The X-ray** (guess, confirmed): coloured by cave type by default, **by cave biome as another option**.
6. **`cave_heavy` as a convenient start for the review** (guess, confirmed).
7. **Ores, aquifers, springs and lava lakes later** (the user): "Me parece bien que lleguen en SP7 y SP8". SP6b's caves stay empty of them.
8. **The flat bedrock floor** (the user, SP6a's check, ruling R33): "a menudo se nota demasiado el límite de altura del mundo por debajo y se expone un suelo plano de bedrock en las cuevas amplias" and "quizás es mejor aplazarlo a SP6b". The candidate shapes in the brief are the controller's; the choice is Point 2.
9. **Anti-goals** (the user's words, carried from SP6): "Patrones excesivamente predecibles" and "encontrar muchos cortes abruptos en la generación"; for biomes the brief (controller's reading) adds no regular grid of zones, no straight borders, no flat cut where one biome's blocks meet another's; no stutter in caves or while generating; X-ray fluid.
10. **Success** (controller's proposal, as SP6a's): go down into caves and recognise lush, dripstone and abyss by their blocks; the bedrock floor no longer shows as a flat plane. Before asking the user, the controller checks the metrics, the absence of stutter, the screenshots and the production check in Chrome and Firefox (the user's Firefox by hand).
11. **Process** (as SP5c and SP6a): spec with reviews, Points for the user, approval, plan, subagent-driven execution, merge after the user's go-ahead, production check, the user's check, "docs: SP6b complete".

Everything below that is not one of these items is this spec's proposal.

## Decisions taken by this spec (for the user's review)

### Decisions with a visible effect

- **D1 Three cave biomes, told apart by their blocks** (§3, §4). *You notice:* **lush** caves have moss_block floors two blocks deep, with clay patches on about a fifth of the floor, and moss on about seven tenths of their ceilings; **dripstone** caves have dripstone_block floors and ceilings two blocks deep, with bare stone patches on about a sixth; **the abyss** is dark rock all through (D4), with abyss_ash floors on about four fifths of the floor. Walls of lush and dripstone caves stay stone or deepslate (D5). Plain caves are unchanged. Seven new blocks (D36); one cracked block, cracked_abyss_stone, stands for the brief's "cracked variants" (a second, for example a cracked shale, is a one-line addition at tuning if the review asks for it). Alternative: one block per biome (moss, dripstone, abyss_stone), which makes each biome recognisable but drops the brief's "cracked variants, shale strata, ash floors".
- **D2 Where each cave biome lies** (§1). *You notice:* lush caves under humid ground and dripstone caves under ground far inland, both between about 26 and 115 blocks under the surface; the abyss deep under mountains (at least about 128 blocks under the surface, never under the sea). Measured with the master's boxes on today's caves (no bottom floor yet): lush 17.6 %, dripstone 13.4 %, abyss 5.6 % and plain 63.4 % of cave air (recon-gen §2). The master's registry (:770-775) is kept as the defaults. Where lush and dripstone overlap, the one deeper inside its box wins, so the border runs midway. Depth is measured from the column's mean surface height (`surfaceEst`, `src/gen/column/columnStage.ts:105`), so it does not jump at a cliff. Alternative: depth from the local pre-cave top (`surfaceEst3`), which follows cliffs and overhangs but makes biome borders jump where the terrain does.
- **D3 Borders: wavy and speckled, no grid** (§1.3-§1.4). *You notice:* a cave biome ends along a wavy line on the floor, never straight; in depth it ends along a wavy surface; at the line, over about 4-5 blocks, blocks of the two biomes mix in a speckle, so neither carpet ends on a clean edge. Two 2D border noises (λ 64 and 96) shift the climate and the depth that the picker reads, and a per-block jitter of ±2 blocks blurs the line. There is no quart grid at all: the biome is computed per block. Alternative: the master's per-3D-quart picker with a 3D jittered-Voronoi zoom (as the surface biomes' 2D zoom), which gives 3D lobes but costs about twice as much per column and needs the zoom to keep borders off the quart planes (D16).
- **D4 The abyss: dark rock all through, really dark** (§3.3-§3.4). *You notice:* in the abyss, not only the cave skins but all the rock below the abyss's upper surface is abyss rock: abyss_stone layered with abyss_shale, cracked_abyss_stone and thin abyss_stone_veined lines, the layers tilted gently by a noise, so walls, floors and a dug shaft all read as the abyss; the upper surface is the same wavy, speckled border the cave air uses, so the abyss rock and the X-ray's abyss colour agree. Its floors are ash, grey and a little lighter than deepslate ([92, 88, 86] against [78, 78, 84], §4), so the ash floor is the abyss's lightest surface; this is this spec's choice (the brief gives ash no colour), and a darker ash (darker than deepslate, still ≥ 24 from every colour by §4's pairwise rule) is the alternative if the review finds it breaks "very dark". No new block emits light, so the abyss is lit only by SP6a's light floor 0.02 (really dark; night vision, L, shows it) until SP7's lava and SP8b's crystals. Its blocks use the `SOUND` group `abyss` (`src/world/blocks/kinds.ts:79`), unused until now; the silence itself is SP8c's. Alternative: skins only (floors and ceilings), as lush and dripstone; steep abyss walls then stay deepslate, and a vertical shaft into the abyss shows no abyss rock.
- **D5 Lush and dripstone: skins only** (§3.4). *You notice:* floors and ceilings carry the biome's blocks; vertical walls stay stone or deepslate (Minecraft's look for both). Alternative: a third skin on walls, which needs a horizontal-distance condition the rule engine lacks (the scan is per column, `src/gen/surface/scan.ts:181-232`).
- **D6 The bottom of caves: a rock floor** (§5; Point 2). *You notice:* no cave shows the flat bedrock plane; the deepest caves close on a rolling deepslate floor of mounds and gullies, in the abyss on ash over dark rock, on average about 17 blocks above the bedrock (in a wide cavern the air starts at about y −53 in the gullies and up to about y −32 on the mounds; the floor's height changes by about 9-11 blocks over 20-50 blocks; no cave air below y −55 anywhere); smaller caves close rounded at varied heights above it. Digging down still reaches the bedrock at y −64 with SP3c's dither above it. Alternative: Point 2's noisy bedrock layer (F2), or both (F3).
- **D7 The X-ray by cave biome** (§7). *You notice:* C (without Ctrl; Ctrl+C stays Copy) switches the X-ray between "by cave type" (the default) and "by cave biome". In biome mode lush caves show bright green, dripstone amber, the abyss violet and plain caves a dim grey-blue, so the three biomes stand out; the surface film stays grey. The strip and the F3 line read "X-ray · y −64 … 319 · by cave biome" and the legend switches to Plain cave, Lush, Dripstone, Abyss and Surface. The switch is instant, works with the X-ray off (it shows at the next X) and lasts for the page (not saved, like the band). Alternative: no key, only the Caves tab's "Colour by" (the map must then be open to switch); or a `WorldSettings` field remembered like night vision.
- **D8 The strip no longer covers the F3 counts** (§9.5). *You notice:* with F3 on, the X-ray strip sits to the right of the counts when there is room and below them when not; with F3 off it stays centred at the top. Fixes SP6a's production-check finding ("the X-ray strip's legend overlaps the F3 HUD's first lines"). Alternative: move the strip to the top right while the HUD shows (a `:has()` rule as `src/ui/styles.css:63`), which still overlaps on a 960 px side-by-side host.
- **D9 The map's cave-biome layer** (§9.1). *You notice:* the map's layer select gains "caveBiome"; with it a "y" slider and number field (−64 … 319, step 4, default 0) appear in the toolbar; each pixel shows the cave biome a cave at that y would have there (lush green, dripstone amber, abyss violet, plain grey), and the relief greyed where y is above the surface estimate. The hover readout adds "cave biome at y n: lush (depth 0.42)". It can differ from the blocks by a few blocks: inside the 4-5-block speckle, and because the map reads each pixel's exact climate and surface estimate where T interpolates them between quart corners (§9.1); it says nothing about whether a cave is there (the slice and the X-ray show the air). Alternative: no map layer, only the slice field and its square on the map (SP6a's pattern for the cave slice, SP6a D13); it drops the master's "map cave-biome layer" (:1932).
- **D10 The slice field "Cave biome"** (§9.2). *You notice:* the Density tab's field select gains "Cave biome" on both planes: cave air coloured by its biome, other solid pale grey, open air the sky colour, with a legend of the biomes present; it redraws on release, as "Cave type" does. With the Caves tab in biome mode, "Cave slice" opens it. The Cross-section's Voxels mode follows the X-ray's colour mode for dry cave air (an addition beyond the brief and the master, Point 1 (a)). Alternative: a colour switch on the existing "Cave type" field (one job, two values per sample).
- **D11 The Caves tab** (§9.3). *You notice:* under the X-ray checkbox, "Colour by: Cave type / Cave biome" (in step with C); "Show cave biomes on the map" (sets the map's caveBiome layer at the band's middle y); "Cave biome parameters" (opens Parameters › Caves › Cave biomes); and a second Find row, "Nearest lush cave", "Nearest dripstone cave", "Nearest abyss", with the same result line, cancel rules and "Save as place" as the kinds' row, searching within 1,024 blocks in 2 s (the buttons are on the cut line, §17). Everything in this item but "Colour by" is an addition beyond the brief and the master (Point 1 (a)). Alternative: no biome finder (the review links and the map layer find biomes).
- **D12 `cave_heavy`** (§6.2). *You notice:* "cave_heavy" appears in New world's preset list and in the map's profile select (no UI code: readiness follows `CURRENT_SP`, `src/core/params/profiles.ts:41-43`). A world made with it has larger and more connected caverns, more entrance tunnels, noodles up to y 96 and half again as many worms, underground worms and ravines, so every kind and biome is close to the spawn. Its cave biomes are the default's. Alternative: also widen the biome boxes in `cave_heavy`, recommended only if D25's checkpoint finds the abyss rare at seed 42.
- **D13 Cave culling** (§8). *You notice:* nothing, by design: underground, sections no line of sight can reach are not drawn, which saves GPU time (recorded in L13); nothing pops in or out: a section is hidden only once a complete walk has shown it unreachable from every eye position within one section of the walk's start, the culler draws everything while the camera is farther than that from the last completed walk's start or after an edit opens a new path, and nothing is culled while the X-ray is on (§8.1-§8.2; `R3.popIn` checks it, §12.4). Alternative: none in SP6b (Point 1 (b): SP11).
- **D14 Finding each biome for the review** (§12.8). *You notice:* before the user's check, the controller hands over one link per cave biome (lush, dripstone, abyss), one at a biome border, one at the widest bottom cavern, and one for `cave_heavy`'s spawn; each opens in flight at RD 12. Alternative: `cave_heavy` and the finder only.
- **D15 The cave-biome parameters on the map** (§6.1). *You notice:* Parameters › Caves gains, at its end, a "Floor" sub-section and then a "Cave biomes" sub-section (with Lush, Dripstone, Abyss, Borders and its noises), each leaf with a Terrain badge; "Floor" holds "Floor y", "Floor relief" and "Floor softness", and "Cave noises" gains "Floor noise" at its end. A change regenerates the world around you as a cave edit does. The leaves live under `caves.biomes.*`, `caves.floor.*` and `caves.noises.floor`, which the terrain stage already hashes (`src/core/stage/registry.ts:20`). Alternative: a new top-level group `caveBiomes` with its own stage before `terrain`, so the map's cave-biome tiles redraw only on cave-biome edits (recon-ui B1); it adds a stage, a schema group and a registry row for a saving the user sees only with that layer open.

### Technical decisions (no visible effect)

- **D16 The picker runs per block from per-position fields** (§1). Every climate field is 2D (`src/gen/column/climate.ts` `sampleClimate`; recon-gen §1.1) and depth is linear in y, so with 2D border noises each position's biome changes along y at most at a few heights. The picker is O(1) per voxel after a per-column pass over the (16 + 2J)² positions the jitter can reach. It amends master §3.10's "per 3D quart" (:794); `caveBiomeQ` keeps a per-quart summary (D17). Cost **estimate** 0.1-0.2 ms per column (§1.6). Alternative: the master's 3D quart picker with a 3D jittered-Voronoi zoom (recon-gen P-A; **estimate** 0.03-0.4 ms per column plus 8 hashes per voxel where lattice biomes differ), which needs `hash3` and three derived seeds and still needs the jitter to keep borders off the quart planes.
- **D17 The biome is stored in the `cave` byte's high nibble** (§2). T writes the cave biome (0 plain, 1 lush, 2 dripstone, 3 abyss) into bits 4-7 of the `cave` byte at every tagged voxel; the bits are reserved and 0 today (`src/core/constants.ts:169-181`; master :351). The X-ray, B3 and the finder read it exactly; aux B's `caveBiomeQ` holds 0 for a quart with no cave air, else 1 + the biome most of its cave-air voxels have. Alternative: the SP6a spec §14's hand-over, the mesh job reading `caveBiomeQ` from aux B; it is per quart, so the X-ray would show a 4-block staircase where the blocks follow D3's per-block border.
- **D18 The `caveBiome` condition** (§3.2): `caveBiome{side: 'floor' | 'ceiling' | 'here', biomes}`. `floor` reads the biome of the voxel above the run's top and holds only if that voxel is tagged cave air; `ceiling` reads the voxel below the run's bottom with the same requirement, so a run resting on the bedrock never qualifies (closing the trap of `stoneDepth{ceiling}` firing at a run's bottom on bedrock, recon-gen §1.4); `here` evaluates the picker at the voxel. The scan gains per-run `floorBiome` and `ceilBiome`. Alternative: `here` only (floors read the rock's own biome, so a moss floor could appear under a plain cave whose floor lies just inside the lush box).
- **D19 A `strata` rule kind** (§3.3): a data band table (states, weights, run lengths) shifted per position by a 2D noise, the generalisation of SP3c's `bandlands` (`src/gen/surface/bands.ts:1-10`; `src/gen/surface/compile.ts:314-329`). The abyss's rock uses it. Alternative: a second hard-coded `bandlands`-like leaf for the abyss (no data table; the panel cannot edit its layers).
- **D20 Four fast-path extensions, output-preserving** (§3.5): ceiling-bounded leaves, a separate depth bound for runs without sky, a biome-gated skip, and a per-position band classification for `caveBiome{here}`. Without them rule 4 drops the default tree off the fast path and every solid voxel is evaluated: **measured** +1.0 ms p50, +1.4 ms p90 on T (recon-gen §1.7), half of the headroom under the 13 ms gate. All four are fuzzed against full evaluation, as SP3c's fast path is (`src/gen/surface/compile.ts:12-26`). Alternative: accept the cost and raise the T gate.
- **D21 Rule 4's under-water branches go to SP7; clay by patch** (§3.4). No cave water exists before aquifers (water v0 fills only `base − 12 < y ≤ waterTop`, and the wet roof keeps caves 16-20 blocks under every water bed, SP6a §1.4), so the master's "clay under water (lush)" and "gravel under cave water" would match no voxel and fail U2 as dead leaves. SP7 adds them with aquifers; lush floors get clay from a noise patch now. Alternative: write the two branches now as dead data with a U2 exemption.
- **D22 Cave culling: a monotone walk with entry faces, no frustum, driving a draw filter** (§8). From the 3 × 3 × 3 sections around the camera's, the walk steps +x only from sections at or east of the box's west layer, −x only at or west of its east layer, and likewise on y and z; it carries per section the set of faces it was entered through and exits through the faces `connectivity15` joins to them (`src/mesh/connectivity.ts:24-30`, `:58-100`). Every section a straight ray from any eye in the box reaches is then reached (§8.2), so R3's "0 wrongly culled" holds by construction, which the master's `dot(face, fromCamera) ≥ 0` test on section centres does not near the camera, and a completed walk stays valid while the camera moves within one section; where it cannot be valid (the camera left the box, a wall dug open) everything draws until the next walk completes, so nothing pops in. Columns within RD that are not yet revealed count as open. With no frustum, the result does not depend on the view direction: it re-runs only on a section change, a connectivity change or after reveals, time-sliced at 0.5 ms per frame. Its result filters the batches' multi-draw lists after three's own culling; the reveal state (`setVisibleAt`, `src/render/regionBatch.ts:213-219`, `:334-344`) is untouched, so R7's probe stays exact. The meta bits 17-31 stay 0: the walk reads `connectivity15` from the staging (`src/render/regionGrid.ts:124-153`). Alternative: the master's form (dot rule inside the frustum, re-run on a turn of more than 10°, 1.5 ms budget, driving `setVisibleAt`), which visits about a third of the sections but can hide a section a ray reaches and pops in at the screen's edges while turning unless its frustum is widened.
- **D23 RegionMesh (Firefox) culls whole region arenas** (§8.4). RegionMesh draws one range per region and pass (`src/render/regionMesh.ts:322`), so per-section culling would cost index writes; an arena none of whose sections the walk reached is given the draw range (0, 0), which keeps its uploads flowing. Alternative: `mesh.visible = false`, which saves the draw call but makes three skip uploads, so leaving a cave uploads whole attributes past `HIDDEN_RANGES_MAX` (`src/render/regionMesh.ts:54`, `:247-258`).
- **D24 The terrain and water passes draw nothing while the X-ray is on** (§7.4). Today they are drawn with every vertex collapsed (`src/render/materials/glsl/terrain.glsl.ts:78`, `water.glsl.ts:30`); with the X-ray on, `gpuMs` p50 is 3.31 ms against 1.97 ms off (`bench-iris-chrome.json` `xray.rd12.gpuMs`; recon-render §1.4). RegionBatch sets their multi-draw count to 0, mirroring the X-ray batches (`src/render/regionBatch.ts:172-179`); RegionMesh gives them the draw range (0, 0). It frees GPU time for the refill (Point 3) and is a precondition for skipping culling while the X-ray is on. Alternative: keep the collapse.
- **D25 Two measuring checkpoints before the rest is built on them** (§12.5). **The generator checkpoint**, after the picker, rule 4 and the bottom floor exist in a local build: B3's shares per 8 × 8 km window and per depth band, the abyss's air with and without the floor, `terrain.real` and `terrain.islands`, the picker's per-column cost, C1, C4's lowest band and `floorPeak`, C5's thinnest, C7's `flatWallShare`, the new floor's relief (`C7.bottomFloorSpread` and a screenshot) and `C7.bedrockExposed` on every group. **The culling checkpoint**, on the pure walk over cached regions: `R3.culledUnderground` and the walk's cost. Each has a rule (§12.5) with its levers in order. Alternative: no checkpoints; misses found at the exit.
- **D26 The metrics: parts of existing ids** (§12). B3 gains its master part per 8 × 8 km window on the default profile's T1 scatter and border parts (lattice, straightness, aperiodicity); C7 gains the bottom parts; R3 its two master parts and a pop-in part in `test/metrics/caves.metric.ts`'s new `caves-biomes` group; DT2 a `caveBiomeProbe` part; R1's X-ray part checks the biome bits. A part is gated from its commit only when it holds by construction (`R3.wronglyCulled`, `R3.popIn`, `C7.bottomPlane`, `DT2.caveBiomeProbe`, `R1.xrayBiome`; `C7.bedrockExposed` from the commit after the floor's, once it reads 0 on every group, §12.3); every other new part is written to `.out` at its commit and gated at the tuning task at its bound (the master's for B3 and R3, this spec's proposal for the rest) or at a bound the user approves with the measurements (SP6a D26's rule). Alternative: a new id `B6` "cave-biome borders" (needs `src/core/ids.ts:33` and a master row).
- **D27 The T gates stay 13 / 20 and 14.5 / 26.5** (§12.5). `TERRAIN_P50_MAX_MS` 13, `TERRAIN_P99_MAX_MS` 20, `ISLANDS_P50_MAX_MS` 14.5, `ISLANDS_P99_MAX_MS` 26.5 (`test/bench/gates.ts:15-18`; ruling R14) are re-asserted with cave biomes (the SP6a hand-over lets SP6b keep or change its own gate: "T ≤ 13 ms p50 as ruling R14 set it, unless SP6b decides otherwise (ruling R15 (c))", the SP6a spec :596). Master §10 SP7's exit "P1: T still ≤ 10 ms" (:1943), written before R14, is left as it stands: it is a later SP's target, which the user approved, so SP7's spec reconciles it; SP6b's §16 note to SP7 states the gap (13 ms in force since R14). Alternative (the first draft's): reword SP7's exit to "T within the gate in force" now, which would lower a later SP's bar without asking the user.
- **D28 `GENERATOR_VERSION` 8 → 9: one output-changing push** (§14.3). The bottom floor, the picker's writes, rule 4 and the tuning change generated output and land in one push, recorded from its base; work that changes no generated output (the CI item, the blocks, the engine's new condition and rule kind before any built-in tree uses them, culling, the X-ray, the UI) is pushed before and after it at will. The floor and the abyss meet (D6 removes part of the abyss's air), so one golden record after their joint tuning is the honest one. 9 at the exit; 10 if the user's check asks for a retune. Alternative: two pushes (the floor, then the biomes; 10 at the exit), which gives CI on the generator commits earlier.
- **D29 Saved worlds move to SP6b's trees** (§14.4). `SCHEMA_VERSION` 4 → 5 with a migration, SP6a D36's pattern: a stored `density.defs` canonically equal to one of the three built-in trees at `GENERATOR_VERSION` 8 (pinned in a new fixture; SP6a's own fixture stays unchanged) becomes SP6b's tree of that kind (the floor's terms); a stored `surface.rules` equal to one of the built-in surface trees (default, floating_islands) becomes SP6b's (rule 4); an edited tree is kept; missing new leaves take the profile's values. Alternative: no migration (worlds saved before SP6b keep a flat bedrock floor and plain caves, and the world list's "opens with today's terrain" becomes false).
- **D30 The CI work item comes first** (§13). Before any generator change: the six exact levers of recon-tests §4 Stage 1 (`apply.metric.ts` beside the default profile's terrain job; T1-T5, S1-S3 and caves split by profile or group; T1's analysis and caves' `measure` moved into worker threads), no sample reductions (ruling R23). Budget: the work item ≤ 5 min 30 s wall in two consecutive green cold runs (Stage 2's items join the work item in order if Stage 1 misses it); SP6b's exit ≤ 6 min 0 s on cold runs, with SP6b's regions and R3 in a 19th job, `caves-biomes`. SP6a's original 3 min 30 s is out of reach without sample reductions (Stage 1 expects 3:45-4:15 on fast runners, 5:00-5:20 on slow ones; §13 says why these numbers). Stage 2 (column slices with a merge job, further unit and fast shards) is the named fallback. Alternative: keep SP6a's 9 min budget and split only if SP6b's additions break it; or 5 min 0 s at the exit, with Stage 2 from the start.
- **D31 Every Chrome route re-measured into `assets/sp6b/`** (§12.6). Culling acts on every frame and the X-ray gains a mode, so L13 (with culling, and a culling-off segment) and L14 (with a biome-mode segment) are re-run, and the other G1 cells are re-read. Alternative: only L13 and L14; the other cells read from `assets/sp6a/`.
- **D32 Items handed on rather than taken** (§16): `C6.latticeBias`'s 1.1 (≤ 1.09 on Task 19's W9 measure, §12.3) with its structural levers → SP12 (they retune the cave shapes, SP12's "final cave tuning", master :2006); `slice.caves128`'s `deriveSeed` memo → taken only if SP6b's slice field or picker pushes `slice.caves128` over 300 ms, else SP12; `replyDuringFlushP95Ms`'s flake → SP10 (no SP6b change touches L11's path); RegionMesh's buffer policy (Firefox) → SP11 (D23 touches no buffer); "Edit in Density" below the fold (`docs/backlog.md:29`) stays in the backlog (the user did not ask for it); the F3 cave-biome line, the probe's cave biome and the "freeze culling" toggle → SP10. Alternative per item in §16.
- **D33 The heavy files and the throttled machine** (§12.9). SP6a's ruling R18 is carried: a timeout-only failure that passes alone counts as green. `metricTest`'s timeout (`test/harness/metric.ts:81`) is scaled by a `METRICS_TIMEOUT_SCALE` environment value (default 1; the plan's full-tier step sets 3), a harness setting, not a gate. The full tier's four heavy files (C7, DT1, S1, T1) run one at a time. Alternative: R18 only (T1 at about 750 s and C7 at about 900 s now exceed 600 s even alone, recon-tests §2).
- **D34 SP6b is XL** (Point 1). Alternative: Point 1's trimmed scope.
- **D35 The refill's 6 s** (Point 3). Recommendation: → SP12, re-measured in SP6b. Alternative: SP6b's measured spike.
- **D36 Seven new blocks, appended** (§4): moss_block, dripstone_block, abyss_stone, abyss_stone_veined, cracked_abyss_stone, abyss_shale, abyss_ash, state ids 58-64 (`test/stateIds.lock.json` holds 0-57), property-less full cubes through the `terrain()` helper (`src/world/blocks/defs.ts:14-22`), carvable, in the creative palette's terrain category (`src/world/blocks/tags.ts:33-34`, `:47`). No new state rotates, so the clipboard needs only a round trip by state key (`src/persist/clipboard.ts:4`, `:57`; SP5c §14). Alternative: reuse existing blocks (mossy look from green wool, dripstone from terracotta), which breaks "bloques característicos".

## Module layout after SP6b


## 1. The cave-biome picker (D2, D3, D16)

### 1.1 Ids and colours

`CAVE_BIOME = { plain: 0, lush: 1, dripstone: 2, abyss: 3 }` in `src/core/constants.ts`, beside `CAVE_KIND` (gen exports no numeric data, `src/core/constants.ts:169-176`); 4-15 reserved. `CAVE_BIOME_COLORS` (sRGB) holds the X-ray's and the map's colours: plain `#6f86a6`, lush `#4dff7a`, dripstone `#ffb347`, abyss `#b05cff` (chosen to stand out under additive blending on #0b0e14, `src/render/materials/glsl/xray.glsl.ts:63-66`; a dark abyss colour would add almost nothing there; recon-ui B4). The X-ray, the slice field, the Voxels mode and the map layer read this one table, so all four agree.

### 1.2 Inputs per position

For a column with ColumnSample `s` and a position p = (x, z) within J blocks of it (J = `caves.biomes.edgeJitter`, 2; the sample's 7 × 7 lattice with its one-quart halo, `src/gen/column/columnStage.ts:1-4`, `:61-62`, covers 4 blocks on each side, so J ≤ 4 is the halo's limit and the leaf's range):
- `C(p)`, `E(p)`, `H(p)`: `readFieldHalo(s, field, x, z)`, a new quart-bilinear read over the whole lattice, cell indices i, j ∈ −1 … 4, of the CDF-uniform climate fields on [−0.9973, 0.9973] (master :580). It is needed because `readField` (`src/gen/column/columnStage.ts:171-185`) clamps the cell index to the column's cells 0-3 (`:176-177`), so outside the column it extrapolates from the edge cell instead of reading the halo, and two neighbouring columns would compute different values for one position (a seam at every chunk border). `readFieldHalo` uses `readField`'s corner order and lerp order, so it equals `readField` bit for bit inside the column, and two columns that share a position use the same corners and the same fractions, so they agree bit for bit (a unit test over every shared halo position of two neighbours, for C, E, H, `surfaceEst` and Y0). The picker, `caveBiomeAt`, the probes and §3.5's classification all use it.
- `Nb_C(p)`, `Nb_E(p)`, `Nb_H(p)`: the noise `caves.biomes.noises.border` (2D, λ 64, 2 octaves) as three named instances, `new NormalNoise(ctx.seed, 'caveBiome.C' | 'caveBiome.E' | 'caveBiome.H', params.caves.biomes.noises.border)` (a `NormalNoise` derives its octaves' seeds from (world, name), `src/core/noise/normal.ts:21-24`, so the names, not `deriveSeed` integers, set them; the plan's Spec correction 11), each as z2 / clampSigma clamped to [−1, 1].
- `Nd(p)`: the schema instance `caves.biomes.noises.depth` (2D, λ 96, 2 octaves; `noiseFor(ctx, 'caves.biomes.noises.depth')`), z2 / clampSigma clamped to [−1, 1].
- The jitter's `hash3` seed stays `deriveSeed(seed, 'caveBiome.jitter')` (§1.4). `cavePickerOf(ctx)` (`src/gen/biomes/cavePicker.ts`) builds these inputs once per GenContext, its settings `params.caves.biomes` minus `noises`.
- The perturbed fields: `C′ = C + w·Nb_C`, `E′ = E + w·Nb_E`, `H′ = H + w·Nb_H`, with w = `caves.biomes.borderWarp` (0.05); the climate gradient is about 1/1000 per block, so 0.05 moves a border by up to about ±50 blocks in wiggles of about 64 blocks (**estimate**; B3's `straightBorder` measures the result).
- `Y0(p) = surfaceEst(p) + dW·Nd(p)`, with `surfaceEst(p)` = `readFieldHalo(s, 'surfaceEst', x, z)` (the 2D mean height, the lattice's `l.offset`, `src/gen/column/columnStage.ts:105`) and dW = `caves.biomes.depthWarp` (10 blocks).
- Depth at height y: `depth(p, y) = (Y0(p) − y) / D`, D = `caves.biomes.depthScale` (128, master :586).

The column pass computes these for the (16 + 2J)² = 400 positions once, into preallocated arrays, in a fixed operation order, so two columns compute the same doubles for a shared position (DT1; the rule of `src/gen/column/columnStage.ts:1-4`).

### 1.3 Boxes and the choice

| biome | inside when | margin (uniform units) |
|---|---|---|
| lush | H′ ≥ `lush.hMin` (0.4) and `lush.depthMin` (0.2) ≤ depth ≤ `lush.depthMax` (0.9) | H′ − hMin |
| dripstone | C′ ≥ `dripstone.cMin` (0.6) and `dripstone.depthMin` (0.2) ≤ depth ≤ `dripstone.depthMax` (0.9) | C′ − cMin |
| abyss | E′ ≤ `abyss.eMax` (−0.5) and C′ > `abyss.cMin` (−0.1) and depth ≥ `abyss.depthMin` (1.0) | min(eMax − E′, C′ − cMin) |

The values are the master's registry (:770-775). Among the boxes that contain the point, the one with the largest margin wins; a tie goes to the lower id; in none, plain. With the defaults lush and dripstone overlap where H′ ≥ 0.4 and C′ ≥ 0.6, and their border then runs where H′ − 0.4 = C′ − 0.6, a curve between two independent noise fields; at the defaults the abyss's depth range is disjoint from theirs (a gap of 0.1 depth, about 13 blocks), but the panel's ranges (§6.1) let an edit make any boxes overlap, and the margin rule then decides there too. In the offset splines low E is high ground (`src/core/params/shapeDefaults.ts:15-21`: at E −1 the offset is 72-130 at C 0.05, 80-175 at C 0.3 and 90-210 at C 0.6, rising with PV), so the abyss lies under mountains and uplands. It never holds cave air under the sea: a sea column has surfaceEst < 63, so depth ≥ 1.0 needs y < 63 + 10 − 128 = −55, not above the rock floor's lowest air (−55, §5.2).

### 1.4 The per-block jitter

For a voxel v = (x, y, z): three integers jx, jy, jz ∈ [−J, J] from h = `hash3(deriveSeed(seed, 'caveBiome.jitter'), x, y, z) >>> 0` (`src/core/hash.ts:32`): jx = ((h & 1023)·(2J + 1)) >>> 10 − J, jy = (((h >>> 10) & 1023)·(2J + 1)) >>> 10 − J, jz = (((h >>> 20) & 1023)·(2J + 1)) >>> 10 − J (three disjoint 10-bit fields; each value's probability is within 1/1024 of 1/(2J + 1)); `caveBiomeAt(v)` is §1.3's choice at position p′ = (x + jx, z + jz) and height y + jy, read from the column pass's arrays. Borders become a speckle about 2J + 1 = 5 blocks wide, in 3D. J = 0 turns the speckle off. The function is pure in (seed, params, x, y, z).

### 1.5 Where it is evaluated

- At every tagged voxel (the `cave` byte's kind ≠ 0) in T's cave-biome step (§2.1): about 2.3k voxels per column on average (**measured** 9.1 per position, recon-gen §3.3).
- At the floors and ceilings of runs: from the tagged voxel above or below (D18), so no extra evaluation.
- For `caveBiome{here}` (the abyss's rock): through §3.5's per-position classification, evaluated per voxel only within J of the abyss border.
- In the map layer (no jitter: the pixel's position at the chosen y), the slice field, the probe and the finder.

### 1.6 Expected shares and cost

- **Shares** (measured with the master's raw boxes, no perturbation, no floor; recon-gen §2): lush 17.6 %, dripstone 13.4 %, abyss 5.6 %, plain 63.4 % of cave air. The abyss box holds 12.8 % of columns, whose offsets are p10 72, p50 109, p90 169, so the abyss starts at y ≤ −56 / −19 / +41. Abyss air by 16-block band: −64…−49 49.4k, −48…−33 51.6k, −32…−17 36.3k, −16…−1 24.3k, 0…15 19.0k, 16…31 9.6k. D6's floor removes about a third of it (**estimate**; D25 measures it); B3's 0.5 % per window is the gate.
- **Every window can hold an abyss** (**measured**, climate only, no T, at this revision: `samplePoint` on a 128-block grid, 4,096 points per window, over the default profile's 4 × 4 windows of 8 × 8 km at seeds '42', '1', '2' and '3'; raw boxes, no border noises): in every window of the four seeds at least 3.4 % of positions lie in the abyss box with surfaceEst − 128 above −47 (room for abyss air above the rock floor), at most 25 %; the mean height of that room per position is at least 1.33 blocks per window (seed '2', its window (1, 2)). Lush's climate holds at least 16 % of positions per window and dripstone's at least 6 %. So no window lacks the abyss for climate reasons alone; whether its cave air reaches 0.5 % is thin in the poorest windows (a rough reading, 1.33 blocks of room times the deep caves' share per y, gives about 0.6-1 %), which is what D25's checkpoint measures with T.
- **Cost** (**estimate**): 400 positions × 4 noise2 at about 45 ns ≈ 0.07 ms, plus about 2.3k picks at tagged voxels and up to about 1.2k for the `here` test's mixed voxels (§3.5 item 4), about 3.5k picks at 10-20 ns ≈ 0.05 ms: 0.1-0.2 ms per column. The bench row `caveBiome.column` (recorded) measures it.

## 2. Where the biome is stored (D17)

### 2.1 The `cave` byte's high nibble

- T's new cave-biome step runs after the tag (`tagColumn`) and before water v0 (§3.6's order): for each tagged voxel it writes `kind | (caveBiomeAt(v) << 4)`. Untagged voxels keep 0.
- `CAVE_KIND`'s comment (`src/core/constants.ts:169-176`) and master §2.3 (:351) say bits 4-7 hold the cave biome (SP6b).
- **Readers.** A new `caveKindOf(byte) = byte & 15` beside `CAVE_KIND` in `src/core/constants.ts` is the one way to read the kind. A comparison with 0 (`=== 0`, `!== 0`) needs no mask, because T writes the nibble only where the kind is not 0. Every reader of the stored byte that compares it with a kind or a range of kinds goes through `caveKindOf`. The masked readers land before the nibble, in their own commit (the plan's Task 8), which changes no output: with bits 4-7 set, `C7.carverReference` (max 0, gated) and DT2's `tagProbe` would fail at T's first write. At 1ac9e7b they are:
  - src: the finder's loaded-column scan (`src/gen/caves/tags.ts:534`, `:537`, a uniform section's byte included) and its yaw (`yawAt`, `:619`, which compares `view.cave(…)` with the kind); the Voxels mode (`src/ui/crossSection/voxels.ts:174`, `caveRgb(caveKindOf(byte))`). The slice job copies the whole byte (`src/workers/sliceJob.ts:150`, `:203`) for the Voxels mode only, unmasked, because the Voxels mode's biome colours need the nibble (§9.2); it is masked at `voxels.ts:174`'s read. The `caves` field re-tags from the density (`caveTagRun`) and needs no mask.
  - test harness and metrics: C7's carver reference (`test/harness/refs.ts:333-334`, `isCarverTag` on `view.tag`) and its stored side (`regionCounts` in `test/harness/caveMetrics.ts`, `tag >= 6 && tag <= 8`, masked at the tag read), both read by the gated `C7.carverReference`; the render metrics' cave-region choice (`crossingPairs`, `test/harness/windowScans.ts:56-59`, fed by `:83` and `test/metrics/render.metric.ts:167`); DT2's stored-against-probe comparisons (`test/metrics/region.metric.ts:293`, `test/harness/dt2.ts:309-312`), which compare the kind with `caveTagAt` (§2.3).
  - unit and integration tests that compare a stored byte with a kind (for example `test/unit/terrainStage.test.ts:441`, `:467`, `:482`, `test/unit/caveTags.test.ts:108`, `:381`, `test/unit/carve.test.ts:582`, `:760`, `test/unit/refsCarve.test.ts:49`, `:109`, `test/unit/crossSectionVoxels.test.ts:232`, `test/integration/caveSavedWorld.test.ts:98`, `:146`); the masking commit re-runs a grep for `.cave(`, `.tag(`, `sectionCave` and `cave[` and masks every comparison it finds. A test that checks the slice job's copy against the store (`test/unit/sliceJob.test.ts:103`, the cave check of `crossSectionVoxels.test.ts:232`) compares the whole byte, as the copy carries it.
  - already masked: the X-ray (`src/mesh/xray.ts:84`, `:116`), the bench's `tagAt` (`src/ui/world/worldPage.ts:471`), `bruteXrayFaces` (`test/harness/refs.ts:191`), the cave metrics' grid (`test/harness/caveMetrics.ts:141`, `TAG_MASK` 15).
  - A unit test pins it: a column with every nibble set gives the same kind-level outputs (the carver reference and its stored side, crossing pairs, the finder's result and yaw over loaded columns with a uniform section, the Voxels mode's colours from the slice job's copy, DT2's `tagProbe`) as the same column with the nibbles cleared. `regionHash` covers the channel (`src/metrics/region.ts:84`), so the region goldens change under D28's bump.
- Memory: a section whose cave voxels mix biomes is no longer uniform; most cave sections already hold an array. The cave channel's shared memory at RD 12 is recorded at D25's checkpoint (SP6a measured it at its prototype).

### 2.2 `caveBiomeQ` in aux B

- `caveBiomeQ[(qy·4 + qz)·4 + qx]`, `qy = (y + 64) >> 2` (master :379; `AUX_B_OFFSETS.caveBiomeQ` 0, `src/world/store/aux.ts:28`): 0 when the quart holds no tagged voxel, else 1 + the biome most of its tagged voxels carry (ties to the lower id). Tagged voxels holding water v0's water count, like dry ones (the nibble is written at both; this spec's choice, so SP7's flooded caves keep their biome; alternative: dry tagged voxels only, as B3's population, §12.1). T writes it beside `surfaceBiomeQ` (`src/gen/pipeline/terrainStage.ts:221-224`); it is 0 today (`:29-30`).
- Readers: SP8b's decoration and O2, SP8c's AU3 (master §6.4), the map's future tile layer (SP10). `regionHash` covers aux B (`src/metrics/region.ts:93-95`).

### 2.3 Probes

- `caveTagAt(x, y, z)` (`src/gen/caves/tags.ts:295`), `caveTagRun` and `caveTagBatch` (`:313-356`) keep returning the kind (0-9): the `caves` slice field, the finder and DT2's `tagProbe` stay kind-level, and none of them pays the picker (`slice.caves128` is 280 ms against its 300 ms gate, the SP6a spec :1029). DT2's `tagProbe` compares `caveKindOf(stored)` with `caveTagAt`.
- `caveBiomeAt(x, y, z)` (new, `src/gen/biomes/cavePicker.ts`) builds the column pass for one position's neighbourhood (from a ColumnSample, read with `readFieldHalo`) and returns the biome; DT2's new `caveBiomeProbe` compares it with the stored nibble at tagged voxels and with `caveBiomeQ` for quarts holding a tagged voxel. The `biome3d` slice field (§9.2) computes the nibble once per chunk column it touches, not per sample.
- The surface probe rebuilds a column as T does (`src/gen/surface/probe.ts:1-9`, `:12-31`) but does not tag today; it gains the tag and the cave-biome step, so `surfaceProbeBulk` holds with rule 4.

## 3. Surface rules (D18-D21)

### 3.1 The scan

`scanColumn` (`src/gen/surface/scan.ts:163`) takes the column's tag array. Per run it records, beside `runTop`, `runBottom`, `waterAbove` and `waterTop` (`scan.ts:181-232`), `floorBiome` (the high nibble of the voxel above `runTop` if that voxel is tagged, else none) and `ceilBiome` (the voxel below `runBottom`, same rule; a run whose bottom is −63 has none, `scan.ts:81`, `:211-213`). Its callers change with it: T, the surface probe, `terrainSurfaceDebug` (`src/gen/pipeline/terrainStage.ts:249-258`) and the bench.

### 3.2 The `caveBiome` condition

- Form: `{ kind: 'caveBiome', side: 'floor' | 'ceiling' | 'here', biomes: CaveBiomeName[] }`, names from `CAVE_BIOME` ('plain', 'lush', 'dripstone', 'abyss'), at least one, no repeats.
- `floor`: true when the voxel's run has a `floorBiome` in `biomes`. `ceiling`: the same with `ceilBiome`. `here`: true when `caveBiomeAt(v)` is in `biomes`.
- Work list: `CONDITION_KINDS` and `FIELDS` (`src/gen/surface/rules.ts:60`, `:180`), validation, both evaluators (`src/gen/surface/compile.ts:238-290`, `src/gen/surface/reference.ts`), the inspector's form, the rule fuzzer's mutators (`treeMutator`).
- `secondaryDepthRange` is not taken: no rule here needs a noisy depth (§3.4's depths are 0-1 with noise patches). It goes to SP8b with that reason (cave decoration reads floors and ceilings; master :805 keeps it "deferred to the first SP whose default rules need it").

### 3.3 The `strata` rule kind (D19)

- Form: `{ kind: 'strata', layers: [{ state, weight, maxLen }], minLen, maxLen, noise, amp }`; `noise` a 2D, remap-'none' `surface.noises.*` leaf as `noiseThreshold` accepts (`src/gen/surface/rules.ts:367-377`); 1 ≤ minLen ≤ maxLen ≤ 8; 1-16 layers; weights > 0.
- Table: 192 entries built once per GenContext from `Xoshiro128(deriveSeed(seed, 'surface.strata.' + k))`, k the node's index among the tree's `strata` nodes in depth-first order. From i = 0 while i < 192: draw a layer by weight (one `nextFloat` over the cumulative weights: of every layer for the first run; with two or more layers, of every layer but the previous run's after it, so each maximal run is one draw and a veined run is never longer than 1; with one layer no exclusion), then `len = minLen + r.nextInt(min(maxLen, layer.maxLen) − minLen + 1)` (with `layer.maxLen` < minLen, len = layer.maxLen and no length draw), write it to [i, min(192, i + len)), i += len. No other draw. The layers' run shares follow the exclusion chain's stationary share w_i(1 − w_i)/Σ_j w_j(1 − w_j), w_i the layer's weight over the total (the abyss default ≈ 0.382, 0.286, 0.195, 0.137). With independent draws, two consecutive draws of one layer would merge into one longer run, so veins of 2 or more and runs above a layer's `maxLen` would occur in almost every table.
- Lookup: `table[((y + o) mod 192 + 192) mod 192]`, `o = Math.round(amp · z / clampSigma)`, z the z2 of `noise` at (x, z), sampled once per position (as `bandlands`, `src/gen/surface/compile.ts:314-329`).
- The abyss's default: layers abyss_stone (weight 50, maxLen 8), abyss_shale (25, 4), cracked_abyss_stone (15, 3), abyss_stone_veined (10, 1, so veins are single lines); minLen 1, maxLen 6; noise `surface.noises.strataOffset` (2D, λ 128, 2 octaves); amp 6.

### 3.4 Rule 4 (inserted at `root.rules[2]`)

Master order (:807): rule 1, rule 3, rule 4, rule 2, then stone. Rule 4 sits after the sky-open rule, so open-sky floors (a ravine floor) keep the surface palette. `surface.noises.cavePatch` is a new 2D leaf (λ 12, 2 octaves). The thresholds are z2 values; the shares assume z2 is about standard normal and are checked at tuning. Every `noiseThreshold` writes both fields (each kind's fields are all present, `src/gen/surface/rules.ts:2-6`, `:184`); 8 and −8 stand for "no bound", as the default rules write them (`src/core/params/defaultTrees.ts:388`, `:561`). For the same reason every `stoneDepth` below writes `addSurfaceDepth: false` (`stoneDepth{floor, 1}` reads `{kind: 'stoneDepth', side: 'floor', offset: 1, addSurfaceDepth: false}`), as floating_islands' island branch writes its own (`src/core/params/presetTrees.ts:199-202`; the plan's Spec correction 10).

```
rule 4 = sequence [
  condition caveBiome{here, [abyss]} → sequence [                                    D4: the abyss's rock
    condition stoneDepth{floor, 1} → condition caveBiome{floor, [abyss]}
      → condition noiseThreshold{cavePatch, min −0.84, max 8} → block abyss_ash               ash on ≈ 80 % of floors, 2 deep
    strata{abyss layers}                                                               every other abyss voxel
  ],
  condition stoneDepth{floor, 1} → condition caveBiome{floor, [lush]} → sequence [
    condition noiseThreshold{cavePatch, min 0.84, max 8} → block clay                         ≈ 20 % of lush floors
    block moss_block                                                                   moss ×2
  ],
  condition stoneDepth{floor, 1} → condition caveBiome{floor, [dripstone]}
    → condition not(noiseThreshold{cavePatch, min −8, max −0.97}) → block dripstone_block      bare stone on ≈ 1/6
  condition stoneDepth{ceiling, 0} → condition caveBiome{ceiling, [lush]}
    → condition noiseThreshold{cavePatch, min −0.52, max 8} → block moss_block                moss on ≈ 70 % of ceilings
  condition stoneDepth{ceiling, 1} → condition caveBiome{ceiling, [dripstone]}
    → condition not(noiseThreshold{cavePatch, min −8, max −0.97}) → block dripstone_block
]
```

A voxel no branch takes falls through to rule 2 (deepslate below the 1…7 dither) and then stone, as today. Grass never appears below the open-sky run (S1). Paths that name `root.rules[2]` as the deepslate gradient move to `root.rules[3]`: `test/unit/surfaceDefaults.test.ts:91`, `test/unit/inspectorForm.test.ts:126-127`, `:170-171`, `:203-209`, `src/metrics/liveness.ts:353`, the comment at `src/core/params/presetTrees.ts:213`, and U2's pinned perturbation "the deepslate gradient's upper bound 8 → 12" (master §6.4 U2). floating_islands' derivation rewrites only `root.rules[1]` (`src/core/params/presetTrees.ts:215-221`), so its `was` check survives the insertion; its tree gains rule 4 at the same index.

### 3.5 The fast path's extensions (D20)

`surfaceFastPath` (`src/gen/surface/compile.ts:152-195`) takes a voxel without evaluation when every leaf is depth-bounded or Y-only and the voxel lies beyond the bounds; non-sky runs take their y band's constant state when the tree is sky-gated (`compile.ts:166`, `:392`). Rule 4 breaks both. Four extensions, none changing output:
1. **Ceiling-bounded leaves:** a leaf whose depth gates are all `stoneDepth{ceiling, c}` (no `stoneDepth{floor}` gate on its path) is bounded by c from the run's bottom (the smallest c over its ceiling gates, + SD_MAX with `addSurfaceDepth`); a leaf with a `stoneDepth{floor}` gate is floor-bounded only, since its floor gate already excludes every voxel beyond it (floating_islands' island branch holds `stoneDepth{ceiling, 100}` under `stoneDepth{floor, 4}` gates, `src/core/params/presetTrees.ts:197-204`; counting it would evaluate the bottom 100 voxels of every run there). A voxel is fast when its floorDepth exceeds its run's floor bound and its ceilDepth exceeds the ceiling bound.
2. **Per-class bounds:** one floor bound for leaves under `skyOpen` (today's 4 + SD_MAX = 15) and a separate one for the rest (rule 4's 1). Without it every non-sky run evaluates its top 16 voxels: about 218 floors × 16 ≈ 3.5k voxels, +0.2-0.3 ms (**estimate**, recon-gen §3.5).
3. **Biome-gated skip:** when every non-sky floor (ceiling) leaf has `caveBiome{floor}` (`{ceiling}`) on its path, a run whose `floorBiome` (`ceilBiome`) is none or not named by any such leaf takes the band state. Only biome runs then evaluate: about 76 floors plus as many ceilings × 2-3 voxels ≈ 0.4k voxels (**measured** run counts, recon-gen §1.7).
4. **`caveBiome{here}` classification:** it classifies the picker's result, not one box. Per position, from the column pass's arrays over the (2J + 1)² neighbourhood, each box's climate test is surely true, surely false or mixed, and each box's depth tests fix y ranges where they are surely true or surely false (J above and below the extremes of Y0 − D·depthMin and Y0 − D·depthMax over the neighbourhood). A y range is **surely b** for a biome b when b's box is surely true there and every other box is surely false there (so the margin rule cannot pick another), and **surely not b** when b's box is surely false there. For a `caveBiome{here, biomes}` leaf, voxels in a range surely in `biomes` take the leaf's branch without evaluation (for the abyss's default: the `strata` lookup, and the floor branch at run tops), voxels in a range surely outside `biomes` skip it, and every other voxel evaluates `caveBiomeAt`. With the default boxes the abyss's range is surely abyss wherever its climate test is surely true, because the lush and dripstone depth ranges end 0.1 depth above it; an edit that makes boxes overlap falls back to evaluation in the overlap, which costs time and never changes output. Without this extension the abyss's rock goes through the tree: about 13k voxels per abyss column × 50-80 ns ≈ 0.6-1.0 ms on about 13 % of columns (**estimate**, recon-gen §3.4), in p90 and p99.

**The rules column cost** (`rulesCost`, `src/gen/inspect/cost.ts`; the plan's Spec correction 20, rulings P11 and P20) learns the same classes, so tier 2 never prices rule 4's leaves as unbounded: a leaf whose depth gates are all `stoneDepth{ceiling}` is bounded by its ceiling bound, a `caveBiome{floor | ceiling}`-gated leaf is counted at its class's bound, `strata` is a leaf beside `block` and `bandlands`, and the nodes in the `then` of a here-only condition (`caveBiome{here}` or a `not` of one) cost a whole column (98,304) each, since the classification may take them at any voxel; every other node costs 256 × (1 + the largest bound over the floor and ceiling classes). The default tree with rule 4 costs 3,520,512 (207 nodes: 198 at 256 × 52 and the 9 under its `caveBiome{here}` at 98,304); `RULES_COLUMN_COST_MAX` stays 138,870,784, 64 × the SP6a default's 2,169,856 (163 nodes × 256 × 52), as `DENSITY_COLUMN_COST_MAX` stays 64 × the SP3d default's, so the default is about 39 × below it. The `blocks` and `rule` slice fields' per-column cost (`tColumnCost`) moves with it.

A fuzz test (SP3c's pattern) compares the fast path with full evaluation on random trees from `treeMutator`, which learns `caveBiome` (every side and any list of biomes) and `strata`, and on random `caves.biomes.*` values (boxes that overlap, `edgeJitter` 0-4), over columns that hold every biome.

**floating_islands** (`terrain.islands` 11.89 ms p50 at SP6a's exit, against 14.5; the SP6a spec :1029): its island branch (`src/core/params/presetTrees.ts:197-204`) is a set of non-sky leaves bounded at 4 + SD_MAX from the floor with no `caveBiome{floor}` on their path, so in that tree extension 2's non-sky bound stays 15 and extension 3 does not apply; rule 4's ceiling leaves add the bottom voxels of each run. Expected cost on islands: +0.3-0.6 ms p50 (**estimate**; D25 measures `terrain.islands`). Its lever if it passes the gate: let extension 3 treat the leaves gated by `yAbove{ISLAND_RUN_MIN_BOTTOM}` as a class of their own, so runs below the islands take the band state. **Taken** at D25's checkpoint (Task 25's lever 25e, output-keeping): a non-sky floor-bounded leaf whose path holds a `yAbove{runTop true}` is in a run-top-gated floor class (`topFloorBound`, at the gate `topFloorMinY`, the smallest over its leaves of each path's largest such minY) that counts only in runs whose top reaches the gate; the other non-sky floor class is then rule 4's alone, with its biome-gated skip. On floating_islands' cluster columns the surface pass evaluates 7,645 voxels per column instead of 10,339 (1.26 ms instead of 1.60 ms per column, measured on 128 columns); the default tree has no such leaf and is unchanged.

### 3.6 Order in T

Density fill → density tops → carve (with σ_b, §5) → tag → **cave biome** (the column pass; the nibble per tagged voxel) → water v0 → surface biomes → surface pass (the scan reads the nibbles) → sections → aux A → aux B (`surfaceBiomeQ` and **`caveBiomeQ`**). Today's order is `src/gen/pipeline/terrainStage.ts:175-225`. The terrain stage's version goes 4 → 5 (`src/core/stage/registry.ts:20`).

## 4. The new blocks (D36)

Appended to `BLOCK_DEFS` (append-only, `src/world/blocks/defs.ts:1-10`) with the `terrain(name, sound, tint)` helper: opaque full cubes, carvable, no properties, tint none.

| id | type | sound | flat colour (sRGB, proposal) | nearest existing (summed |ΔRGB|) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 58 | moss_block | grass | [78, 112, 52] | green_wool 33 |
| 59 | dripstone_block | stone | [150, 120, 96] | light_gray_terracotta 30 |
| 60 | abyss_stone | abyss | [36, 34, 44] | bedrock 28 |
| 61 | abyss_stone_veined | abyss | [58, 48, 74] | mud 24, gray_wool 26 |
| 62 | cracked_abyss_stone | abyss | [50, 44, 46] | abyss_stone 26 |
| 63 | abyss_shale | abyss | [70, 64, 48] | mud 30, brown_terracotta 32 |
| 64 | abyss_ash | abyss | [92, 88, 86] | deepslate 26 |

- The colours are proposals from recon-gen §3.4; the commit adjusts any by a few units if the pairwise rule fails (every pair, plus sky, water, lava and unknown, ≥ 24 apart in summed |ΔRGB|, `test/unit/crossSectionVoxels.test.ts:69-80`). The dark corner is crowded (bedrock [28, 28, 30], black_wool [16, 16, 20], mud [60, 57, 61], gray_wool [52, 66, 76], deepslate [78, 78, 84], `src/textures/flatColors.ts:16-72`); the abyss's rock stays darker than deepslate (the brief's "very dark stone").
- Counts that move: `stateCount` 58 → 65 and `typeCount` 50 → 57 (`test/unit/crossSectionVoxels.test.ts:72-73`), the named types 49 → 56 and the array layers 60 → 67 (`test/unit/flatColors.test.ts:33`, `:76-83`); `LOG_END_LAYER` and `LAVA_LAYER` follow `stateCount` (`src/mesh/greedy.ts:68-69`); the solid word's 10-bit layer (`src/mesh/packing.ts:52`) has room.
- Recognition depends on light: under the light floor a stone face reads about sRGB 18 (SP6a §6.1), so dark albedos read near black. Night vision normalises each texel to its brightest channel (SP6a §6.2), so it shows the palettes apart; the review shots and the user's check use it in the abyss (§12.8).
- `EMIT` is 0 for all seven (a unit test pins it; R21 of recon-obligations: the abyss stays dark).
- No new renderer code: both renderers sample one flat-colour array built once (`src/render/textureArray.ts:37-53`; `src/render/renderer.ts:670`).

## 5. The bottom of caves (D6; Point 2)

### 5.1 Where the flat floor comes from

y −64 is always bedrock (`src/gen/pipeline/terrainStage.ts:196-198`), carvers never carve y ≤ −64 (`src/gen/caves/carve.ts:12`, `:119`, `:305`), and rule 1 dithers bedrock over −63…−60 on solid voxels only (`src/core/params/defaultTrees.ts:361-364`; master :808). No cave family closes above y −63: cheese and spaghetti 3D have `minY` −56, spaghetti 2D and entrances −64, the noodle −60, and each cap's steep part starts only 16 blocks below `minY` (`defaultTrees.ts:100-115`; SP6a §1.4). **Measured:** 14.4 tagged cave-air voxels per column sit at y −63 directly on the bedrock (5.6 % of positions show the plane), 78 % of them cheese; 76,455 of 316,946 flat faces on quick sit at y −63 (24.1 %; 69 % of the default group's flat floors) (recon-gen §1.6, recon-tests §5.3). Those `minY` values are SP6a's: with the floor (§5.2) they lie in its shadow, where g_b = 1 saturates every cap's gentle sum (corners −64 and −56 for every floor value), so U2's steps of them had no witness; Task 19 raised them to the shadow's edge, cheese, spaghetti 3D, spaghetti 2D and the noodle −55 and entrances −54, the least move that gives a step a witness (ruling R10, the user's SP6a rule R5 "Bajarlo sin cambiar el mundo"). It changes no generated output at the defaults and for every `caves.limits.bandSpan` ≥ 2 and every `caves.floor.*` value in range (0 of 96 columns on default, amplified and floating_islands; 0 of 160 at `caves.floor.y` −58, −60, −62; inv-19-u2.md, inv-19-u2-adversarial.md); a saved world keeps its stored values (no migration remap), which give the same world. Their liveness needs corner −48 left unsaturated by g_b, so a retune of `caves.floor.*` or `bandSpan` re-runs their witnesses (Tasks 25, 29, 30).

### 5.2 The rock floor (F1)

- **The floor height:** `floorY(x, z) = caves.floor.y + caves.floor.amp · |Nf(x, z)|`, Nf the z2 of `caves.noises.floor` (2D, λ 48, 3 octaves) clamped to [−3, 3], a COLUMN-class `noise2` evaluated once per position (`src/gen/density/nodes.ts:309-311`). Defaults: `caves.floor.y` −60, `caves.floor.amp` 7, so floorY runs from −60 (where Nf crosses 0: the gullies) up to −39 (the mounds' tops), about −54 on average (the mean of |z| is about 0.8 for a standard normal z). D25's checkpoint raised `caves.floor.amp` 5 → 7 (Task 25's lever 25d: `C7.bottomFlatRatio` read 2.67 on amplified at amp 5, 0.94 at amp 7) and then lowered `caves.floor.y` −58 → −60 (lever 25c: at amp 7 C4's lowest band read 0.0183 < 0.02, at −60 0.0262), so the floor keeps about the first draft's mean height with more relief (the Exit evidence's lever table).
- **Why the relief must be in floorY** (the technical review's M2): in the open middle of a wide cavern the cheese slot is between about −16 and −5.8 (16·clamp(…, −1, 1) plus non-negative terms, `src/core/params/defaultTrees.ts:141-145`; the gentle rise `CAP_GENTLE.cheese` is 5.8, `:70`, so the gentle term cannot close a slot below −5.8), and the steep term closes it where S_b = −slot, that is at y between floorY(x, z) + 7 and floorY(x, z) + 7.6. The cave noise adds under one block of relief there, so the floor of every wide cavern has floorY's shape. A smooth floorY (the first draft's ±3 blocks over 100 blocks) would make a nearly flat floor about 19 blocks above the bedrock; the folded noise |Nf| at λ 48 gives mounds 15-30 blocks across with gullies between them.
- **Noise families:** each family cap `G_f·min(1, g_f + …) + 128·min(1, h_f + …)` gains a gentle term `g_b = clamp((floorY(x, z) + 8 + s_b − y) / s_b, 0, 1)` inside its gentle sum, s_b = `caves.floor.span` (8), written once as `defs.floorGentle` and read by the five caps (Task 19, ruling R11: the same doubles; the slice reference's corner memo then evaluates it once per corner, not once per cap); and a steep term `S_b = 128 · clamp((floorY(x, z) + 8 − y) / 8, 0, 1)` is added at voxel level to the caves slot and the noodle, through the def that today holds the wet roof's `S_w` (`defaultTrees.ts:115-121`; SP6a §1.4): `defs.wetSteep` becomes `defs.steep` = S_w + S_b, so the root's node paths stay as documented at `defaultTrees.ts:250-255` (`root.a.a.b.b` and `root.a.b.b` stay the two refs; the tests that name `defs.wetSteep` follow the rename; alternative: a separate `add` beside each ref, which moves the root paths that `test/unit/inspectorModel.test.ts`, `caveTerms.test.ts`, `fieldSlice.test.ts`, `defaultTrees.test.ts`, `caveTags.test.ts`, `sliceCost.test.ts`, `densityResolve.test.ts`, `test/unit/fixtures/editedTrees.ts` and `test/harness/sp5cTrees.ts` pin). With the family floor F = 48, S_b ≥ 64 > F at y ≤ floorY(x, z) + 4, so no noise-family air lies there.
- **Carvers:** `σ_b = clamp((floorY(x, z) + 8 + s_b − y) / s_b, 0, 1)` joins `σ = max(σ_w, σ_i, σ_u)` (`src/gen/caves/carve.ts:13-17`, `:85-108`), so carvers shrink to nothing at y ≤ floorY + 8; `carvedAt` reads the same noise.
- **The constraint holds over the whole range:** the lowest air voxel is `caves.floor.y` + 5 (noise-family air needs y ≥ floorY(x, z) + 5, and floorY ≥ `caves.floor.y` because |Nf| ≥ 0); it must leave at least two rock voxels above rule 1's dither (top −60), so air ≥ −57, that is `caves.floor.y` ≥ −62. The leaf's range is −62 … −16, so no edit can break it and no lint is needed. At the defaults the lowest air is −55 and carver air −51.
- **The look:** in a wide cavern the floor's air starts at floorY(x, z) + 7: about y −53 in the gullies, −47 on average, up to −32 on the highest mounds; within one cavern the floor's height spreads by about 10-11 blocks between its 10th and 90th percentiles (if z2 is about standard normal: |z| from 0.13 to 1.64, times 7), with slopes of up to about 0.7 block per block on the mounds' flanks, which the 8-block steep part rounds. Smaller caves close rounded at varied heights through the gentle part. The floor block is deepslate (rule 2), in the abyss ash on dark rock (§3.4). D25's checkpoint takes a screenshot of the `bottomCavern` pose and measures `C7.bottomFloorSpread` (§12.3) before the rest is built on the floor.
- **What it costs** (**estimate**, from recon-gen §1.6's histogram: 11.4 % of cave air lies at y −64…−49): about 10-14 % of cave air and about a third of the abyss's. Likely a saving in T: cells with S_b ≥ 64 are decided solid by the bounds without corners (`src/gen/density/bounds.ts:2-23`). D25 measures both.
- **The slice work bound** (Task 19; SP6a §7.4): the floor's reads of `noise2 floor` cost the slice reference real time (measured 5-15 % per slice, inv-19-slice.md), and the bound charges them as the reference evaluates them. Two charges are corrected to what is computed (the user's rulings U2 and U3): the `caves` field, which runs the stage's own compile, charges n × (`compiledVoxelWork`, the voxel work with each def evaluated once per sample as the compile's CSE does, + 64) instead of the reference's per-read `memoWorkCost` voxel (on the floorless tree 165 instead of 179 per sample: `ref wetSteep` is read twice); and a horizontal `density` or `node` slice whose expression reads `col steep` charges the distinct quart lattice points its sample column reads × `CORNER_STEEP_COST` / 4 (`sliceLatticePoints`: 132 per 128² row at 256 blocks, 258 at 512, 4n from 1024, where no two samples share a point) instead of n × `CORNER_STEEP_COST`, since the part's corner sampler computes each point once; the other acquisition modes are unchanged. The steep edit of the default tree's 128² at 256 blocks was refused with the floor (it takes about 0.5 s while 1.6-1.9 s slices at 512 are admitted; inv-19-slice-verify.md) and is admitted again; the `caves` 128² at 512 (SP6a §7.4's admission) keeps a thin margin, re-checked by every later task that changes the default density tree (`sliceCost.test.ts`). With the floor (Task 19, `defs.floorGentle`): the `density` 128² at 512 is admitted with 2,497,536 to spare (refused by 4,426,752 with g_b written in each cap), the `caves` 128² at 512 with 223,104 (0.17 %), the steep edit's 128² at 256 with 60,422,144; a vertical 128² `density` part holds 28 sample columns (289,864 each; 30 before the floor).
- **Existing gates the floor moves:** `C4.floorPeak` ≤ 0.1 (`test/thresholds.ts:93`; the largest share one y holds of the floors of components ≥ 10k voxels, `test/harness/caveMetrics.ts:500-523`; 0.014-0.049 today, with the bedrock plane holding its floors at one y; a floor spread over about 15 levels is not expected to raise it) and `C7.flatWallShare` ≤ 0.037 (`test/thresholds.ts:105`; 0.0319 today, a quarter of its flat faces on the y −63 plane, which goes; the mounds' tops add some). Both are measured at D25's generator checkpoint.
- **Alternative** (this spec's decision): a symmetric floor `floorY + amp·Nf` (λ 48, amp 4, `caves.floor.y` −45 to keep the constraint), which gives similar relief without gullies but ends wide caverns about 9 blocks higher on average (more cave air lost); or a 3D lump term in S_b, which costs a voxel-level noise.
- **SP7:** master :751's "everything below y −54 is lava" would fire at the defaults only on the deepest gullies' lowest voxel (no air lies below −55); SP7 places the abyss's lava from aquifer cells or lowers the floor locally (§16).

### 5.3 F2 and F3 (Point 2's alternatives)

- **F2:** `bedrockTop(x, z) = −63 + round(b0 + bAmp·N2)` ∈ [−63, −56], below it bedrock whatever the density, written by T as y −64 is today, with a 1-2-block dither above. Caves reach a bumpy black floor. Cost negligible. S2's `bedrockFloor` (bedrock at −64 100 %) holds; T4's "exposed bedrock under water 0" must be re-checked where the layer rises.
- **F3:** F1 plus F2; F2 is then invisible from caves.

## 6. Parameters and `cave_heavy` (D12, D15)

### 6.1 The leaves

All under groups the terrain stage hashes (`src/core/stage/registry.ts:20`; scope terrain, `:29-31`), so each regenerates T, shows a Terrain badge and needs Modify terrain in saved worlds. Nothing goes under `biomes.*`, which belongs to `biome2d` (`registry.ts:19`) and would invalidate ColumnSamples and the 2D map.

| leaf | default | range | panel |
|---|---|---|---|
| `caves.biomes.lush.hMin` | 0.4 | −1 … 1 | Cave biomes › Lush |
| `caves.biomes.lush.depthMin` / `depthMax` | 0.2 / 0.9 | 0 … 2 | Lush |
| `caves.biomes.dripstone.cMin` | 0.6 | −1 … 1 | Dripstone |
| `caves.biomes.dripstone.depthMin` / `depthMax` | 0.2 / 0.9 | 0 … 2 | Dripstone |
| `caves.biomes.abyss.eMax` / `cMin` | −0.5 / −0.1 | −1 … 1 | Abyss |
| `caves.biomes.abyss.depthMin` | 1.0 | 0 … 2 | Abyss |
| `caves.biomes.depthScale` | 128 | 32 … 256 | Cave biomes (first) |
| `caves.biomes.borderWarp` | 0.05 | 0 … 0.2 | Cave biomes (first) |
| `caves.biomes.depthWarp` | 10 | 0 … 32 | Cave biomes (first) |
| `caves.biomes.edgeJitter` | 2 | 0 … 4 (int; 4 is the ColumnSample halo's limit, §1.2) | Cave biomes (first) |
| `caves.biomes.noises.border` / `depth` | λ 64 / 96, 2 octaves, 2D | schema noise leaves | Cave biomes › Noises |
| `caves.noises.floor` | λ 48, 3 octaves, 2D | | Cave noises (at its end) |
| `caves.floor.y` | −60 (−58 until Task 25's lever 25c) | −62 … −16 | Floor |
| `caves.floor.amp` | 7 (5 until Task 25's lever 25d) | 0 … 12 | Floor |
| `caves.floor.span` | 8 | 1 … 32 | Floor |
| `surface.noises.cavePatch` | λ 12, 2 octaves, 2D | | Surface noises |
| `surface.noises.strataOffset` | λ 128, 2 octaves, 2D | | Surface noises |

- The panel makes a sub-section per schema group only (`src/ui/paramPanel/model.ts:85-100`), so there is no "Borders" sub-section: `depthScale`, `borderWarp`, `depthWarp` and `edgeJitter` are the Cave biomes group's own leaves, and the panel renders a section's own leaves before its sub-sections, so the Cave biomes section shows them first, then Lush, Dripstone, Abyss and Noises (in the schema they follow Abyss and precede Noises; the plan's Spec correction 12, ruling P18).
- The `caves` group stays last (`src/core/params/schema.ts:181-184`). Every new caves leaf comes after every existing one: `caves.noises.floor` at the end of `caves.noises` (today the group's last sub-group, `:292-318`), then a new `floor` sub-group, then the `biomes` sub-group with its noises. So `randomParams` and `randomPatch` (`test/harness/params.ts:131`, `:144`) draw every earlier caves leaf as before (the first draft appended the floor leaves to Limits, which precedes `caves.noises`, `:286-291`, and would have shifted every noise draw); the panel shows "Floor" and then "Cave biomes" after "Cave noises". Adding `surface.noises.cavePatch` and `strataOffset` (they must be `surface.noises.*` leaves for `noiseThreshold` and `strata`, `src/gen/surface/rules.ts:367-377`) shifts the draws of the groups after `surface`; the commit that adds them re-records whatever pins those draws. Alternative: let `noiseThreshold` accept a `caves.biomes.noises.*` leaf and keep both noises in the caves group.
- Lints (`src/ui/paramPanel/lints.ts:26-30`): `depthMin` < `depthMax` for lush and dripstone ("the biome never occurs"). §5.2's floor constraint needs none: it holds over the leaves' ranges.
- Every new leaf lands in the generator push (§14.3), because adding schema leaves changes the `sp1.params` golden (it did at each SP6a commit that added leaves: b550af4, d184dc9, eab335e), which needs the `GENERATOR_VERSION` bump (`test/harness/governance.ts:46-52`). The picker's pure module, pushed earlier, takes its values as arguments, not from the schema.
- Rule 4's blocks, patch thresholds and the strata layers are data in `surface.rules`.
