- U2 covers every new leaf (§12.7).

### 6.2 `cave_heavy`

`cave_heavy`'s overlay (today `{}`, `src/core/params/profiles.ts:38`), on the SP6a spec §4's leaves, against today's defaults (cheese `offset` 0.5, entrances 0.37, noodles `maxY` 48, worms `chance` 0.15, underground worms 0.07, ravines 0.015; recon-obligations K3):

| leaf | overlay | why |
|---|---|---|
| `caves.cheese.offset` | set at tuning from {0.43, 0.38, 0.33, 0.30}: the largest value whose quick-tier C1 on `cave_heavy` is ≥ 12 %; as tuned, **0.30** (no value reached 12 %: 9.3-10.3 % on quick; the user's choice, ruling U6; SP12 finalises it) | 0.43 is the master's step (0.27 → 0.20, −0.07) below today's default; 0.30 is the absolute 0.20's distance below it (ruling R21 (f)); C1's 10-22 % is recorded now, gated in SP12 |
| `caves.entrances.offset` | 0.30 | the master's row (:936) |
| `caves.noodles.maxY` | 96 | noodles are twice as thick since ruling R32, so the master's 120 (written against 64) breaks the surface often; 96 keeps them out of most peaks; alternative 120 |
| `caves.worms.chance` | 0.225 | × 1.5 |
| `caves.undergroundWorms.chance` | 0.105 | × 1.5 |
| `caves.ravines.chance` | 0.0225 | × 1.5 |

`readyFrom` stays 'SP6b', so the first commit (§14.1) makes it ready everywhere. Its dirty stages become ['terrain', 'decorate', 'light', 'mesh'] (`test/unit/profiles.test.ts:55`): an overlay under `caves` dirties terrain and every stage that reads it, as amplified's density overlay does; its badge scope is terrain. C1, C2 and C9 are recorded on it (§12.3).

## 7. The X-ray by cave biome (D7, D24)

### 7.1 The mesh

`meshXray` (`src/mesh/xray.ts:94-135`) keys a wall on `cave[v] & 15` (`:116`) and merges on (kind, face) (`:12-14`, `:125`); the sink writes kind and face, biome 0 (`:69-77`). SP6b: the key becomes `kind | biome << 4` from the full byte, the film stays kind 15 with biome 0, and the sink writes `(key >> 4) & 15` into the pack's `biome` bits 7-10 (`src/mesh/packing.ts:11-13`, `:73-75`). Merges split only where the biome changes inside one kind's walls: at most +5 % of X-ray quads (**estimate**; R1's X-ray count on a biome region measures it), ≤ +1.1 MB on Chrome RD 12 (21.9 MB of X-ray vertices) and ≤ +2.1 MB on Firefox (41.5 MB), inside `xrayGpuMB`'s 150 (132.3 today). No vertex bytes are added.

### 7.2 The material

The vertex stage decodes `pkX_biome(data)` (already generated from `PACK_LAYOUT`, `src/render/materials/glsl/packing.glsl.ts:10-20`, `:29`) into `flat out uint vBiome`; the fragment colour is `xrayColorMode == 1 && vKind != 15 ? xrayBiomePalette[vBiome] : xrayPalette[vKind]`, times the face shade (`src/render/materials/glsl/xray.glsl.ts:63`). `xrayBiomePalette[16]` (linear, from `CAVE_BIOME_COLORS`) and `xrayColorMode` join the uniforms (`src/render/materials/uniforms.ts:28-35`, `:49-53`, `:122-129`); `XRAY_BIOME_COLORS` and `XRAY_BIOME_LABELS` join `src/render/xrayPalette.ts`. A mode switch writes one uniform: O(1), allocation-free, no remesh (SP6a §5.4).

### 7.3 Toggle and text

`createXrayControl` (`src/render/renderer.ts:628-667`) gains `setColorMode(0 | 1)`. The page's X-ray state (`src/ui/world/worldPage.ts:2210-2272`) gains `mode`; `stripText(b, mode)` (`src/ui/world/xrayStrip.ts:66-69`) ends "· by cave type" or "· by cave biome"; the strip holds two prebuilt legends and shows one (`xrayStrip.ts:71-91`), so a switch writes `hidden` twice and allocates nothing (SP6a §5.3's zero-allocation rule); the HUD's `xray` field becomes `{band, mode} | null` (`src/ui/hud.ts:93`, `:134`). C's notice: "X-ray by cave biome" / "X-ray by cave type" (1.5 s, as night vision's).

### 7.4 The terrain passes while the X-ray is on (D24)

RegionBatch's opaque, cutout and translucent batches set `_multiDrawCount` 0 in their `onBeforeRender` while the X-ray is on, as the X-ray batches do while it is off (`src/render/regionBatch.ts:172-179`); their rows keep uploading. RegionMesh's terrain and water arenas get the draw range (0, 0) (not `visible = false`, for the upload reason in D23; `src/render/regionMesh.ts:33-36`). The collapse uniform stays as a second guard. `rowVisible` (`src/render/regionMesh.ts:565-575`; `src/render/regionBatch.ts:357-363`) ignores both. L14 records `gpuMs` with the X-ray on before and after (gain up to the 1.34 ms gap, **estimate**).

## 8. Cave culling (D13, D22, D23)

### 8.1 Data (preallocated, no allocation per frame)

A culler owned by `createStreamingRenderer` (`src/render/regionGrid.ts:254`) sees every event: stage (`:433-461`), reveal (pump step 5, `:566-577`), swap (`:348-354`), retire, remove and unstage (`:342-345`, `:462-481`). The walk is a pure module with no three import, `src/render/culling/caveCulling.ts`, so Node's R3 runs the same code. `onColumnRevealed` (`:578-579`) stays the bench's and the edit-latency probe's.
- `conn: Uint16Array(64·64·24)` on a 64² column torus, slot `((cz & 63) << 6 | (cx & 63))·24 + sy`, with `slotKey: Float64Array(4096)` holding the owning `columnKey` (`src/render/regionGrid.ts:119`). `RD_MAX` 16 (`src/engine/renderDistance.ts:10`): a disc 33 columns wide plus the removal lag fits without collision.
- On a reveal or a swap the culler copies the column's 24 `connectivity15` values from the staged payload (`p.connectivity`, `src/mesh/column.ts:56-57`) and stamps its sections with the walk in progress; on retire it clears the slot key.
- `entry: Uint8Array(98,304)` (faces entered, 6 bits) validated by `entryWalk: Uint32Array`; `queue: Int32Array(98,304)` as a ring holding a section at most once while it waits, new entry bits merging into its waiting mask; a section already popped is pushed again when an entry bit it has not yet been popped with arrives (otherwise a later entry face's exits would be lost). Under the monotone rule a section outside the seed box has one entry face per axis, or two on an axis where it lies in S0's plane (it can then be entered from both sides on that axis), so at most 3 + (the number of axes on which it lies in S0's plane) ≤ 5 entry faces, and it is popped at most that many times; a seed-box section, seeded with every face, is popped once (the plan's Spec correction 22); the cost estimate (§8.2) counts pops, not sections. `stamp: Uint32Array(98,304)`, the id of the walk that last reached the section.
- **The draw rule:** a section is drawn when `drawAll` is set or `stamp ≥ shown`, `shown` being the id of the last completed walk. A section reached by the walk in progress passes, so a half-done walk never hides what the previous one showed (no pop-out); a revealed or swapped column draws until a walk that saw its connectivity completes. `drawAll` (no pop-in, §8.2) is set when the last completed walk no longer covers the eye or the world opened a new path, and cleared when a walk that covers both completes.
- Per RegionBatch batch, `sectionOf: Int32Array(maxInstanceCount)` (instance → torus slot), written in `upload` (`src/render/regionBatch.ts:318-333`), regrown in the `'instances'` structural operation (`:291-293`).
- About 1.4 MB of JS heap at RD 16; GPU memory +0.

### 8.2 The walk

- **Start:** the seed box, the 3 × 3 × 3 sections around the camera's section S0 at the walk's start (floating origin plus `camera.position`), each seeded with every face as an exit, even when the eye is inside an opaque voxel in flight.
- **Pop t:** `exits` = the union, over the entered faces f, of the faces g with `connectivityBit(f, g)` set in `conn[t]` (`src/mesh/connectivity.ts:24-30`), computed with at most 5 bit tests per entered face (alternative: a 32,768 × 6-byte exits table, 192 KB).
- **Monotone rule, relative to the box:** step +x only if `sx ≥ S0x − 1`, −x only if `sx ≤ S0x + 1`; likewise y and z.
- **Why it misses nothing a ray sees from any eye in the box:** a 3D DDA ray from an eye in section e visits a 6-connected chain of non-opaque voxels until it stops; its section coordinates move monotonically away from e on each axis, and e lies within one section of S0, so every step it takes is allowed by the rule; e is seeded with every face an exit; each section it crosses joins its entry face to its exit face through those voxels, so the connectivity bit is set (opaque = PASS opaque with `fullFaces` 63, `src/mesh/connectivity.ts:69`); the section where it stops is entered, so it is stamped. One visit per section would lose a later entry face with different exits, so entry sets are kept. A completed walk therefore stays valid for every eye inside its box.
- **Neighbours:** a column within RD of the camera that is not revealed (streaming, after a teleport, while an Apply refills) is treated as fully open (connectivity 0x7FFF in every section) and stepped through, because it draws nothing and the player sees through it; only a column beyond RD (not in the torus) stops the walk. A newly revealed column can only close paths, so a reveal never sets `drawAll`. Nothing above y 319 or below y −64 is stepped into; an eye outside −64 … 319 (flight has no altitude clamp, `src/player/fly.ts`) sets `drawAll` and starts no walk.
- **Triggers and `drawAll`:** (1) the camera's section differs from S0: a new walk starts (after the one in progress completes); the old set stays in force while the camera's section is inside the old box, and `drawAll` is set if it leaves the box before a walk seeded around it completes (a teleport, or flight faster than one section per walk). (2) A swap (an edit or an Apply) whose new `connectivity15` has a bit the old one lacks in any section (a wall dug open): `drawAll` is set and a walk starts; a swap that only removes bits keeps the set (it was a superset). (3) Reveals: after reveals, a walk starts when none is running, at most once every 250 ms while the reveal queue is not empty and once when it drains, so a disc streamed in while the camera stands still is culled (§8.1's rule keeps new columns drawn until then). One walk at a time; dirt during a walk restarts it after it completes. `drawAll` is cleared when a walk completes whose box holds the camera's section and that started after the last trigger that set it.
- **Budget:** time-sliced at 0.5 ms per frame, `performance.now()` polled every 256 pops (the master's 1.5 ms is lowered because `live`'s main p95 is 5.06 ms against the 6 ms gate). A full walk at RD 12 (11,736 sections, up to 5 pops each, 3 for most: only the sections in S0's planes reach 4 or 5, §8.1) is 0.3-1.5 ms, at RD 16 (about 21,400) 0.5-2.5 ms (**estimate**, the box start adds a few per cent; the bench row `cull.walk` measures it).
- **Off:** no walk while the X-ray is on (its pass is never culled and the terrain passes draw nothing, D24), during `warmUp` (`src/render/renderer.ts:763-765`) and before the reveal frame; `drawAll` is then set so everything draws.
- **Alternative** (this spec's decision; the builder check's question 2): a single-section seed with `drawAll` set on every camera section change until the next walk completes, which is simpler but draws everything for 1-5 frames at every section boundary.

### 8.3 RegionBatch (per section)

Passes 0-2 get an `onBeforeRender` that calls three's own (`node_modules/three/src/objects/BatchedMesh.js:1523-1684`, three r186) and then compacts `_multiDrawStarts`, `_multiDrawCounts` and `_indirectTexture.image.data` in place, keeping entry k only if `drawAll` or `stamp[sectionOf[indirect[k]]] ≥ shown`, and sets `_multiDrawCount`. Order is kept, so translucent sorting is unaffected; cost O(instances listed), about 12k reads a frame (≤ 0.1 ms, **estimate**). Draw calls do not change; a culled section saves its vertices and fragments. A unit test pins r186's private field names, as `xrayBeforeRender` relies on them (`src/render/regionBatch.ts:172-179`).

### 8.4 RegionMesh (per region arena)

An arena (region, pass 0-2) none of whose columns has a drawn section (§8.1's rule) gets the draw range (0, 0); the X-ray arenas keep their own toggle (`src/render/regionMesh.ts:341`, `:620-626`). Before SP6b several writers set an arena's draw range: `setHighWater` (`:318-323`), called by the compaction (`:400`), the growth (`:432`) and the last row's release (`:563`); the upload's first-row span raising `highWater` (`:478-481`); and `setXray` (`:620-626`), which touched only the X-ray arenas' visibility and must now re-apply the range to the terrain and water arenas. A reveal or a hide writes no draw range (the plan's Spec correction 21). One function, `applyDrawRange(a)`, derives it from (culled, X-ray on for the terrain and water passes, D24, the high-water mark) and every writer, `setXray` included, calls it, so a span change cannot undo culling or D24 for a frame. The span writers alone would leave an arena at (0, 0) after the camera moves or a wall is dug open, so on each frame where the culler's `shown` or `drawAll` changed RegionMesh judges each region again and re-runs `applyDrawRange` over its pass 0-2 arenas (O(arenas × columns × 24), no allocation; the plan's Spec correction 23), in its step before the draw (`sortTranslucent`, after the culler's); between such frames no writer or reveal changes the verdict, and a column revealed into a culled arena shows once the reveals' walk completes. A (0, 0) range still issues the draw call in r186 (`:33-36`'s note): region culling saves vertex and fragment work, not calls.

### 8.5 The master's main-thread row

"cave BFS (on change, time-sliced) ≤ 1.0 ms amortised" (master :1683) is measured as `cullMainMeanMs` (the walk's and the filter's main-thread time over L13's frames) and gated ≤ 1.0 (§12.6); a mean hides spikes, so `cullMainMaxMs` (the largest per-frame walk plus filter time; the filter is not sliced) is recorded and gated at the Chrome commit at a bound set from its measurement (proposed ≤ 1.5 ms); G1's main p95 ≤ 6 ms must hold.

## 9. The UI (D8-D11, D14)

### 9.1 The map's cave-biome layer

- `LAYERS` (`src/gen/map/layers.ts:21`) gains `'caveBiome'` at the end; `layerStage` (`:29-33`) returns `'terrain'` for it, so its tiles key on the terrain hash (D15's alternative would give it its own stage); `layerColor(layer, p, k)` (`:52-85`) cannot paint it from a `PointRecord` and y alone, since the border noises live in the GenContext (`cavePickerOf(ctx)`), so the layer is painted by `caveBiomeColor(picker, p, y, k)` (`src/gen/map/layers.ts`), and the tile painter, which holds `ctx`, passes `cavePickerOf(ctx)` and y (`paintTileAbortable` and `paintTile` gain `y?`, required for this layer; the preview too: `src/gen/map/tile.ts:74`, `:117`, `:163`); `layerColor`'s `caveBiome` case is the layer's relief part, the greyed relief. The layer is not a `needsRelief` layer: the shaded fine pass keeps only `surfaceEst`, water, river and lake fields per pixel, so the layer paints in the unshaded path, which samples each pixel whole, its relief part unshaded (k = 1) (Spec correction 13).
- `MapTileMsg` gains `y` (`src/workers/protocol.ts:75`, `:510`); the tile key gains `:y<n>` for this layer only (`src/ui/map/tileSource.ts:33-36`; `src/ui/map/mapView.ts:141`). The pool's `TileRequest` gains `y` as well, which the canvas sets to the view's y for this layer only and passes to the key, both when it requests and in its cancel filter (ruling P50).
- `MapView` gains `caveY` (an integer in −64 … 319; absent, y 0), written to the hash only when the layer is caveBiome and read as optional (`src/ui/map/mapState.ts:20-26`, `:45-51`, `:66`), so every existing hash, SP6a's review links included, stays byte-identical and valid; a view keeps its `caveY` across layer switches. With the layer selected the toolbar shows a "y" slider and number field (−64 … 319, step 4; a typed y is rounded and clamped).
- A pixel: §1.3's choice at the pixel's position and y, without jitter, from the `PointRecord`'s climate and `surfaceEst` (`src/gen/column/columnPoint.ts:30-43`) plus the two border noises; the relief greyed where y > surfaceEst. The `PointRecord` values are exact per point, while T reads their quart-bilinear interpolation (`readFieldHalo`, §1.2), so between quart corners the map's border can sit a few blocks off T's (the map is a guide to where each biome lies, not a voxel-exact picture; alternative: paint from a ColumnSample lattice per tile, which costs a column-stage pass per 16 × 16 pixels). Tiles carry per-pixel cave-biome ids (255 above the ground), and the canvas gains a cave-biome highlight (`highlightCaveBiome`) that dims every id but the chosen one on this layer's tiles, as the biome layer's highlight does (`mapView.ts:64`, `:92`); the biome table's surface-biome highlight never masks this layer's tiles. The Caves tab's biome-mode legend rows drive it while the map shows the layer (Task 47); `?map` has no legend, so the highlight is absent there (ruling P51). The hover readout (`src/ui/map/hoverPanel.ts:14-26`) gains the line "cave biome at y n: lush (depth 0.42)" ("none (above the ground)" above surfaceEst). The readout receives only a `ColumnPoint` from the worker's point job, and the border and depth noises live in the worker's GenContext (Spec correction 13's limit), so the point job gains an optional `y` (an integer in −64 … 319) and its reply an optional `caveBiome: { id, depth }`, the caveBiome layer's pixel at the point (`caveBiomeIdAt`, 255 above the ground, and the depth of `caveBiomeAtPoint` over the point's values and `cavePickerOf(ctx)`); `point(x, z, priority?, y?)` on the pool, the hover's pool and the latency hook's, each returning the `ColumnPoint` with `caveBiome` when the reply carries it (ruling P49). The readout sends the map's `caveY` while the layer is caveBiome and no y otherwise (the plan's Spec correction 24).
- Cost: about the relief layer's plus four noise2 per pixel (**estimate**).

### 9.2 The slice field `biome3d` and the Voxels mode

- `SliceField` gains `'biome3d'` (`src/gen/inspect/fieldSlice.ts:271`, `:299`, `:366-369`; `src/workers/protocol.ts:317`, `:347` refusable); its samples go through the `caves` field's `CAVE_RUN` grouping (`fieldSlice.ts:476-501`) and read the tag's nibble: 0 open air, 1 + biome for tagged air, `CAVE_SLICE_SOLID` 16 for solid (`src/core/constants.ts:207`).
- `FieldChoice` gains it with the label "Cave biome", a label map replacing the ternary (`src/ui/inspector/sliceModel.ts:61-62`, `:70`, `:95-100`); it joins the cave gesture hold (`:27`, `:663`).
- Gate: `slice.biome3d128` p50 ≤ 300 ms. `slice.caves128` runs at about 280 ms (SP6a §14); if either passes 300 ms, the `deriveSeed` memo per kind (`src/gen/caves/carvers.ts:322`) is taken (D32). Taken at D25's checkpoint (Task 25's lever 25f: `slice.caves128` read 370.6 ms with σ_b, 266 ms after it, `carverSourceSeed`).
- The Cross-section's Voxels mode colours dry cave air by biome in biome mode (`src/ui/crossSection/voxels.ts:8`, `:150`).

### 9.3 The Caves tab and the biome finder

- Order (`src/ui/map/cavesTab.ts:131-187`): X-ray (X); **Colour by** (Cave type / Cave biome); band line; sliders; All depths; the legend, following the mode; radius; "Cave slice" (opens `caves` or `biome3d` by the mode); **"Show cave biomes on the map"** (the layer at `caveSliceY`, `cavesTab.ts:120-123`); "Cave parameters"; **"Cave biome parameters"** (reveals `caves.biomes.lush.hMin`; `panel.reveal` needs a leaf, `src/ui/paramPanel/panel.ts:130-141`); Find.
- **Find a cave biome:** "Nearest lush cave", "Nearest dripstone cave", "Nearest abyss"; texts "Lush cave at x, y, z · d blocks away", "No lush cave found within 1024 blocks", "No lush cave found in 2 s". `FindCaveMsg` gains an optional `biome` (`src/workers/protocol.ts:164-167`); `nearestCave` (`src/gen/caves/tags.ts:452-604`) gains a biome mode: (1) tagged voxels with that nibble in loaded columns; (2) beyond them, a 2D prefilter on a 16-block grid of column-stage points whose climate meets the biome's box and whose depth window meets −64 … 319, then `caveTagAt` at density-cell lattice voxels inside the window. Radius 1,024 as a new `FIND_BIOME_RADIUS` beside `FIND_RADIUS` (512, "the largest a `findCave` may ask", stays for kinds, `src/workers/protocol.ts:155-158`), with `findCaveProblem`'s range check taking the biome radius when `biome` is set; budget 2 s (`FIND_BUDGET_MS`, `:159-160`). If D25 finds the abyss sparser than about one per 2 km at seed 42, the button's text says so ("No abyss found within 1024 blocks" stays honest).
- `?map` keeps no Caves tab (`src/ui/map/layout.ts:16-31`); there the layer and the slice field find biomes.

### 9.4 Keys, help, tips

- Action `xrayColor`, `code('KeyC', false)`, label "X-ray colours: cave type or cave biome", group 'view', since 'SP6b' (`src/player/keymap.ts:100-104`); plain C is unbound (copy is Ctrl+C, `:92`, and a `ctrl: false` binding never matches with Ctrl, `:20-24`). The action count 43 → 44 (`test/unit/keymap.test.ts:54`, `test/unit/tips.test.ts:13`).
- The help's Keys table is generated (`src/ui/help.ts:62-75`). The tip (`src/ui/loading/tips.ts:35`): "{xray} shows the caves through the rock; {xrayColor} colours them by cave type or cave biome; {xrayDown} and {xrayUp} move the depth band, {xrayAll} shows every depth; {nightVision} is night vision." The first-X notice (`src/ui/world/worldPage.ts:2204-2205`) names C.

### 9.5 The strip and the F3 HUD (D8)

One top container `.world-top` (absolute; top, left and right 0.5rem; `display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; align-items: flex-start; gap: 0.5rem; pointer-events: none; z-index: 2`) holds the HUD then the strip. The HUD becomes a static flex item (`src/ui/styles.css:42` loses its position); the strip becomes a static flex item with `margin-inline: auto` and `max-width: 40rem` (`styles.css:66-69`). Flex items cannot overlap; with the HUD hidden (`styles.css:44`) the strip centres alone. `mountWorldHud` and `createXrayStrip` mount into it (`worldPage.ts:5217`, `:5231`; the bench's strip `:4689`).

## 10. Failure modes and review focus

- **A straight or grid-aligned biome border:** §1.2's perturbation and §1.4's jitter; `B3.straightBorder`, `B3.borderLattice`, `B3.zoneAperiodicity`; the `biomeBorder` review link.
- **A seam at chunk borders** (two columns reading a shared position differently): `readFieldHalo` and its two-column unit test (§1.2); `B3.borderLattice`.
- **An abrupt cut between two biomes' blocks** (the jitter off, or a rule reading a different biome than the X-ray): rocks and air read one function (`caveBiomeAt`, D16-D17); the recorded transition width per biome pair (§12.2); the review shot.
- **A level line where abyss rock meets deepslate on a wall** (rock against rock, which the air-to-air border parts do not see): `B3.abyssRockStraight` (§12.2); its levers: `caves.biomes.depthWarp` 10 → 16, then the depth noise's λ 96 → 48.
- **The X-ray's colour disagreeing with the blocks:** both read the nibble; `R1.xrayBiome` 0.
- **A biome too rare or absent in a window** (the abyss under F1): D25's checkpoint and its levers; `B3.abyssMin`.
- **A moss carpet under a plain cave** (a floor reading the rock's biome): D18's `floor` side reads the cave air above; a unit test with a run whose rock is lush and whose air is plain.
- **A ceiling rule on a bedrock-resting run:** D18's `ceiling` side requires tagged air below; a unit test.
- **The fast path changing output:** §3.5's fuzz test against full evaluation on `treeMutator` trees.
- **T over budget** (the fast path lost, the abyss through the tree): `terrain.real` and `terrain.islands` gates; `caveBiome.column`; D25's levers.
- **The floor too high or too steep** (C4's lowest band below 2 %, C5's thinnest median below 4, C7's vertical step): D25's checkpoint; the levers `caves.floor.y` −58 → −60 → −62 (the range's end, §5.2), then `caves.floor.span` 8 → 4.
- **The floor too flat** (`C7.bottomFloorSpread` below 6 blocks, `C7.bottomFlatRatio` above 1.5, or the checkpoint's screenshot reading as a plane): `caves.floor.amp` 5 → 7, then the floor noise's λ 48 → 32; these levers add relief and never flatten it.
- **Bedrock still exposed** (a family or carver under the floor's reach, or air the floor's terms do not close: density air of kind 9 or a preset's body air): `C7.bedrockExposed` 0 on the default and preset groups, measured on every group before it is gated (§12.3); §5.2's constraint holds over the leaves' ranges.
- **Grass or snow under a cave ceiling:** S1 `buried`, `grassNoSky`, `grassSky0` (rule 4 places none of grass_block, snow_block, red_sand).
- **Deepslate share broken below y 0:** S2's `deepslateBelow0` counts deepslate / (stone + deepslate), so the new blocks leave both counts; the strata place no stone.
- **The abyss lit:** no new emitter (a unit test); the recorded `L2.abyssLit`.
- **Worlds saved before SP6b keeping the flat floor and plain caves:** D29's migration and its tests.
- **A wrongly culled section:** `R3.wronglyCulled` 0 every tier, with adversarial poses; the half-done-walk rule (§8.1).
- **Pop-in when the camera moves, a column is revealed or a wall is dug open:** the seed box and `drawAll` (§8.2); new columns draw until a walk saw them (§8.1); unrevealed columns count as open; `R3.popIn` 0 (§12.4); R7's `partialColumnFrames` and `mixedEpochFrames` 0; L13's frames.
- **Pop-in at the screen's edge while turning** (only if D25's culling checkpoint takes its frustum lever, §12.5): the lever's 10° margin; its cost is named at the checkpoint.
- **Culling the X-ray:** no walk while it is on; the L14 part `xrayCulledSections` 0.
- **Culling too weak underground** (a cave open to the sky floods the walk): D25's culling checkpoint and its lever.
- **Main-thread spikes from the walk or the filter:** the 0.5 ms slice; `cullMainMeanMs`, `cullMainMaxMs`, `caveMainP95`, `mediumStreamMainP95`.
- **three's private fields renamed on an upgrade:** the unit test pinning r186's names.
- **Stutter with the X-ray in biome mode or on a mode switch:** L14's biome segment (`xrayBiomeP95`, `xrayBiomeOver33`, `xrayColorToggleMaxMs`).
- **The strip over the HUD again:** the smoke's rect check.
- **CI's wall past 6 min:** §13's Stage 2, in order.

## 11. What is kept

SP6a's cave shapes above the floor (every family, carver, cap and the wet roof; `sp6a.carver.paths` unchanged), the tag's kinds (bits 0-3), the X-ray's type colouring as default, its band, film and keys; the light floor and night vision; SP5c's editing, clipboard and history; SP5b's persistence (`wi10` unchanged; the cave channel is not persisted; the params schema moves to 5 only to migrate the built-in trees, D29); the column stage (`sp2a.*` and `sp3e.column.point.*` unchanged); the surface biomes and their zoom; rules 1-3 (`sp3c.registry` unchanged); every existing key's meaning; the map page `?map` (no Caves tab); every existing map layer and hash.

## 12. Metrics and tests

### 12.1 B3: shares per 8 × 8 km (D26)

- **Population:** cave air = tagged voxels (kind ≠ 0) that are air with no fluid; each counted under its nibble.
- **Where:** the default profile's T1 scatter (`terrain-default`, §13): its ±16,384-block square is 4 × 4 windows of 8 × 8 km, 512 columns per window on quick (256 fast; 512 × 4 seeds full). No new generation.
- **Gated parts** (every tier, at the tuning task): `B3.lushMin`, `B3.dripstoneMin`, `B3.abyssMin` = the smallest share of cave air over the windows, ≥ 0.005 (master :1505); `B3.windowAir` ≥ 50,000 cave-air voxels per window (an insufficient sample fails rather than passes).
- **Recorded:** per biome the share by depth band (0.1 bins of §1.2's depth), by 16-block y band and by cave kind; plain's share; every window's values; large_biomes per 32 × 32 km window (its climate is zoomed × 4, master §3.2, so an 8 km window can lack a biome; not gated); `cave_heavy`'s shares (§12.3).

### 12.2 B3: borders and zones (D26)

Over the caves passes' contiguous 32 × 32-chunk regions (`test/metrics/caves.metric.ts`): the six default-group regions (`caves`), the three preset regions (`caves-presets`), and in the new `caves-biomes` job (§13) `cave_heavy`'s two regions and one new **biome region**: a 32 × 32-chunk region of the default profile at seed '42' chosen by a climate-only scan (as §1.6's: `samplePoint` at the 64 centres of a 64-block grid, read with the raw boxes, each box holding **at least 8 of the 64 points**, `BIOME_SCAN_MIN_POINTS`, ruling R21; with one point per box the first candidate's lush | plain borders fall short of the 2,000 below on fast) to hold all three biomes, then a T check (some 8 × 8-chunk tile holding stored nibbles 1, 2 and 3, ruling P33), so the abyss's borders are sampled (the six default regions may hold few or none; recon-tests :31-34). The cave grid packs `tag & 15 | SOLID 16 | FLUID 32` into a byte (`test/harness/caveMetrics.ts:87-90`, `:141`); its free bits 6-7 hold the biome. Each border part is computed per job over the regions it holds, and gated on the worst job's value (as C7-C9 gate the worst group); within a job it is computed overall and per biome pair, the job's overall value being the one gated. Which jobs gate is a literal per group in `caves.metric.ts`, decided from the tuning's dry run and never from a run's own counts, so a short sample cannot turn into a pass (the plan's Spec correction 25, rulings P29, P38): a group gates the border parts when its overall border faces are ≥ 2,000 on every tier it has regions on, else it is recorded, not gated. At Task 30 all three gate: the default group read 122,960 border faces on fast, 1,203,848 on quick and 1,281,162 on full; the presets group 825,389 on quick and 2,349,531 on full (fast holds no preset region); the biomes group 295,214, 861,555 and 1,346,896 (and it gates by the pair rule below in any case). In `caves-biomes` the three pairs biome | plain each need ≥ 2,000 border faces, else the part fails as an insufficient sample (as `B3.windowAir`); lush | dripstone is recorded (their overlap may be absent from the regions).
- `B3.borderLattice`: border faces (between face-adjacent cave-air voxels of different biomes) on planes ≡ 0 (mod 4) over the mean of residues 1-3, per axis, the ratio farthest from 1 (`C7.borderPlaneRatio`'s pattern, `test/harness/caveMetrics.ts:41-45`). Proposed 0.9-1.1 (a quart-read picker puts every border on those planes).
- `B3.straightBorder`: on horizontal layers every 4 blocks, the share of border edge length in axis-aligned straight runs ≥ 8 blocks. Proposed ≤ 10 %.
- `B3.flatBorder`: border faces in axis-aligned planar patches ≥ 4 × 4 over all border faces (`flatWallDetail`'s patch finder, `caveMetrics.ts:1264-1304`). Proposed ≤ 5 %.
- `B3.abyssRockStraight`: the abyss's rock boundary on walls (rock against rock, which the parts above do not see, because they read faces between air voxels): over exposed wall voxels (solid voxels with a cave-air face neighbour) in the biome region and the default regions, the boundary edges between two vertically face-adjacent exposed wall voxels, one abyss rock and one other rock, on a wall plane both face (a horizontal side with cave air beside both), grouped into runs along x or z at one y on that wall plane; the part is the share of that edge length in runs ≥ 8 blocks (ruling P30: only `straightBorder`'s run rule is shared, not its every-4th-layer sampling, which never crosses a level line between two layers). Proposed ≤ 10 %, recorded and gated at tuning (≤ 10 %); levers in §10. Gated by the same literal rule: in a group with ≥ 2,000 of these boundary edges on every tier, so in the biomes job (the biome region: 5,948 edges on every tier); the default regions show no abyss rock on a wall (0 edges on fast, quick and full), so the default job records the part (NaN, an empty population) and does not gate it; the presets job never measures it.
- Recorded: the painted skin's mix within 2 blocks of a border per biome pair (the transition's width).
- **The regular-grid check** (pure: the picker only, no T; about 10-15 s): the picker on a 64-block lattice over 64 × 64 km at depths 0.3, 0.6 and 1.1 (about 1 M points; `samplePoint`'s climate and surfaceEst, the picker's border and depth noises, y = Y0 − D·depth). `B3.zoneAperiodicity`: per biome indicator, C8's ratio at lags 256 … 16,384, the smallest; proposed ≥ 0.9. C8's ratio at lag L is Σ|m(p) − m(p+L)| / 2f(1 − f) over the pairs, 1 minus the indicator's correlation at L: it measures autocorrelation, not periodicity, so zones that follow the climate fields' 1,600-2,400-block wavelengths read about 0.2-0.4 at lag 256 and the proposed bound is expected to miss by design (the plan's Spec correction 19). Its periodicity form, `B3.zonePeriodicDip` = min over L ≥ 1,024 of ratio(L) / max(ratio at lags < L), per indicator and axis (a periodic field dips well below 1 at its period; a smooth random one rises and saturates near 1), is recorded beside it with the ratio per lag; the gated form and its bound are the user's choice at the tuning task. Recorded: `B3.zoneClarkEvans` (zone centroids of patches ≥ 4 points, no edge correction; a lattice ≈ 2.0 (square) to 2.15 (hexagonal), random ≈ 1; proposed 0.7-1.5; the plan's Spec correction 18) and `B3.zoneSizeSpread` (log-sd of patch areas, proposed ≥ 0.8). **As chosen** (Task 30, ruling U4, the user's): `B3.zonePeriodicDip` ≥ 0.9 is the gated zone part, in the biomes job (`metricTest('B3', ['zonePeriodicDip'], …, { outName: 'B3.zones' })`; measured 0.972, the same on every tier); recorded beside it in `.out/B3.zones.json`: `zoneAperiodicity` (0.272), `zoneClarkEvansMin` and `zoneClarkEvansMax` (the smallest and the largest indicator's value, both kept rather than the one farthest from 1) and `zoneSizeSpread` (1.665).

### 12.3 C1-C9 and the bottom (D6, D26)

- **C6.latticeBias, a measurement correction** (Task 19, ruling U1, the user's): each y residue's carve rate is read over its lattice-neutral local mean instead of the band's mean: per band, a residue's tagged voxels over Σ voxels(y)·m(y) over its rows, m(y) the rows' carve rate averaged over y − 4 … y + 4 with weights ½, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, ½ (÷ 8; `W9`, one period of 8, so a pure period-8 modulation reads as over the band's mean and a depth trend linear over 9 rows reads 1; the rows y −60 … 43 are counted for it); x and z residues as before. SP6a's 16-block bands were meant to keep depth variation out of C6 ("so depth variation is not read as lattice bias"); the floor's closure is a ramp inside the band −56 … −41 (its carve rate rises about 22 × across it), which SP6a's reading took for a bias: 1.666 on fast, 1.548 on quick, with no floor setting under 1.25 (a flat plane at −57 reads 1.29); on W9 1.112 and 1.111 (inv-19-c6.md). On the floorless tree W9 reads 1.101 on fast and 1.111 on quick (1.165 and 1.123 as SP6a read it). The bound stays 1.25 (re-judged at Task 20 with σ_b: W9 1.116 on fast and 1.111 on quick, as defined 1.881 and 1.784, `C6.floorFaceLattice` 1.60 and 1.46; 1.25 kept). Recorded beside it in `.out/C6.json`, not gated: `C6.latticeBiasAsDefined` (SP6a's reading) and `C6.floorFaceLattice`, the floor faces (a tagged voxel whose voxel below is solid) of the rows y −56 … −33 at y ≡ 0 (mod 8, the density cells' corner layers) over their W9 expectation (each residue's in `caves.C1-C6.json`; 1.45 on fast without the floor), the check that the floor does not terrace on the corner layers, which W9 reads near the floor's knee with ±0.1 of curvature. SP12's 1.1 is restated on W9 as ≤ 1.09: the as-defined 1.1 cut the SP6a tree's corner excess on quick from 12.3 % to 10 %, and the same cut of its W9 excess (11.1 %) is 9 %. No quadratic-exact window (it reads the floor band worse, inv-19-c6-verify.md).
- **C1-C9** stay gated at their bounds (`test/thresholds.ts:86-115`). Rule 4 does not move the tag, so only the floor moves them: C1's void share (the lost volume), C4's lowest band (y −56 … −41, ≥ 2 %), C5's largest and thinnest (median exactly 4 on non-noodle components today, ruling R32), C7's `flatWallShare` (should fall) and `verticalStep` (y −60 … 120, ≤ 8.1), C8's `largeShare`.
- **New C7 parts** (per group, the worst; recon-tests §5.3):
  - `C7.bedrockExposed`: cave-air voxels face-adjacent to a bedrock voxel, max 0. It holds by §5.2's constraint for the noise families and the carvers only (S_b is added to the caves slot and the noodle, `defaultTrees.ts:289-291`, not to the body), so density air of kind 9 or a preset's body air could still touch the bedrock dither: it is written to `.out` on every group in the commit that lands the floor, and gated in the next commit if it reads 0 everywhere (a non-zero reading goes to the floor's terms first). It is never committed before the floor (it fails there).
  - `C7.bottomPlane`: flat floor faces at y −63 over all flat faces; today 15.7-34.5 %; max 0.01, gated from the floor's commit.
  - `C7.bottomFlatRatio`: the share of flat floor faces (≥ 6 × 6 patches) among wall faces for cave voxels at y ≤ −40, over the same share at y −39 … 0 (−40 is the bottom's); proposed ≤ 1.5 (the bottom no flatter than caves above it), recorded. At the tuning (Task 30) it read ≤ 1.10 in every group on quick, but 2.34 on fast (the default group's two 16 × 16 regions, flat patches at y −41/−40) and 1.78 on full (archipelago's four regions; per region 0.11-5.70, the flat floors gathering on the density cells' corner layers y −48, −40 and −32), so the proposed bound misses on two tiers and its bound is the user's (Task 30 Step 4). **As chosen** (ruling U5, the user's): a regression guard at max 2.05 (the full worst 1.78 × 1.15), gated on quick and full and recorded on fast (`tiers`, as `C5.largest`: fast's two 16 × 16 regions read it high by their size), re-judged in SP12 (§16).
  - `C7.bottomFloorSpread` (the technical review's M2): for each cave component of ≥ 10k voxels with floor faces at y ≤ −40, the 10th-90th percentile range of the y of its floor faces at y ≤ −32; the median over those components; ≥ 6 blocks (§5.2 expects 7-8), recorded until the tuning and gated from it on every tier (Task 30). With `C7.bottomPlane` and the checkpoint's screenshot it is the check that the new floor is not a plane at another height.
  - Recorded: the flat-floor histogram y −64 … −32, `C7.bottomFloorPeak` (C4's `floorPeak` restricted to floor faces at y ≤ −32), cave air per y band at the bottom, `flatWallShare` and `C4.floorPeak` before and after.
- **`cave_heavy`:** 2 regions at seed '42' on quick (4 seeds on full) in `caves-biomes` (§13): C1's `voidShare` recorded against 10-22 % with `activeFrom: 'SP12'` (the fixture's pattern, `test/unit/metric.test.ts:9`), C2 and C9 recorded.
- **Interlocks re-asserted:** S2 `bedrockFloor` ≥ 1 (`test/thresholds.ts:121`), T4 `exposedBedrock` 0 (`:66`); S1's parts (`:117-120`; rule 4 places no grass_block, snow_block or red_sand, `test/metrics/surface.metric.ts:43-44`); L2 `enclosedLit` 0 (`test/thresholds.ts:142`), with a recorded `L2.abyssLit` (abyss cave air with sky light > 0 or block light > 0; expected about 0 apart from openings to the sky) beside `L2.abyssAir`, the abyss cave air counted (L2's sites need not hold any; 0 reads "not sampled", ruling P37).

### 12.4 R3 and the raycast visibility reference (D22)

- **Function under test:** `caveCulling.ts`'s walk, called by Node with each section's `connectivity15` and the camera.
- **Reference** (`test/harness/refs.ts`, `raycastVisible`; master :1430): from the eye, 3D DDA rays in 6 × 128² cube-map directions out to the region's edge; a ray stops at the first opaque full cube (`PASS` opaque, `fullFaces` 63, connectivity's predicate, `src/mesh/greedy.ts:151`) and passes cutout, partial shapes and water; every section a ray enters, the stopping one included, is "seen". A ray-sampled set is a lower bound of visibility, so a "wrongly culled" count never accuses falsely.
- **Data:** the proto blocks of the T regions the `caves-biomes` job already holds in process (`test/metrics/caves.metric.ts:412`, `:464`): `cave_heavy`'s two regions and the biome region (§12.2); `connectivity15` computed from each section's padded copy (`src/mesh/connectivity.ts:58`); poses in the regions' central 8 × 8 chunks so RD 12 fits.
- **Poses** (fast 8, quick 32, full 64; deterministic): tagged cave air at several depths and kinds (eye ≥ 16 below the pre-cave top: "underground"); surface poses; eyes within 0.5 block of a section face, edge and corner; pitch ±90; an eye inside an opaque voxel; an eye above y 319 and one below y −64 (`drawAll`, §8.2); a sealed room opened by an edited wall; a pose with one column of the disc marked not revealed (counted as open, §8.2).
- **Parts:** `R3.wronglyCulled` = seen sections the walk did not stamp, max 0, every tier, gated from its commit; `R3.popIn` = over pose paths replayed frame by frame (a walk through a doorway into a cavern, flight at two sections per walk, a teleport, a wall dug open into a sealed cave), the sections the reference sees at frame n that §8.1's draw rule hides at frame n, with the walk sliced at a fixed budget of 2,000 pops per frame (deterministic in Node), max 0, every tier, gated from its commit (it holds by §8.2's construction); `R3.culledUnderground` = 1 − drawn non-empty sections / non-empty sections in range (non-empty: a section with a non-air voxel that has a non-opaque face neighbour, computed from the blocks without meshing), pooled over underground poses, ≥ 0.5 (master :1538), gated after D25's culling checkpoint. Recorded: per-pose culled shares (p10, median), the surface poses' share, the walk's over-draw (stamped / seen), pops per walk.
- **Where:** a describe in `test/metrics/caves.metric.ts`'s `caves-biomes` group, on the regions that job holds (so R3 generates no region of its own; a separate file in the render shard would regenerate them cold, about 12 s per region, `.github/scripts/metricShards.mjs`'s `caves` comment); the walk stays in `src/render/culling/caveCulling.ts`, the reference in `test/harness/refs.ts` and the pose builder in `test/harness/cull.ts`. Cost about 0.1-0.3 s per pose plus about 30 µs per section flood (**estimate**): +20-60 s on quick in `caves-biomes`.
- **Unit tests carrying the "0 wrongly culled" proof** (the reference's rays are about 0.7° apart, about 2.3 blocks at 192 blocks, so a section seen only through a 1-voxel hole can be missed by R3): 1-voxel apertures at several distances and at section faces, edges and corners; an L tunnel; a sealed room; the seed box's edges.

### 12.5 Determinism, bench and the checkpoints

- **DT1:** unchanged harness; the nibble and `caveBiomeQ` are inside `regionHash` (`src/metrics/region.ts:84`, `:93-95`); `DT1.isolated` and `DT1.carverCache` cover the picker's halo positions.
- **DT2:** new `caveBiomeProbe` max 0 (§2.3), gated from its commit; `surfaceProbeBulk`, `surfaceReference` and `editedSurfaceReference` re-asserted with `caveBiome` and `strata` once the reference evaluator and `treeMutator` learn them (`test/metrics/region.metric.ts:443`; `presets.metric.ts:120`).
- **R1:** `R1.xray` gains `xrayBiome`: each X-ray face's 4 biome bits against the nibble at its hollow voxel, mismatches 0 (`bruteXrayFaces`, `test/harness/refs.ts:175`, adds the biome to its keys).
- **P1** (`npm run bench`, the reference-machine protocol of SP6a's exit: turbo, load < 1): `terrain.real` ≤ 13 / 20 ms and `terrain.islands` ≤ 14.5 / 26.5 ms (D27; SP6a's exit 10.457 / 16.758 ms); `surface.column` re-baselined (0.747 ms, `test/baselines.json:94`); new `caveBiome.column` (recorded), `cull.walk` at RD 12 and RD 16 (recorded), `slice.biome3d128` ≤ 300 ms; `slice.caves128` ≤ 300 ms; `BENCH_EXEMPT` for rows rising by design. Master §7's "25 surface + about 300 cave quarts" is restated as measured (cave-air quarts per column p50 60, p90 177, max 575, **measured**, recon-gen §1.7).
- **D25, the generator checkpoint** (local, after the picker, rule 4 with the fast path, the floor, B3's shares per window and C7's bottom parts exist; before B3's border, abyss-rock and grid parts, the `caves-biomes` job, the tuning and the golden record). Measured: B3 per window and band; the abyss's air with the floor and without (`caves.floor.y` set below the world, as a measuring run only); `terrain.real` and `terrain.islands`; `caveBiome.column`; C1, C4's lowest band and `floorPeak`, C5's thinnest, C7's `flatWallShare`, `C7.bottomFloorSpread` and `C7.bedrockExposed` on every group; a screenshot at the `bottomCavern` pose; the cave channel's shared memory at RD 12. **Rule:** if `terrain.real` p50 > 12 ms, find the fast-path leak first (count evaluated voxels per class), then measure; if `B3.abyssMin` < 0.005 in any window, levers in order: `abyss.depthMin` 1.0 → 0.9 → 0.85, then `abyss.eMax` −0.5 → −0.4, then (with the user, at the checkpoint) the window gate's form; if C4's lowest band < 2 % or C5's thinnest median < 4, the floor's lowering levers of §10; if `C7.bottomFloorSpread` < 6 or the screenshot reads as a plane, its relief levers (§10); if `terrain.islands` passes its gate, §3.5's islands lever. A miss after the levers goes to the user with the measurements.
- **D25, the culling checkpoint** (Node, on the pure walk and the cached regions, before the renderer integration). **Rule:** if `R3.culledUnderground` < 0.5, the lever is the master's frustum restriction, widened by 10° on each side, with the walk re-run on a turn of more than 10° and R3's reference cast inside the widened frustum; a miss after it goes to the user. Its cost, named at the checkpoint if it is taken: the frustum walk is time-sliced like the walk, so a turn faster than the widened margin per walk shows sections popping in at the screen's edge for a few frames (`R3.popIn` then adds turning paths).

## 13. CI impact (D30)

**The work item** (the plan's first work after §14.1, on its own commits, before any generator change; recon-tests §4). Against SP6a's runs (exit run 42769333417 at 9:01; later runs 8:10-8:31, wall ≈ 0:20 + the longest job; the long poles `metrics-quick (terrain)` 354-502 s and `metrics-quick (caves)` 360-470 s; runs on one `src/**` vary by up to ±45 %):

| lever | change | why it is exact |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | `apply.metric.ts` out of T1's job, into `terrain-default` (`.github/scripts/metricShards.mjs:20`) | a move between shards |
| L2 | T1-T5 quick by profile: `terrain-default` (8,192 columns, T4's default regions, apply) and `terrain-large` (32,768 columns, T4's large_biomes regions); the file reads `METRICS_PROFILES` (absent: both) | every gated part is the worse profile or a sum with max 0 (`test/metrics/terrain.metric.ts:120-125`, `:243-244`) |
| L3 | S1-S3, B4, B2 quick by profile: `surface-default` (with `islandBranchOnGround`) and `surface-large` | the same (`test/metrics/surface.metric.ts:182-198`, `:230-233`) |
| L4 | caves quick by group: `caves` (the six default-group regions: C1-C6, the default group's C7-C9, their `carverReference`) and `caves-presets` (the three preset regions, the pure parts, `islandCapAir`); SP6b adds a third, `caves-biomes` (§13's additions) | C7-C9 gate the worst group; construction parts are max 0 per region; C1-C6 stay pooled in one job (`test/harness/caveMetrics.ts:945-990`, `:1804-1868`) |
| L5 | T1's analysis into the region workers (as S1's already is, `test/harness/surfaceScatterWorker.ts`); the main thread sums | the accumulators are sums, histograms and a sorted list; a unit test pins sliced-and-summed == serial on a fast-size sample |
| L6 | caves' `measure` and `referenceCarve` per region in 4 worker threads reading the cached dumps | per-region counts are pure functions of the region |

The shard tables name a shard by (file, env) (`metricShards.mjs:18-44`, `shardLib.mjs:18-28`, `:29-56`; `test/unit/shards.test.ts:24-73`), and `coverageProblems` accepts a file named by several shards whose envs partition it. Cleanups taken with it: the terrain and surface shards drop their region-cache step (they never save one); `slowest.mjs` adds per-test rows for metric jobs. Order: L1-L4 (tables and env) → push → two green runs measured; then L5, L6 → push → two green runs. Expected (recon-tests §4): wall 3:45-4:15 on fast runners, 5:00-5:20 on slow ones; runner time about 54-59 min (from 52-56); 18 parallel test jobs (≤ the Free plan's 20; the account's plan is confirmed in the work item). **Budget for the work item: ≤ 5 min 30 s wall in two consecutive green cold runs.**

**Expected job times** after Stage 1 (recon-tests §4, low = fast runner, high = slow): `terrain-large` 2:50-5:00 with L5 (4:00-6:35 without: after L2 it holds 32,768 columns against `terrain-default`'s 8,192 and stays the long pole until L5 lands); `terrain-default` with apply 2:50-4:15; `surface-large` 2:50-4:55; `surface-default` 1:15-2:15; `caves` cold 3:00-3:35 with L6 (5:00-5:50 without); `caves-presets` cold 2:20-3:55; the unchanged jobs up to about 4:35. The work item records each job's measured time beside these.

**SP6b's own additions** (**estimates**): T rises by the picker and rule 4 (+5-15 % on every terrain-generating job); B3 rides on `terrain-default`'s scatter (+5-10 % of that job); the border and bottom parts on the caves passes (+5-15 s per region before L6); a new quick job **`caves-biomes`** (`caves.metric.ts` with a group env, as L4's split): `cave_heavy`'s two regions (about 20 s cold plus 60 s measured), the biome region (about 12 s cold plus 30 s measured), R3 (+20-60 s), the regular-grid check (+15-20 s): about 3:10-4:00 cold. Putting these into `caves-presets` instead would take it to about 4:30-6:40 on a slow runner, past the exit bound. The jobs become 19, within the Free plan's 20 concurrent jobs while one run is active (overlapping pushes queue). Unit +20-40 s, the slow files into `unit (heavy)`. Region caches are keyed on `src/**`, `test/harness/**` and the lockfile (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:128`, `:161`), so every SP6b push that changes `src/**` starts the caves jobs cold (441-470 s cold before L6).

**Why 5 min 30 s and 6 min, not 3 min 30 s.** SP6a's spec set 3 min 30 s (its §11); SP6a's runs took 6 min 37 s to 9 min 1 s, and the user accepted 9 min for SP6a and deferred the reduction to SP6b (ruling R23; the brief asks this spec to plan it and set SP6b's budget). Stage 1's expected wall is 3:45-4:15 even on fast runners and 5:00-5:20 on slow ones, so 3 min 30 s is out of reach without sample reductions, which R23 rules out; 5 min 30 s is Stage 1's slow-runner expectation with a margin for the ±45 % spread between runs, and 6 min leaves room for SP6b's own additions above. Alternative: 5 min 0 s at the exit, which would need Stage 2 from the start.

**Cold caches.** Every bound in this section applies to runs whose region caches miss (a change under `src/**` or `test/harness/**`, the cache key's inputs): a warm run says nothing about the caves jobs' generation. L5 and L6 change `test/harness/**`, so their runs are cold; for L1-L4, whose commits may hit warm caches, the cold figure is the first run after them that misses (the job log names a cache miss). A warm re-run is recorded beside each cold one.

**A miss inside the work item** (this spec's decision; the builder check's question 4): if the two cold runs after L1-L6 read above 5 min 30 s, Stage 2's items below join the work item in order, each pushed and measured, before any generator change, until two consecutive cold runs read ≤ 5 min 30 s or the list is exhausted; then the measured wall is recorded and the generator work starts (the exit bound still applies). Alternative: record the miss at once and leave Stage 2 to the exit.

**Exit criterion: CI wall ≤ 6 min 0 s** (against SP6a's 9 min, ruling R23), measured on the last two cold runs before the exit commit (the exit commit is docs-only, and CI's docs-only path skips the metrics jobs, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:170-186`, so its own run says nothing). If missed, Stage 2's items in order (those not already taken), each measured: (1) `terrain-large` and `surface-large` split into 2 column slices each with a `metrics-merge` job (slice k takes draws k, k + 2, … of the unchanged list; partial accumulators as artifacts); the caves default group merged by seed the same way; (2) `unit (rest)` into a third shard (liveness, densityFast, carve, densityDefaults, floatingIslands); (3) `persist.metric.ts` into a fast shard of its own; folding `metrics-fast (caves)` and `metrics-quick (clipboard)` into neighbours keeps the job count ≤ 20 (19 with `caves-biomes`). No sample reductions (R23). The docs-only path grows by the unit additions only; the Chrome legs, the bench and the smoke are not in CI. The Exit evidence records every measured run's job table.

### 14.2 Locks

The thresholds lock changes with each metric commit's construction parts (`C7.bedrockExposed`, `C7.bottomPlane`, `R3.wronglyCulled`, `DT2.caveBiomeProbe`, `R1.xrayBiome`), the culling checkpoint (`R3.culledUnderground`), the tuning task (B3's parts with the zone part `zonePeriodicDip`, `C7.bottomFloorSpread`, and `C7.bottomFlatRatio` as the user's regression guard on quick and full) and the Chrome commit (L13's and L14's new parts), each with a Threshold-log line and this spec in the push (`test/harness/governance.ts:54-56`). B3 and R3 gain `THRESHOLDS` rows (none today, `test/thresholds.ts`; `MetricId` has both, `src/core/ids.ts:33`, `:41`). The schema-shape lock changes with the new leaves; `test/stateIds.lock.json` appends (`test/harness/governance.ts:58`).

### 14.3 `GENERATOR_VERSION` and goldens (D28)

- **The generator push** (the floor, the nibble and `caveBiomeQ`, rule 4 in the built-in trees, the migration, the tuning): 8 → 9 in its first output-changing commit, the terrain stage 4 → 5. Within it every generator commit records from the push's base (`git checkout <base> -- test/goldens.json`, then `npm run test:goldens`), so each commit is green and the push shows one bump (`test/harness/governance.ts:46-52`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:69`). Every new schema leaf (`caves.biomes.*`, `caves.floor.*`, `caves.noises.floor`, `surface.noises.cavePatch` and `strataOffset`) lands in it, because adding leaves changes `sp1.params` (§6.1). Before it, the blocks, the condition and rule kinds (unused by built-in trees), the picker as a pure module taking its values as arguments, culling, the X-ray and the UI change no generated output and are pushed at will; no such commit sits between unpushed generator commits.
- **Allowed changes:** `generatorVersion`; `sp1.params`; `sp3a.region.T.default`, `.large_biomes`; `sp3e.region.T.amplified`, `.archipelago`, `.floating_islands`; `sp6a.region.T.caves`; `sp3c.surface.ops` (its fixture columns go through the surface probe, `src/metrics/sp3cGoldens.ts:54`, `:126-136`); `sp3b.density.default` (the default tree; the floor is a density term). **Expected unchanged:** every `sp2a.*`, `sp1.*` but params, `sp3e.column.point.*`, `sp3b.density.ops`, `sp3a.registry`, `sp3c.registry` (fixed state ranges, `src/metrics/sp3cGoldens.ts:3-4`), `sp6a.carver.paths` (σ_b acts at carve time, not on paths), `sp6a.density.ops` (it hashes a fixture tree that uses `noiseScaled`, resolved for `density.noises.detail.clampSigma`, `src/metrics/sp6aGoldens.ts:5-12`, never the default tree, so the floor does not reach it; a move is a bug; the plan's Spec correction 7).
- **New keys (62 → 65)** in `src/metrics/sp6bGoldens.ts`: `sp6b.registry` (states 58-64's byte stream over that fixed range), `sp6b.caveBiome.points` (the picker at fixed 3D points, seed '42'), `sp6b.region.T.biomes` (`regionHash` of an 8 × 8-chunk window at seed '42' holding all three biomes, found by a scan).
- **The number:** 9 at the exit; 10 if the user's check asks for a retune (its push records from its branch's base with the same allowed keys).

### 14.4 Saved worlds and preset files (D29)

- `SCHEMA_VERSION` 4 → 5 (`src/core/params/migrate.ts:14`) in the generator push's first commit, before rule 4, the floor or any other tree change (Spec correction 3: rule 4's change to `DEFAULT_SURFACE_RULES` would turn `test/unit/saves.test.ts:74-80` red, the SP5b fixture, schema 2, loading equal to `resolveProfile('default')`, until `MIGRATIONS[3]` maps the GV8 surface tree; at that commit the map sends each GV8 tree to the live built-in tree, still the same JSON, so no output sees it), with `MIGRATIONS[3]`: a stored `density.defs` canonically equal to one of SP6a's three built-in trees at `GENERATOR_VERSION` 8 (the trees at 1ac9e7b, exported once at the push base aed4e07, whose trees are 1ac9e7b's, before any tree changes, into a new `test/fixtures/gv8-density-trees.json` with its own pin test, `test/unit/gv8Trees.test.ts`, as `test/unit/sp6aTrees.test.ts` pins SP6a's; `test/fixtures/sp6a-density-trees.json` stays unchanged, because it holds SP6a's trees from before ruling R31, which that test and `MIGRATIONS[2]`'s `SP6A_TREE_HASHES` pin, `src/core/params/migrate.ts:49`) becomes SP6b's tree of that kind; a stored `surface.rules` canonically equal to `DEFAULT_SURFACE_RULES` (`src/core/params/defaultTrees.ts:358`) or floating_islands' rules (`src/core/params/presetTrees.ts:207-217`) at 1ac9e7b (hashes pinned over a new `test/fixtures/gv8-surface-trees.json`) becomes SP6b's; density and surface trees are mapped apart; a schema-3 document runs 3 → 4 first (`sp6aNoodles`, `migrate.ts:82`), which already maps SP6a's pre-R31 trees to SP6b's in one step (Spec correction 9: `SP6A_TREE_HASHES`, as 2 → 3's `SP5C_TREE_HASHES`, map to `DEFAULT_DENSITY_TREE`, `AMPLIFIED_DENSITY_TREE` and `FLOATING_DENSITY_TREE` by reference, `migrate.ts:24-28`, `:49-53`, so they reach the live trees, and 4 → 5 leaves them the live trees), with SP6a's noodle defaults 0.11 / 64 becoming 0.22 / 48; any other tree is kept (its world gets the carvers' floor shrink only, and plain caves if its rules are edited). Missing `caves.biomes.*`, floor and new noise leaves take the profile's values.
- U4 adds `test/fixtures/preset-v4.json` (schema 4, SP6a's default trees, one edited leaf) and `expected-v4.json`; `expected-v1.json` … `expected-v3.json` are regenerated with today's defaults (`test/metrics/params.metric.ts:24-30`).
- Every stored-params path goes through `migrate`: saved worlds (`src/persist/saves.ts:237-245`), `.wiworld` files and preset files (`src/core/params/presets.ts:43`). The map URL's patch carries no schema version and is read as today's.
- The world list keeps SP5b D8's line ("opens with today's terrain; your blocks stay at their coordinates", `src/ui/worldsMenu.ts:104`), which the migration keeps true; builds may stand over or inside the changed bottom.
- `test/unit/presetFile.test.ts:165`'s case (`schemaVersion: 5`, "newer than this build (4)") becomes `schemaVersion: 6` and "(5)".

## 15. Master-spec amendments made with this spec

Each item lands in the master with the commit that makes it true (not all at the spec's approval: at 441b67d the master still held the SP6b header `(size set by the SP6b spec; SP6a)` and §1's `frustum.ts caveCulling.ts`), as SP6a's §13 did; the first commit (§14.1) lands item 1's header. Each is pinned in a new describe 'master spec amendments (SP6b §15)' in `test/arch/masterSpec.test.ts`, with an `SP6B_MODULES` table checked against the files (MST:2173-2196's pattern), a header-comment paragraph (MST:104-149's pattern) and the completeness test reading this section between "## 15. Master-spec amendments made with this spec" and "## 16. Notes handed to later SPs" (MST:2474-2496's pattern). Pins that read text SP6b rewords move out of the SP6a describe into SP6b's: MST:1926 (the SP6b header), :1927 (its contents line), :1931-1932 ("set by the SP6b spec"), :1983 ("SP6b decides whether the meta holds it"), :1984 ("leaves `caveBiomeQ` 0 until SP6b"), :1991 ("`caveBiome` stays SP6b's"), :1992 ("SP6b inserts rule 4 before rule 2"), :1993 ("are decided in the SP6b spec;"), :1995 ("the 3 cave biomes arrive in SP6b."), :2007-2017 (the user-check line, once worded as delivered), :2295-2297 (the raycast reference naming SP6b). MST:1949-1950 and :1963-1965 stay: the abyss hand-overs go into new "Received from SP6b" lines, never into the "Received from SP6a" lines. MST:2000 ("the block list grows in SP6b, SP8a, SP8b and SP9") stays.

## 16. Notes handed to later SPs

- **SP7:** lava may appear in the abyss and light it a little (the user: "y la propia lava que también emite luz"; no water there, guess, confirmed); the abyss box (§1.3) widened by 0.1 depth disables aquifers there (master :755); rule 4's under-water branches, clay under cave water in lush and gravel under cave water elsewhere (D21); under SP6b's rock floor master :751's "everything below y −54 is lava" rarely fires, so SP7 places the abyss's lava from aquifer cells or lowers the floor locally (`caves.floor.y` by a lava mask), and re-checks `C7.bedrockExposed`; the cave channel's nibble marks flooded caves' biome too; SP7's exit "P1: T still ≤ 10 ms" (master :1943) predates ruling R14's 13 ms p50 gate, which SP6b keeps (D27): SP7's spec reconciles the two.
- **SP8a:** painters for the seven flat-colour blocks; the abyss's dark albedos re-judged with textures under the light floor; the X-ray's biome palette stays flat.
- **SP8b:** the abyss's faintly glowing crystals or gems, exclusive to the abyss, and its light gothic touch (the user's words above); the abyss mineral (O2) placed by the nibble or `caveBiomeQ`; cave decoration (glow lichen, cave vines, pointed dripstone, moss carpets, roots) reads the scan's per-run `floorBiome` and `ceilBiome` and the nibble; `secondaryDepthRange` if its rules need a noisy depth (D18); emitters lift caves above the light floor.
- **SP8c:** the abyss silent (the user: "Oscuro y silencioso"; little sound, not unsettling music, guess, confirmed); AU3's abyss label from the nibble at the eye; the `SOUND` group `abyss` now assigned to five blocks (footsteps).
- **SP9:** structures may read `caveBiomeQ` to choose cave pieces; a structure's boxes shrink the floor's terms as they shrink carvers.
- **SP10:** the F3 line "cave biome at the eye" with the kind; the probe's cave biome and the picker's inputs; a "freeze culling" toggle and the culled count in F3; the map's cave tile layer (SP6a) gains the biome; the items of §17 if cut; `replyDuringFlushP95Ms`'s flake (D32).
- **SP11:** RegionMesh's buffer policy (Firefox geometry 181 MB, SP6a Task 52) and per-section culling on RegionMesh (index writes or multi-draw), if Firefox needs them (D23, D32); the X-ray's biome palette is a uniform of the GLSL material (the TSL seam).
- **SP12:** the X-ray refill's 6 s target (Point 3's recommendation) with its levers (a frame cap while regenerating with a scaled upload budget; a cached non-cave density); `C6.latticeBias`'s 1.1, read as ≤ 1.09 on SP6b's measure (§12.3, ruling U1: the SP6a tree reads 1.10-1.11 on it), with the structural levers (cell height 4, per-voxel noise for the thin families); the `deriveSeed` memo if SP6b did not take it; `cave_heavy`'s finalisation with C1's 10-22 % gate; the B3 border bounds and `C7.bottomFlatRatio` re-judged with the final tuning; the cave biomes in the extreme presets.

## 17. Cut line

May slip, in this order, each named in master §10's Default receivers and worded at the exit by whether it was taken:
1. **The Caves tab's "Find a cave biome" buttons, with its "Show cave biomes on the map" and "Cave biome parameters" buttons** (additions beyond the brief and the master, Point 1 (a)) → SP10. D14's review links replace them for the user's check.
2. **The map's cave-biome layer** → SP10, with the cave tile layer SP6a sent there. The slice field and the X-ray by biome still show biomes.
3. **The Cave biome slice field and the Voxels mode's biome colours** → SP10.
4. **RegionMesh's region culling** → SP11. Firefox then draws unculled, as today; RegionBatch's culling and R3 stay.

Each is cut whole. The plan builds all four unless the controller cuts one, or the user takes Point 1 (a), which removes item 1 and the Voxels mode's half of item 3 before the plan.

Not cuttable (the brief and master §10): the picker and the three biomes; rule 4 and the seven blocks; the abyss's rock; the bottom floor (the user's finding); the X-ray by biome with its key; `cave_heavy`; cave culling on RegionBatch with R3 and the raycast reference; B3; C1's `cave_heavy` part; the migration; the CI work item; C1-C9, L2, S1, DT1, DT2, P1; L13 and L14.

(Filled during and at the exit: the CI work item's runs (job tables, walls, runner minutes) per step; D25's generator checkpoint — B3 per window and band, the abyss's air with and without the floor, `terrain.real` and `terrain.islands` p50/p99, `caveBiome.column`, C1, C4's lowest band, C5's thinnest, the cave channel's shared memory, the levers applied; D25's culling checkpoint — `R3.culledUnderground`, the walk's cost, the lever if applied; the golden diff of the generator push against its base; `cave_heavy`'s cheese offset and its C1, C2, C9; the bench; L13 and L14 in Chrome and the user's Firefox run; the review links and screenshots; the production check; the user's check.)

### The CI work item (Tasks 2-6)

**Done at Stage 1 (Task 5; §13's rule): two consecutive green cold runs at 5:10 and 5:17, both ≤ 5 min 30 s, so Stage 2 does not join the work item (Task 6 skipped).** Every run is on `sp6b/cave-biomes`. L1-L4 are at 596796b (Task 2). L5 is at 7b00c74 (Task 3), pushed with L6 at 27d50c9 (Task 4), whose run 43277407950 is cold runs 1 and 2. A cold run is one whose `regions-*` caches miss, as each caves job's log names (`Cache not found for input keys: regions-Linux-metrics-quick-caves-09fc3bde…`). Each attempt's wall runs from its start (creation for #1) to its last job's end. Runner minutes are the sum of the job times.

- **27d50c9, run 43277407950 #2** (warm re-run, every saved `regions-*` cache hit): 18:24:42 → 18:30:40Z, wall 5:58, green. Long pole `unit (rest)` 5:39.

Runner minutes are 50.2-50.6 against the expected 54-59. Above "the unchanged jobs up to about 4:35":
  - `metrics-quick (rest)` (4:52 and 4:58 cold) is now the cold long pole.
  - `unit (rest)` reaches 5:39 and 5:44 on the two warm re-runs, and the unit jobs restore no region cache. #3 and #4 ran the same 289 files and 5,626 tests, with vitest's duration at 199 s in #3 and 328 s in #4. Each of the nine files over 10 s in both ran 1.5-1.9 × slower in #4, so the runner was slower (±45 %, §13). `cavesMetric.test.ts` (Task 4's worker test, which generates the two fast regions itself) took 61 s in #3 and 105 s in #4. This bears on the 6:00 exit bound (Task 58), not on this rule.
- **Cache misses.** Runs #1 and #3 missed every `regions-*` key (the caves, caves-presets, render and rest jobs of both tiers saved theirs). Runs #2 and #4 hit those six. `edit`, `persist` and `clipboard` miss on every run because they save no cache, as before. The terrain and surface jobs no longer run a region-cache step (L1-L4's cleanup).
- **The account.** `gh api user --jq .plan` reads null: the plan needs the `user` scope, and the CLI token holds `gist`, `read:org` and `repo`. The repository is public. In all six attempts the 18 test jobs (`check`, 2 unit, 4 metrics-fast, 11 metrics-quick) started within 1 s of each other, so the concurrent limit is at least 18. 18 ≤ 20, the Free plan's limit, which is the lowest one.

### D25's generator checkpoint (Task 24)

Measured at the generator push's local head after Task 23 (206c1f4 plus Task 24's bench row; `GENERATOR_VERSION` 9, the picker, rule 4 with the fast path, the rock floor, C7's bottom parts and B3's shares in). Node: `BENCH_EXEMPT=terrain.real,terrain.islands,surface.column,caveBiome.column npm run bench` twice (1-minute load 0.57 and 0.76 before them); `npx vitest run --project metrics-quick test/metrics/caves.metric.ts` (every group); `METRICS_PROFILES=default npx vitest run --project metrics-quick test/metrics/terrain.metric.ts`; two scratch scripts (not committed) for the abyss's air without the floor and the abyss spacing. Both bench runs left the turbo window before `terrain.real` (the calibration read 0.636 ns at the start and 0.73-0.79 ns from `terrain.real` on, and `measureBracketed` ran each T row 3 times), so the absolute rows below are at the throttled clock. "At turbo" is the row's ratio × 0.637 ns, the baseline's own calibration. Both runs stopped at the absolute gates (`terrain.real` p99, `terrain.islands` p50, `slice.caves128` p50), so `npm run bench:record` refuses to write. `test/baselines.json` gains only the `caveBiome.column` row (run 2's kernel) and is re-recorded in full after the levers (Tasks 25, 31). The Chrome step did not run because the GPU conditions did not hold: eDP-1 had no current mode, HDMI-1-0 was primary at 60 Hz, `LockedHint=yes` and the 1-minute load was 2.37. It ran later, once the user restored them (ruling R18): after Task 25's levers, at a99f7fe, `npm run measure:chrome -- --route bench --gpu iris --probe-only`, then `… --route bench --gpu iris --workers 4` twice (1-minute load 1.06 and 1.18 before them; PRIME on-demand, eDP-1 primary at 240 Hz, HDMI-1-0 also connected at 143.6 Hz, `LockedHint=no`, the package at 53-54 C). The run's files are not committed, and `assets/sp6a` keeps SP6a's.

### B3's border parts on `caves` and `caves-presets` (Task 26)

Recorded, not gated (Task 30 gates them; the `caves-biomes` job and its pair minimum arrive with Task 27). Measured at the generator push's local head after Task 25 (`GENERATOR_VERSION` 9, the picker, rule 4 and the rock floor in), per job over its regions: `METRICS_CAVE_GROUPS=default` and `=presets npx vitest run --project metrics-quick test/metrics/caves.metric.ts`, each writing `.out/B3.borders.<group>.json`; fast through `npm test` (`metrics-fast`, both groups in one process: the default group only, fast has no preset region). As built (`test/harness/caveMetrics.ts`' header): cave air as §12.1's (tagged, neither solid nor fluid); a border face lies between two face-adjacent cave-air voxels of different biomes, on the plane of its + side voxel; `borderLattice` reads each residue's border faces over its cave-air face pairs (the faces a border may lie on), on all three axes, the axis farthest from 1; each pair's parts are read over that pair's own faces; the skin mix counts the wall faces of the cave air within 2 blocks (Chebyshev) of a border voxel, by the solid neighbour's block class.

### The `caves-biomes` job (Task 27)

The biomes group (`METRICS_CAVE_GROUPS=biomes`, the 19th CI job `caves-biomes`; with no env, every group in one process, so `npm test`'s metrics-fast run measures it too, rulings P2, P28): on quick `cave_heavy`'s two 32 × 32 regions at seed '42', (−16, −16) and (16, −16) (the default group's origins; seeds '42', '1', '2', '3' on full; none on fast), then the biome region on every tier. The group registers none of the C1-C9 calls of the default and preset groups (§10, §12.3) and no `carverReference` (its regions skip `referenceCarve`); it registers `C1.caveHeavy` (`caveHeavyMin`, `caveHeavyMax`: both `cave_heavy`'s void share, `activeFrom: 'SP12'`), B3's border parts per job (Task 26's test, now with each biome | plain pair's 2,000-face minimum in this job: fewer fails as an insufficient sample), the biome region's T re-check and the review poses.

**The biome region** (`BIOME_REGION` in `test/harness/caveSites.ts`, chunk origin (−112, 80), 32 × 32 chunks, seed '42', default profile; ruling P33's shape). The scan runs over the preset scan's candidates R(a, b) in its ring order (chunk origin (8a − 16, 8b − 16)) and takes the first that passes the climate check and then the T check. Climate (as §1.6's, no T): `samplePoint` on a 64-block grid, 64 points, the master's raw boxes (lush H ≥ 0.4; dripstone C ≥ 0.6; abyss E ≤ −0.5, C > −0.1 and surfaceEst − 128 > −47, §1.6's room above the rock floor), **each box holding at least 8 of the 64 points** (as built: with one point per box the first candidate holding the T window is R(−11, 11), climate lush 1, dripstone 52, abyss 37, whose generated cave air holds only 30 lush | plain border faces, short of §12.2's 2,000 on the fast tier, where the biome region is the biomes group alone). T (ruling P33): some 8 × 8-chunk tile, in `sp6a.region.T.caves`' order, holds stored nibbles 1, 2 and 3. Run at the generator push's local head after Task 26 (`GENERATOR_VERSION` 9, Task 25's levers in): R(−12, 12), climate lush 11, dripstone 47, abyss 39, its tile (1, 2) (chunks (−104, 96) … (−97, 103)) holding the three; skipped by the T check before it: R(−7, 10) and R(−7, 11) (`BIOME_SCAN_SKIPPED`; 30 tiles generated in all). `caveSites` re-checks the climate predicate at every run, the metric re-checks the T window over the measured region (`.out/caves.biomeRegion.json`), and `cavesMetric.test.ts` re-runs the scan (about 37 s alone on the laptop: 30 tiles up to T, no region cache in the unit job). A box retune (Task 30) re-runs it.

### The Chrome cells (Task 50; measured with HDMI-1-0 connected, then disconnected, then after a reboot, then SP6a's 702559b once, then both on kernel 7.0.0-42, then after an EC reset; gated from the EC-reset set)

Measured three times on the same code, 13da5ec (unchanged through 90dca8c and this section's later commits: the commits since 13da5ec are this section's text). The first set ran with HDMI-1-0 connected. The second set ran with it disconnected (ruling U7, the user's answer to the first STOP). The third set ran after the user rebooted the machine, HDMI-1-0 still disconnected (ruling U8, the answer to the second STOP), and `assets/sp6b/` holds the third set. Ruling R20's investigation compares the three sets with SP6a's committed files and with SP6a's own code measured under the same conditions. Two later checks measured the bench route only: 702559b once on kernel 7.0.0-43 (U9), then 702559b and SP6b once each on kernel 7.0.0-42 (U10). The cause was then found in the laptop's embedded controller (EC), a package-wide CPU clock cap without heat; after the user's EC reset (U11) 702559b reproduced its committed figures and every Task 50 route was re-run on SP6b's code: `assets/sp6b/` holds that sixth set, and the gates are switched on it (the last block of this section).

**The first set: HDMI-1-0 connected.** Measured at 13da5ec (Task 49's bench: L13 with culling and a culling-off segment, L14's biome segment), 2026-11-15/16, each run by `npm run measure:chrome` (its own build, `vite preview` on a free port and headful Chrome on a fresh `mktemp` profile, cold driver shader cache, Chrome 155.0.8059.39, ANGLE Mesa Iris Xe ADL GT2, RegionBatch, RD 12, the window fullscreen at (0, 0) 1920 × 1080 @ 1). Checked read-only right before each run: PRIME on-demand; eDP-1 primary at 1920 × 1080, 240.00 Hz current; **HDMI-1-0 also connected, 1920 × 1080 at 143.60 Hz, right of eDP-1** (reverse PRIME through the NVIDIA GPU); session 2 (seat0, x11) `LockedHint=no`; the hottest thermal zone 50-56 °C; 1-minute load 0.52-0.97 (a first wait of 15 min for the user's Firefox at 115-145 % CPU to stop; one start refused at 66 °C and retried). Every run read `displayIntervalMs` 4.165 and frame p50 4.165 ms: Chrome's window paced at eDP-1's 240 Hz, never at HDMI's 143.6 Hz (6.9 ms). These files were the `assets/sp6b/` candidates (run 2 as `bench-iris-chrome.json`) until the second set replaced them; they are kept git-ignored in `test/metrics/.out/sp6b-task50/` (run 2, w4, flight and enter byte-identical to what was committed-ready) with run 1, the A/B files and every run's log. `assets/sp6a/` is untouched. Two of the four misses are each one frame, in run 2 only (its `longFrames`): **`caveOver33`'s 1 is a single 2,007.5 ms frame** in L13 (frame 79516, raw 2,008.3 ms, main 2.52 ms, the frame before 1.84 ms: the main thread idle, so a 2-second presentation stall in the GPU process, the compositor or the OS, not a slow frame of the page; `caveMaxMs` 2,007.53), and **`mediumLiveOver33`'s 1 a single 179.1 ms frame** in L8 (the undo): L8's second frame, the one right after the undo's Apply frame (frame 21042, main 3.32 ms; the Apply frame before it took 26.8 ms of main-thread work, `prevMainMs`). No other first-set run has a frame over 33 ms in L8 or L13: SP6b run 1 and w4, both A/B runs on SP6a's code (L13 max 16.66 each) and SP6a's committed files (12.49). (The second set's run on SP6a's code, C, missed at the same L8 frame, 37.5 ms: see the Apply-frame paragraph in the third set.) Task 51's levers do not apply: 51a fires on `cullMainMaxMs` or `caveMainP95` and 51b on the biome segment, which all hold.

**The investigation (ruling R20).** (1) The SP6b GPU path: with culling on, L13's GPU time is lower than in the culling-off segment on every run (p50 2.04-2.79 against 2.75-3.03 ms, fewer triangles), and the frame p95 equal or lower; with the X-ray on, SP6b's main p95 (4.71-4.83) and GPU p95 (4.20-6.16) are below SP6a's code measured the same night (7.58-7.84; 7.39-7.49): D24's terrain passes drawing nothing help, as designed. (2) SP6a's own code (main ae33227, extracted with `git archive` into the scratchpad, its own build), measured twice the same night under the same checked conditions (bench only, no `--workers 4` run), reads the same frame figures as SP6b (stream p95 8.33, live p95 12.50, paste p95 8.33, refill 17-20 s against its committed 8.3 s) and misses its own gates (stream `over33` 1, `editVisibleP95` 54.2, `flushEditVisibleP95` 52.9, `xrayOver33` 3-4, `xrayMainP95` 7.58-7.84), mostly in cells SP6b's run 2 holds (stream `over33`, `editVisibleP95`, `xrayMainP95`). Of SP6b's four gated misses, cell by cell: `xrayOver33` is reproduced (SP6a's code 4 and 3; SP6b 3, 4 and 1 in run 1, run 2 and w4); `caveOver33` and `mediumLiveOver33` are not reproduced (SP6a's code 0 and 0 on each), and are the isolated single-frame stalls named above; `mediumFill4wMs` was not measured on SP6a's code (no w4 A/B run), only the single-worker reveal, which is comparable (SP6b 4,578-5,649 ms, SP6a's code 4,719-5,027), and the other session's build overlapped the end of SP6b's w4 run. So the general shift since SP6a's committed runs (one interval more at p95 on the heavy legs, the refill about twice as long) is in the machine's state, not in SP6b's code; that shift explains `xrayOver33`, but the A/B neither explains the two single-frame misses nor tests `mediumFill4wMs`. (3) The second display: Chrome's frames pace at 240 Hz on eDP-1 (interval and p50 4.165); the terrain-only flight reads SP6a's figures (p95 4.165, GPU p95 2.97 against 2.96), so HDMI-1-0 alone does not double light frames; the heavy legs (workers generating, the X-ray) read one interval more at p95 than in SP6a's committed runs, on SP6a's code as well, and the stream's GPU p95 rises (2.61-3.06 on SP6b, 3.39-3.41 on SP6a's code, against 2.20 committed). The SP6a records do not say whether HDMI-1-0 was connected then. A reverse-PRIME output at 143.6 Hz was taken to add a copy of the desktop per HDMI frame on the Iris Xe: a hypothesis, not measured here (the second set measures the GPU time with HDMI-1-0 disconnected). (4) Load: the bench's own workers raise the load to 3.45-4.15 (SP6a's 2.72; the reveal takes 4.6-5.6 s against 3.75 and the X-ray refill about twice as long, on SP6a's code too; the enter medians nearly double as well, unsaved 8,208, saved 8,563 and a new world 7,233 ms against SP6a's 4,538, 4,215 and 3,875, on SP6b's code only: the enter route was not run on SP6a's code), and other sessions' processes ran beside the A/B runs and the end of the w4 and enter runs (a node build at 100-250 % CPU, traced every 30 s; `test/metrics/.out/sp6b-task50/*.loadtrace`). Turbo on (`no_turbo` 0), the performance platform profile, on mains, the iGPU's max 1,400 MHz. Undecided between (3) and (4) and the machine's 2-day uptime with 2.2 GB in swap; only the user can disconnect HDMI-1-0, so this was the first STOP: the misses went to the user with these figures (no gate loosened, nothing committed but this evidence). The user disconnected HDMI-1-0 and asked for Step 1 again on the same code (U7).
