The gate read on the second set (`npx vitest run --project metrics-quick test/metrics/g1.metric.ts` with Task 50's `g1.metric.ts`): 6 of 9 calls pass; **missed: `xrayOver33` 2, `replyDuringFlushP95Ms` 6.93 > 5, `ghostSliceMaxMs` 4.22 > 4**. Run 1, read the same way (copied in for the read, then run 2 restored): 4 of 9 pass; missed `mediumStreamOver33` 1, an L7 structural frame at 33.32 ms (G1.live), `replyDuringFlushP95Ms` 5.04, `ghostSliceMaxMs` 4.54, `xrayOver33` 4, and **`mediumLiveOver33` 2**, which the read does not list: G1.live's structural-stutter assertion comes first in the same call (`g1.metric.ts`, "L7-L9 structural frames whose stutterMs is over 33 ms") and its failure hides the over33 check. Three of run 1's long frames are presentation stalls of the first set's kind (its `longFrames`; the main thread idle in each): **a 154.1 ms frame in L1** (the stream; frame 4954, main 1.75 ms, the frame before 1.83), which is `mediumStreamOver33`'s 1; **a 158.3 ms frame in L8** (the undo, mid-leg: frame 24080, 1,193 frames after the undo's Apply frame; main 3.63 ms, before 2.51), which with the L7 33.32 ms frame makes `mediumLiveOver33`'s 2; and **a 162.4 ms frame in L14** inside the gated RD 12 segment (frame 114991, main 3.04 ms, before 3.23; the segment's max 162.43), one of `xrayOver33`'s 4. Run 1's load trace shows no other session's process beside it (its samples: the bench's Chrome, Xorg, gnome-shell, this workflow's own terminal, one `nv_queue` sample at 42 %). The first set's other misses do not recur on run 2: `mediumFill4wMs` 5,538 holds, `caveOver33` is 0, and run 2's largest long frame is 83.3 ms (L14, main 2.54 ms). The 2 s L13 freeze repeats in neither run; the 179 ms frame after the undo's Apply does not repeat in run 2, but run 1 has the 158 ms L8 frame, the 154 ms L1 frame and the 162 ms L14 frame above, each with main ≤ 3.6 ms. So stalls of 150 ms or more on an idle main thread recur with HDMI-1-0 disconnected: they are not an HDMI artefact. Across both sets they appear in 2 of SP6b's 4 single-worker bench runs (first set run 2, second set run 1), in neither w4 run (largest 62.5 ms), and in none of the 3 runs on SP6a's code (largest 75.0 ms) or SP6a's committed files (83.3 ms): too few runs to tell the code from chance. A bound on `mediumStreamOver33`, `mediumLiveOver33` or `xrayOver33` at run 1's counts would admit these frames. `replyDuringFlushP95Ms` is not SP6a's flake under §12.6's rule: every second-set run reads over 5 ms (5.04, 6.93, w4 5.41; SP6a's code 5.57). Every new SP6b part holds on every run: `cullMainMeanMs` 0.037-0.039, `cullMainMaxMs` 0.685-0.755 (§8.5's 1.5 kept), `xrayBiomeP95` 4.165, `xrayBiomeOver33` 0, `xrayColorToggleMaxMs` 4.165. Task 51's levers still do not apply.

**The investigation, continued (R20).** (1) HDMI-1-0's cost is real and on the GPU. With it disconnected, GPU p95 falls on every leg. The flight goes from 2.97 to 2.07 ms and the stream from 2.61-3.06 to 1.97-2.03 (SP6a's committed 2.20). L13 with culling goes from 3.65-4.31 to 2.46-2.53, and the X-ray from 4.20-6.16 to 3.52-3.56. The frame p95 is back at one interval (4.165) on paste, on L13 and its culling-off segment, on the biome segment and on the w4 stream, and the X-ray's p95 falls from 12.50 to 8.33. So the reverse-PRIME output loaded the Iris Xe (how, the desktop copy above, stays unmeasured). It accounts for the first set's 8.33 ms p95 on paste and L13 and its higher GPU figures. (2) HDMI-1-0 was not the cause of the gate misses. Every second-set miss is a long-frame or main-thread cell: `xrayOver33`, most of whose L14 long frames have main 2.2-10.0 ms after a short frame (`prevMainMs` 1.5-7.5), so they are not slow frames of the page, while a few (at most two per run) follow a 31-40 ms main frame (`prevMainMs`), a main-thread frame presented one interval late; `ghostSliceMaxMs`, a main-thread slice; `replyDuringFlushP95Ms`, a worker reply; and run 1's stream and live frames (the 154 and 158 ms idle-main stalls above). SP6a's own code, measured once under the same HDMI-off conditions (C), misses the same cells: `xrayOver33` 3, `ghostSliceMaxMs` 4.49, `replyDuringFlushP95Ms` 5.57, paste L12c `over33` 1 and live `over33` 1 (a 37.5 ms frame right after L8's undo Apply frame, whose main-thread work took 35.5 ms: the frame where the first set's 179 ms frame sits), and also `xrayMainP95` 6.83 > 6. (3) What is left on the bench route is the machine's CPU-side state, the same on both codes and with or without HDMI-1-0. The bench's own workers raise the load to 3.6-4.6 (SP6a's committed runs 2.72). The reveal takes 4.55-5.54 s against 3.75 (4.55 s on SP6a's code). The X-ray refill takes 14.3-17.4 s against 8.3 (15.6 s on SP6a's code). `editVisibleP95` reads 40-47 ms against 29.9 (47.31 on SP6a's code). The enter route is outside that comparison: it was never run on SP6a's code (both A/B sets ran the bench route only), and its per-pair shape does not fit a steady machine state. In the second set the first pair, the cold one the route discards, reveals in 4,995 / 4,827 ms (unsaved / saved) and every later pair in 8,189-8,541 ms; the first set reads the same, 5,116 / 5,784 then 8,125-9,178. SP6a's committed file reads 3,799 / 3,775, then 3,729-3,773 for two pairs, then 4,215-4,673 for the last three (a step of about 1.2 times). On SP6b every reload in the same Chrome costs about 1.7 times the cold first load, while the bench's first reveal on the same machine is 4.89 s. A steady CPU-side slowdown would slow the cold first load as well; a cost that builds up across reloads (workers, SharedArrayBuffers, IndexedDB or swap pressure) fits the shape, and whether it belongs to SP6b's code or to the machine is untested. G1.enter gates only the ratio (1.000, held), so nothing fails on it; but if the growth is SP6b's, a fresh boot will not remove it. No other session's process ran beside the second set. The variable not yet tried is the machine's own state after 2 days 13 h up with 2.2 GB in swap (a fresh boot). So this is the second STOP: the misses go to the user with both sets (no gate loosened; the gate switch, the lock and the Threshold-log line stay uncommitted).

**The third set: after a reboot, HDMI-1-0 disconnected (2026-11-16, U8).** Same code, same tool and route list: `--probe-only` (exit 0, and it built), bench twice, `--workers 4`, flight, enter. Then SP6a's code (main ae33227 by `git archive` into the scratchpad, its own build and its own `measureChrome`) ran the bench once (D) and, for the first time, the enter route once (D′). The user rebooted at 09:35 and started nothing heavy after it. Checked read-only right before each run (each log opens with the output):
- PRIME on-demand.
- eDP-1 primary at 1920 × 1080, 240.00 Hz current. HDMI-1-0 disconnected, and no other output connected.
- Session 2 (seat0, x11) `LockedHint=no`.
- The hottest thermal zone 53-56 °C.
- 1-minute load 0.12-0.94 at every start (the runner waited for ≤ 1.0).
- Free memory ≥ 12.5 GB; **swap 0 MB before and after every run**.
- Uptime at each start: probe and run 1 12 min, run 2 23 min, w4 34 min, flight 45 min, enter 46 min, D 51 min, D′ 61 min. The second set ran at 2 days 13 h with 2.2 GB in swap.

Each `measure:chrome` ran in a capped systemd scope (`MemoryMax=6G`). Every run read `displayIntervalMs` 4.165, the window fullscreen at (0, 0) 1920 × 1080 @ 1. Kernel 7.0.0-43-generic, as in the second set. Chrome 155.0.8059.39, the same in all three sets and in SP6a's committed files. A load trace every 30 s ran beside every run; each sample keeps only the top 4 processes by CPU at that instant (`top -b -n1`), so a process that ran between samples, or below the top 4, is not in it. It shows:
- the bench's own Chrome and preview, Xorg and gnome-shell;
- this workflow's own session (its terminal, warp, and its `claude` process);
- one `nv_queue` sample in each bench run (50-100 %);
- three `gh` processes at 108-125 % each in one sample at 09:48:44, a minute into run 1 (its stream legs), and one `gh` at 33 % in run 2. This task did not start them, and their owner is unknown.

Recorded on the third set's SP6b runs (1 / 2 / w4):
- L13: culled sections p50 71, max 172; triangles p50 302,969 / 302,773 / 302,773 (culling off 306,771, max 584,161 against 460,459 on); walks per second 0.45 / 0.43 / 0.47.
- The culling-off segment: frame p95 4.165, `over33` 0, main p95 2.79 / 2.76 / 2.67, `cullMainMeanMs` 0.020-0.022 (the filter only).
- The X-ray: `xrayCulledSections` 0 on every run; `xrayVertexBytes` by type 19,616,952 / 19,678,544 / 19,638,424 and by biome 18,437,280 / 18,437,280 / 18,561,760; the biome segment's GPU p95 3.61 / 3.65 / 3.59; `xrayMapOpenMaxMs` 70.8 / 79.1 / 70.8; RD 16 p95 8.33, `over33` 0, `gpuMB` 204.5 / 204.4 / 202.7.
- The flight (load 0.90 → 1.42): frame p95 4.165 ms, p99 4.165, max 8.33, `over33` 0, draw calls max 11, main p95 2.34, GPU p95 2.04 (second set 2.07; first set 2.97).
- The enter route (load 0.94 → 6.35): `enterRatio` 1.015 (pairs 1.322, 1.029, 1.015, 0.974, 1.004, 1.022); medians unsaved 8,224 and saved 8,341 ms; a new world 7,258 ms. The discarded cold first pair reveals in 4,865 / 6,431 ms, then every pair in 8,170-8,480. Its 13 console lines are the `beforeunload` lines.
- SP6a's code on the enter route (D′, load 0.94 → 8.17): `enterRatio` 1.010; medians 7,686 / 7,843 ms; a new world 7,115 ms. Its pairs reveal in 4,607 / 4,556 and 4,953 / 4,662, then 7,681-8,098.

The gate read on the third set (`npx vitest run --project metrics-quick test/metrics/g1.metric.ts` with Task 50's `g1.metric.ts`): 6 of 9 calls pass. **Missed:**
- **`mediumLiveOver33` 1.** One 33.32 ms frame, L8's second (frame 23,087), the one right after the undo's Apply frame: main 4.8 ms after the Apply frame's 28.9 ms of main-thread work (`prevMainMs`), so the undo's synchronous work presented the next frame one interval late, 0.32 ms over the line. SP6a's code missed at the same frame in the second set (C: 37.5 ms after a 35.5 ms Apply frame); see the Apply-frame paragraph below.
- **`replyDuringFlushP95Ms` 5.215 > 5.**
- **`ghostSliceMaxMs` 4.445 > 4** (L12b).

`xrayOver33` holds (0) for the first time on a gated run.

Run 1 was read the same way (copied in for the read, then run 2 restored and checked with `cmp`): 5 of 9 pass. It missed:
- `mediumStreamOver33` 1;
- `mediumLiveOver33` 2;
- `replyDuringFlushP95Ms` 5.39;
- `xrayOver33` 3;
- `xrayBiomeOver33` 1, a 33.32 ms frame in the biome segment (the segment's max 33.32). This is the first miss of a new SP6b part on any run, though not on the gated file. The bench file does not mark the segment windows inside L14, so the frame is placed by position only: the biome segment comes right after the X-ray's first hold (`bench.ts`, L14's phases), and the one L14 long frame of that length early in the leg is frame 109,097 (L14's frame 3,553 of 16,572; main 1.45 ms after a 1.82 ms frame), an idle-main presentation stall, not a slow frame of the page. The only other 33.32 ms L14 frame, 118,181, sits 9,000 frames later and follows a 33.8 ms main frame.

Run 1's two long frames differ in kind from the earlier sets' stalls. A 154.1 ms frame in L3 (the stream) has `prevMainMs` 154.9, and a 166.6 ms frame in L7, L7's second, the one right after the Apply frame, has `prevMainMs` 166.8: the Apply's own frame carried a 167 ms main-thread task. In each, the frame before was a main-thread task of that length: not an idle-main presentation stall, and not a structural frame (no L7-L9 structural frame is over 33 ms). Run 1's other live miss is the gated run's frame: L8's second, 33.32 ms after the undo's 28.6 ms Apply frame. The `gh` sample at 09:48:44 falls in run 1's stream legs. The file keeps no wall-clock time per frame; by frame count (the review's estimate) the 154 ms L3 frame falls about 5-10 s before that sample, and the 30 s samples keep only the top 4 processes, so the `gh` burst may have overlapped it: the trace can neither tie it to the frame nor rule it out. The 167 ms L7 frame is the page's own Apply work, whatever ran beside it.

Frames of 150 ms or more, of either kind (idle-main stalls or main-thread tasks), across the three sets: 3 of SP6b's 6 single-worker bench runs (first set run 2, second set run 1, third set run 1), none of its 3 w4 runs, and none of the 4 runs on SP6a's code (A, B, C, D; their largest long frames 75.0, 66.6, 70.8 and 91.6 ms) or SP6a's committed files (83.3 ms). If the three affected runs were spread at random over these 10 single-worker runs, all three would land on SP6b's 6 with probability C(6,3) / C(10,3) = 20 / 120 ≈ 0.17: chance is not excluded. (The 702559b run below, a fifth SP6a-code run, has none either, largest 83.3 ms: 20 / 165 ≈ 0.12.)

**The Apply and undo frames.** L7 starts the page's Apply (`edit.path`'s urgent change, `live.set`) and L8 its undo (`live.undo`) on each leg's first frame (`bench.ts`, the live legs), so that frame carries the change's synchronous main-thread work and the leg's second frame is presented after it. Most live misses across the three sets sit on that second frame:
- L8's, in 5 of the 13 bench runs: first set run 2, 179.1 ms after a 26.8 ms undo frame; second set w4, 33.32 after 31.3 (recorded); SP6a's code C, 37.5 after 35.5; third set run 1, 33.32 after 28.6; third set run 2 (the gated miss), 33.32 after 28.9;
- L7's, in third set run 1: 166.6 ms after the 166.8 ms Apply frame, once in SP6b's 6 single-worker runs and in none of the 4 runs on SP6a's code (their L7 main max 17.4-28.0).

The L7 / L8 main max reads 15.8-29.5 / 19.4-35.5 ms on the three sets' 13 bench runs on both codes (2026-11-15 23:33 to 2026-11-16 10:24; run 1's 166.8 aside), against 12.7 / 16.3 in SP6a's committed file (w4 14.1 / 17.1): the same CPU-side shift as the doubled refills, and it puts the undo frame at about 29 ms against the 33 ms line. So a `mediumLiveOver33` bound of 1 would be used up by the undo frame alone under this shift, and it would also admit a 167 ms Apply frame like run 1's, which is the page's own Apply work, not an environmental stall.

`replyDuringFlushP95Ms` is not SP6a's flake under §12.6's rule. Every run since HDMI-1-0 was disconnected reads over 5 ms, on both codes: second set 5.04, 6.93, w4 5.41 and SP6a's code 5.57; third set 5.39, 5.22, w4 5.33 and SP6a's code 5.07. `ghostSliceMaxMs` reads 3.75, 4.45 and w4 4.27 on SP6b and 4.67 on SP6a's code (the second set 4.54, 4.22, 4.33, 4.49; SP6a's committed 2.09).

The new SP6b parts on the gated run: `cullMainMeanMs` 0.037-0.038 and `cullMainMaxMs` 0.650-0.770 on every run (§8.5's 1.5 kept); `xrayBiomeP95` 4.165, `xrayColorToggleMaxMs` 4.165 and `xrayBiomeOver33` 0 on run 2 and w4 (1 on run 1). Task 51's levers do not fire on the gated file.

**The investigation, third part (R20).**
1. **The reboot did not move the CPU-side figures.** SP6b's runs, before the reboot (second set) → after it:
   - the X-ray refill 14.3-17.4 → 14.8-17.3 s;
   - the reveal 4.89-5.54 → 4.56-5.75 s;
   - the teleport refill 13.1-13.4 → 13.2-13.4 s (the w4 runs' L9 timed out in both sets);
   - the Apply's full refill 10.4-13.6 → 10.4-12.9 s;
   - `editVisibleP95` 40.5-41.9 → 43.6-46.1 ms;
   - the enter medians 8.35 / 8.39 → 8.22 / 8.34 s.

   The GPU figures are the second set's: flight 2.07 → 2.04, stream 1.97-2.03 → 1.90-2.02, X-ray 3.52-3.56 → 3.51-3.54. So the long uptime and the swap were not the cause. With HDMI-1-0's GPU cost removed in the second set, the remaining shift from SP6a's committed files survives a fresh boot.
2. **SP6a's code (ae33227, D) after the reboot misses the same two G2 cells as SP6b's gated run:** `replyDuringFlushP95Ms` 5.07 and `ghostSliceMaxMs` 4.67. It also misses `xrayOver33` 1 and `xrayMainP95` 6.68 > 6 (X-ray p95 12.50), and holds `mediumLiveOver33` (max 29.15 ms). Its refill (15.5 s), teleport refill (12.1 s) and Apply full (10.1 s) are SP6b's, about twice SP6a's committed 8.3, 6.2 and 5.0 s; its reveal (4.5 s) is 1.2 times the committed 3.75.
3. **The enter route on SP6a's code (D′), its first A/B, has SP6b's shape.** Two pairs reveal in 4.6-5.0 s, then every later pair in 7.7-8.1 s. Its medians are 7,686 / 7,843 ms against SP6b's 8,224 / 8,341 (SP6b 7 % above, from one run each) and SP6a's committed 3.8 then 4.6 s. So the per-reload growth the second set flagged is on SP6a's code too, at nearly the same level: it is not specific to SP6b. Both ratios hold (1.010 and 1.015). The step's onset differs, from one run each: on SP6b it begins at the second load (the first pair's saved load, 6,431 ms after the cold 4,865, pair ratio 1.322) and every load from the third reads 8.2-8.5 s; on SP6a's code the first four loads (two pairs) stay at 4.6-5.0 s and the step comes at the fifth. So SP6b's step starts about two loads earlier; whether that is code or chance one run cannot say.
4. **What is left untested.** Two differences between the Task 50 runs and SP6a's committed Chrome files have not been separated by any set:
   - **(a) The kernel.** SP6a's Chrome cells were recorded on 2026-11-10 (commit 702559b, 00:14), in a boot on 7.0.0-42-generic. 7.0.0-43-generic was installed on 2026-11-13 at 06:49 (`/var/log/dpkg.log`), and every Task 50 run up to here ran on it (the boots that started on 2026-11-13 at 17:26, the first and second sets, and on 2026-11-16 at 09:35, the third set; the committed run's boot, 2026-11-09 18:50 to 2026-11-10 10:11, and the boots up to the one started 2026-11-12 16:29 ran 7.0.0-42).
   - **(b) The code.** ae33227, "SP6a's code" in every A/B, is not the code of SP6a's committed files. Those were recorded at 702559b, and 702559b..ae33227 changes `src/` in 20 commits. Among them are the noodle retune eab335e (a generator change: a third as many noodles, twice as thick; R31, R32) and cd25cd2 (apply's heap keys). The X-ray geometry measured on ae33227 (D) is smaller than in the committed file (20.2 against 21.9 MB of vertex bytes), yet its regeneration takes twice as long. So (b) does not obviously explain the shift, but it is untested.

   What has been tested: HDMI-1-0 (a GPU cost, removed in the second set), other sessions' load (none in the second and third sets beyond the `gh` sample), and the long uptime and swap (removed by the reboot, with no change). So this is the third STOP. The misses go to the user with all three sets (no gate loosened; the gate switch, the lock and the Threshold-log line stay uncommitted). The user chose to measure the bench route once on 702559b (ruling U9), below.

**The fourth check: SP6a's 702559b on today's kernel (2026-11-16, U9).** One bench run on 702559b, the commit that recorded SP6a's committed Chrome files, to separate (b) from (a). (SP6a's evidence says the committed Iris bench was measured at 8d0ae34, 702559b's parent; `git diff 8d0ae34 702559b -- src/` is empty, so the 702559b run is the measured code.) SP6b's routes were not re-run. 702559b was extracted with `git archive` into a fresh `mktemp -d` directory in the scratchpad, its dependencies installed with `npm ci` from its own lockfile (inside a capped systemd scope; `package.json` and `package-lock.json` are identical at 702559b and ae33227, and so is `test/tools/measureChrome.ts`), and its own `measureChrome` ran `--route bench --gpu iris` with its own build, `vite preview` on a free ephemeral port and a fresh `mktemp` profile, writing only into the scratch tree. The gate read with 702559b's own `g1.metric.ts` in the scratch tree (its `assets/sp6a/bench-iris-chrome.json` replaced by this run, the other files its committed ones; `npx vitest run --project metrics-quick test/metrics/g1.metric.ts`): 5 of 9 calls pass. **Missed: `editVisibleP95` 59.85 > 50, `replyDuringFlushP95Ms` 7.195 > 5, `pasteOver33` 1 and `ghostSliceMaxMs` 4.835 > 4, `xrayOver33` 3 and `xrayMainP95` 7.68 > 6.** It holds `mediumLiveOver33` (L8's frame max 24.99 ms, its main max 26.4) and stream `over33`.

**What 702559b shows.** The code that recorded SP6a's committed files, run once on today's machine and kernel, does not reproduce them. It reads the shift every Task 50 set reads, on every figure of it: the X-ray refill 14.9 s against its own committed 8.3 (SP6b 14.8-15.0, ae33227 15.5), the teleport refill 13.3 s against 6.2, the Apply's full refill 10.7 s against 5.0, the reveal 4.7 s against 3.75, the L7 / L8 main max 21.5 / 26.4 ms against 12.7 / 16.3, and one or two intervals more at p95 on the stream, live and X-ray legs. It misses both G2 cells SP6b's gated run misses, by more (7.20 against 5.22; 4.83 against 4.45), and four cells SP6b's gated run holds (`editVisibleP95`, `pasteOver33`, `xrayOver33`, `xrayMainP95`). It holds `mediumLiveOver33` (live max 24.99 ms), as ae33227 did in D, where SP6b's run 2 missed by one 33.32 ms frame; with one run each, that cell turns on whether the undo frame's main-thread work (L8 main max 19.6-28.9 ms in these four runs) pushes the next frame past the 33 ms line, as the Apply-frame paragraph says. Its load rises to 6.10 against its committed 2.72, the highest end load of any Task 50 bench run (SP6b's 3.53-4.56, D's 4.42), and its "by more" G2 misses and its four extra misses come from that single run: they do not show 702559b to be worse than SP6b's code (the conclusion below rests on the refills, which read about twice the committed figures on every run at end loads from 3.53 to 6.10). So (b), the code between 702559b and ae33227, is not the cause of the shift: 702559b misses like SP6b and like ae33227. Of the differences between the Task 50 runs and SP6a's committed files, what is left is the system under the same Chrome build: the kernel 7.0.0-42 → 7.0.0-43, installed on 2026-11-13 at 06:49, and in the same upgrade, two minutes later, glibc (`libc6` 2.39-0ubuntu8.9 → 8.12, `/var/log/dpkg.log`). Mesa and Chrome did not change; the other packages upgraded since 2026-11-10 are libnfs, libsoup, FreeRDP, perl, libgphoto2, GStreamer's base plugins, HPLIP, AMD GPU firmware (unused here), glibc's development and debug packages, linux-libc-dev, linux-tools-common, python3-pil and the terminal. `dpkg.log` records packages only: it does not exclude state outside dpkg (snaps, among them canonical-livepatch; BIOS/EC firmware; power settings) or conditions SP6a's evidence never recorded (turbo, the platform profile, EPP, whether HDMI-1-0 was connected). Booting 7.0.0-42 from GRUB tests the kernel alone; it keeps the new glibc. This is the fourth STOP: the result goes to the user (no gate loosened; the gate switch, the lock and the Threshold-log line stay uncommitted). The user booted 7.0.0-42 (ruling U10), below.

**The fifth check: kernel 7.0.0-42 (2026-11-16, U10).** The user booted 7.0.0-42-generic from GRUB's advanced options (glibc stays 2.39-0ubuntu8.12, so this boot tests the kernel alone). First the control: 702559b's bench once, from a fresh `git archive` into a `mktemp -d` directory in the scratchpad (`/tmp` was cleared by the reboot), `npm ci` from its own lockfile in a capped systemd scope, its own build and `measureChrome`, writing only into the scratch tree. 702559b did not reproduce its committed figures (below), so by the controller's relaunch rule for this check (the 702559b control first, then Step 1 on SP6b only if the control reproduces its committed figures; U10 itself states no condition) SP6b's routes were not re-run in full: SP6b's bench ran once for the side-by-side, from the working tree (its file copied out, then the third set's run 2 restored to `assets/sp6b/bench-iris-chrome.json` and checked with `cmp`). Checked read-only right before each run (each log opens with the output):
- `uname -r` 7.0.0-42-generic.
- PRIME on-demand.
- eDP-1 primary at 1920 × 1080, 240.00 Hz current. HDMI-1-0 disconnected, and no other output connected.
- Session 2 (seat0, x11) `LockedHint=no`.
- x86_pkg_temp 53 °C, the hottest zone 53-54 °C.
- 1-minute load 0.62 (702559b) and 0.61 (SP6b); the runner waited for ≤ 1.0, about 1.5 min each time after the run before. Free memory 12.8 GB; swap 0 MB before and after.

**What kernel 7.0.0-42 shows.** The kernel is not the cause. On 7.0.0-42, the kernel its committed files were recorded on, 702559b reads the same shift as on 7.0.0-43, within one run's spread: the X-ray refill 14.6 s against its committed 8.3 (on -43 14.9), the teleport refill 13.2 against 6.2 (13.3), the Apply full 10.4 against 5.0 (10.7), the reveal 4.70 against 3.75 (4.71), L7 / L8 main max 22.1 / 31.0 against 12.7 / 16.3. SP6b's run on 7.0.0-42 reads the third set's figures (refills 15.0 / 13.1 / 10.6 s, reveal 4.73 s), and once more misses only cells SP6a's code misses too under the Task 50 conditions (`mediumLiveOver33`, `editVisibleP95`, `replyDuringFlushP95Ms`, `ghostSliceMaxMs`, `pasteOver33`), while holding the X-ray cells 702559b misses. The figures of either code are not SP6b's cost. What is left, read-only (nothing was changed):
- **glibc 2.39-0ubuntu8.9 → 8.12** (`/var/log/apt/history.log`: unattended-upgrade, 2026-11-13 06:51, two minutes after the 7.0.0-43 kernel), still installed in this boot. Chrome's renderer and GPU process and the bench's workers run on it.
- **State outside dpkg.** Snaps refreshed after SP6a's committed run (2026-11-10 00:14), by the revision files' dates in `/var/lib/snapd/snaps/`: core22 (2026-11-11), core18 (2026-11-12) and code (2026-11-14); `snap changes` keeps no older change records. canonical-livepatch v11.1.0 (rev 418, file dated 2026-10-16, before the committed run) reports `nothing-to-apply` on 7.0.0-42, so no live patch is loaded. `fwupdmgr get-history`: no history; BIOS FB05 (2022-02-25); the EC's state cannot be read without root. Chrome's deb (155.0.8059.39, binary dated 2026-10-06), node v24.21.0 (nvm, 2026-09-08) and the system Mesa are unchanged; the mesa-2404 snap serves snaps only, not the deb Chrome.
- **Conditions SP6a's evidence never recorded**: turbo, the platform profile, EPP, the governor, the iGPU's clock cap (1,400 MHz now), and whether HDMI-1-0 was connected. In the five checks every single-worker bench run ends at a load of 3.45-6.10 (SP6a's committed 2.72).

HDMI-1-0's reverse-PRIME GPU cost (the second set) is the only measured cause so far; it moves the GPU figures, not the CPU-side shift. This is the fifth STOP: the result goes to the user (no gate loosened; the gate switch, the lock and the Threshold-log line stay uncommitted; `assets/sp6b/` holds the third set). The controller then found the cause, and the user reset the EC (U11), below.

**The cause: the EC's package-wide CPU cap (U11).** After the fifth STOP the controller checked the CPU with its own short busy processes (read-only otherwise), on 7.0.0-42. The firmware power limits read from MMIO RAPL were PL1 45 W and PL2 115 W. 20 busy threads held 2.3 GHz for about 8 s, then collapsed to 1.2-1.4 GHz at 57 °C. 6 busy threads were capped at 3.0 GHz. `package_throttle_count` rose from 337 to 687 in 16 s. The battery read 96 % "Not charging" on AC (the factory barrel charger, GNOME's performance profile). That is a package-wide cap asserted by the laptop's embedded controller (EC) without heat, the same cap SP5a met (its rulings R52, 2026-10-29). The user reset the EC: a full power-off, AC unplugged, the power button held 30-60 s, then a normal boot (2026-11-16 16:13, the default kernel 7.0.0-43-generic). The controller repeated the same 16 s check: 6 threads ran at 4.0-4.1 GHz (89-92 °C); 20 threads ran at 3.5-3.7 GHz for about 4 s (PL2), then at 2.2-2.4 GHz at 63-64 °C (PL1 45 W), with no collapse. So the cap was gone.

**The sixth set: after the EC reset (2026-11-16, U11).** Same code, 13da5ec (the commits since are this section's text), kernel 7.0.0-43-generic, HDMI-1-0 disconnected. That is the ordinary thermal limit at high temperature, and the clocks stay high through it (P-core max 2.4-4.6 GHz); the collapse the controller measured before the reset came at 57 °C.

Its L8 main max is 17.1 against 16.3. Its L7 main max is 18.8 against 12.7, a single frame; SP6b's runs read 11.3-13.6.
- **SP6b, the gated file (run 2), with Task 50's `g1.metric.ts`: 9 of 9 pass**, every earlier G1 cell, G1.enter and G2 re-read from `assets/sp6b/` and every new SP6b part: `cullMainMeanMs` 0.027, **`cullMainMaxMs` 0.580** (§8.5's 1.5 kept; Task 51a does not fire), `xrayBiomeP95` 4.165, `xrayBiomeOver33` 0, `xrayColorToggleMaxMs` 4.165; `caveMainP95` 2.22; `mediumFill4wMs` 5,011 (the w4 file); `enterRatio` 1.031. Run 1, read the same way (copied in for the read, then run 2 restored and checked with `cmp`): 9 of 9 pass. The w4 file holds every cell as well.

**What the EC reset shows.** The CPU-side shift every earlier set read was the EC's package-wide clock cap, not SP6b's code, SP6a's late code, the kernel, glibc or the uptime. With the cap gone, 702559b reproduces its committed figures, and SP6b's code reads them or better on every leg. The X-ray refill is 6.7-6.8 s (14.6-15.0 s under the cap), the teleport refill 6.0 s (13.1), the Apply full 4.2-4.3 s (10.4-10.6), the reveal 3.56-3.59 s (4.56-4.73), `editVisibleP95` 28-30 ms (44-51) and `replyDuringFlushP95Ms` 3.3-3.75 ms (5.07-7.20). The enter route's per-reload growth also returns to SP6a's 1.2 times. The SP6b X-ray refill now reads below 702559b's (6.7 against 8.2 s), with less X-ray geometry (19.7 against 21.7 MB of vertex bytes at RD 12). The single-frame stalls of the earlier sets (the 154-179 ms idle-main frames, the 2 s L13 freeze, the 167 ms Apply frame) do not recur in any of the four runs: the largest long frame is L0's first-reveal frame (50.0-62.5 ms, ungated, as in SP6a's committed file), and the only other is each run's 33.3 ms X-ray map-open frame. The live misses on the frame after the Apply or undo do not recur either: L7 / L8 main max 11.3-13.6 / 10.5-16.2 ms on SP6b, against 20-36 ms under the cap. Two causes were measured over the six checks:
- **The EC's package-wide CPU cap** (the controller's before and after figures above). It moves every CPU-side figure: the refills, the reveal, the edit and worker-reply latencies, and the main-thread cost of the Apply and undo frames.
- **HDMI-1-0's reverse-PRIME GPU cost** (the second set). It moves the GPU figures: with HDMI-1-0 connected the Iris Xe's GPU p95 rose on every leg (the flight 2.97 against 2.07 ms).

No gate was loosened, and no bound beyond the brief's was added: the gates are switched on this set as Task 50 specifies (the Threshold log's Task 50 line).

### The Firefox run (Task 52)

The user ran it by hand in their own Firefox 158 (`Mozilla/5.0 (X11; Ubuntu; Linux x86_64; rv:158.0) Gecko/20100101 Firefox/158.0`), so the shader cache was not controlled. The run took place on 2026-11-17, about 00:05-00:24, on the controller's `vite preview --port 4173 --strictPort` of b0293b6's build (`index-BkdAkf-i.js`). b0293b6's `src/` is 13da5ec's (`git diff 13da5ec b0293b6 -- src` is empty), the code of the Chrome cells' sixth set. Conditions:
- kernel 7.0.0-43-generic, PRIME on-demand;
- eDP-1 at 240 Hz, HDMI-1-0 disconnected (ruling U12);
- on AC after an EC reset (U12). The controller's 16 s CPU check ran right before: 6 threads at 4.1 GHz; 20 threads at 3.5 GHz, then 2.2-2.4 GHz with no collapse. So the cap was gone, as before the sixth set (U11);
- nothing else heavy ran.

Committed in `assets/sp6b/`:
- `selftest-firefox.json`, the user's pasted reading;
- `bench-iris-firefox.json`, byte-identical (`cmp`) to the user's download `bench-firefox(9).json` (saved 00:24).

Firefox's figures are recorded, not gated (§12.6). `g1.metric.ts` now reads this bench file from `assets/sp6b/` (`othersDir`): `dir` reads `assets/sp6b` in its `.out/G1.*.others.json` and `G2.*.others.json` records, and its 9 calls pass with the file in place. Firefox exposes no timer query (`timerQuery` false), so every `gpuMs` in the file is null: the stream, live and edit blocks, L13 with culling on and off, and L14's three segments. No GPU time is recorded for Firefox. The rest of the setup is as in SP6a's Firefox files: the window at 1760 × 990 and a device pixel ratio of 1.09, `displayIntervalMs` 4.14, the masked renderer string `Intel(R) HD Graphics, or similar`, `sectionRenderer` `mesh` (RegionMesh), `multiDraw` false, 6 workers. Times in ms.

- **`?selftest=1`** (§12.8): 65/65. Every key's expected equals its actual and its entry in `test/goldens.json` (`generatorVersion` 9; sp1 27, sp2a 20, sp3a 3, sp3b 2, sp3c 2, sp3e 5, sp6a 3, sp6b 3). The page's second section read `IndexedDB contract: 10/10 passed`. The file holds only the goldens, so the contract's result is the user's reading.
- **`?bench=medium`** (recorded): every leg L0-L14 ran, `timedOut` is empty in the live, cave and X-ray blocks, and `stoppedBy` is null. The reveal took 4,247. L0's two long frames are the first reveal's, 146.1 and 173.9 ms with main ≤ 0.3 ms (ungated; SP6a's Firefox file 75.0 and 129.3).

**L13 with RegionMesh's region culling** (D23, §8.4; ruling P58's culled sections). Frame p50, p95 and p99 8.28, max 12.42, `caveOver33` 0, `caveMainP95` 0.96. `cullMainMeanMs` 0.013 and `cullMainMaxMs` 0.70 (Chrome's gates 1.0 and 1.5; the culling-off segment, where the walk does not run, 0.006 and 0.16). 0.37 walks per second (Chrome run 2: 0.40). The finder hit Chrome's entrance (20408, 198, −4795) in 16.5 ms, the route walked 258 blocks, 3 growth events, `gpuMs` null. **Culled sections: p50 0, max 0** on Firefox, against p50 71 and max 176 in Chrome run 2 (the gated file) on the same route, seed, profile and RD. Read from the code (nothing run):
- **What the figure counts on each backend.** On RegionBatch (Chrome) it counts the entries the filter dropped from three's per-frame draw lists after three's per-instance frustum culling. Each entry is one section (`regionBatch.ts:51-55`; pinned in `regionBatch.test.ts:761`). On RegionMesh (Firefox) it counts the rows of the shown spans in the terrain and water arenas of culled regions (`culledRows`, `regionMesh.ts:400-410`), counted only while some region is culled (`:739`). A region is culled only on a re-apply frame where no section of any of its live columns (at most 64 columns × 24 sections) is drawn: `culled = !k.drawAll && !regionDrawn(g, k)` (`:394`, `regionDrawn` `:366-375`). A section is drawn when it carries the last completed walk's stamp or `drawAll` is set (`caveCulling.ts:435-436`). `renderer.test.ts:833-861` pins the count on both backends with only section (0, 4, 0) drawn. RegionMesh counts 3, the terrain rows of the region none of whose sections is drawn; the region holding the drawn section keeps its arenas drawn and counts nothing. RegionBatch counts 4: those 3 rows plus that region's one row the walk did not reach. So a culled region holding shown spans would show in the figure.
- **The culling ran.** The walk completed 0.37 walks a second (0 in the culling-off segment). The walk slice and the re-apply took 0.013 ms per frame on average, against 0.006 ms with culling off. So the region verdicts were taken. On no recorded frame of the culling-on segment was a region with a shown terrain or water span wholly undrawn: each kept at least one drawn section. (A culled region whose arenas hold no shown span counts 0, and such an arena draws nothing either way.)
- **Chrome's 71 and 176 do not contradict it.** They are single sections that lie in regions which may still hold drawn sections, and RegionBatch takes no region verdict. The bench records no per-region figure, so how close any region came to being wholly undrawn on this route is not measured.
- **Triangles.** p50 2,471,295 and max 2,620,319 with culling on; 2,604,605 and 2,682,419 off. With no region culled, the difference is not culling's: the off segment walks the route's first 20 s again while the world streams (`bench.ts`, L13). The figure is not comparable with Chrome's (p50 303,527). RegionMesh draws each region arena whole, its range up to the high-water mark (`applyDrawRange`, `regionMesh.ts:362-365`), after three culls the region's bounds. RegionBatch draws per section after three's per-section frustum culling.

**L14 with the biome segment** (recorded; `gpuMs` on and off null in each segment):

So the leg as a whole reads `over33` 4. `xrayCulledSections` 0: the culler walks nothing and sets `drawAll` while the X-ray is on (`caveCulling.ts`' header).
- **Against SP6a's Firefox file:** RD 12 p95 12.72, `over33` 1, max 45.58; RD 16 p95 16.96, `over33` 1, max 59.36; GPU MB 217.8 and 331.3; `xrayVertexBytes` 41,468,544 and 64,219,392.

### The review links and screenshots (Task 56)

**The poses** (§12.8, D14; rulings P17, P59, P60): `assets/sp6b/caveBiomes.examples.json` merges the quick tier's `.out/caveBiomes.examples.json` (the biomes group: `lush`, `dripstone`, `abyss`, `biomeBorder`, `caveHeavySpawn`, by Task 27's rule above as ruling U13 re-picked the three biome poses) with `bottomCavern` from `.out/caves.examples.json` (the default group: Task 21's separate field, the component with the most floor faces at y ≤ −40, re-picked after Task 25's levers), both written by `caves.metric.ts`' quick run (`bottomCavern` at bcafcf2, the biomes group's re-run after U13's rule; `GENERATOR_VERSION` 9; no generated output has changed since the generator push). `bottomCavern` gains its profile (the default, its file's top level). `uiSmoke.ts` mirrors the six as `SP6B_POSES` (each with its profile), which `uiSmoke.test.ts` keeps equal to the committed file; Task 55's `LUSH_POSE` is pinned equal to its `lush` entry. SP6a's `CAVE_POSES` and `CAVE_AT` are unchanged.

**The links.** Each opens seed 42 on its pose's profile (the hash of the map state with that profile; `cave_heavy`'s spawn on `cave_heavy`) in flight at RD 12, `y` the eye's, yaw and pitch as `hereUrl` writes them. `at` takes integers, so the eye stands on a corner of the pose's voxel: `SP6B_AT`, by SP6a's `CAVE_AT` rule on the pose's own profile (`solidAt` in `uiSmoke.test.ts`). One pose has no corner the rule accepts in the eye's row (`SP6B_RAISED`: `biomeBorder`, whose voxel is the floor of a one-wide slot under an open cave, every corner with rock beside the eye): its link raises the eye one block, to y −50.5, into the cave, and takes the rule's corner in that row (the voxel above is open). The three biome poses have every corner clear by U13's rule, so each opens at its voxel's low corner. `bottomCavern`'s link opens at (186, −22), not at the low corner (185, −22), where Tasks 24-25's shots stood on the face of the x = 184 deepslate column and the near plane showed the clear colour (the light-blue patch); at (186, −22) the four voxels of the eye's row are open, and neither bottom shot has the patch.

**The screenshots** (`SP6B_SHOTS`, 1400 × 900, in `assets/sp6b/`; SwiftShader; every world page at RD 12 after the stream settled, the counts hidden except in `review-hud-strip.png`; the lush, dripstone and abyss shots and the three from the dripstone pose re-taken at U13's poses, the others kept from the first run, their poses unchanged):
- `review-abyss.png`: the abyss at the light floor. Almost black: sampled every 50 px (the hotbar and the pointer prompt left out), the brightest channel reads 1-13 (median 2), as §4 expects.
- `review-abyss-night-vision.png`: the same view with night vision: abyss_ash floors and ledges, the walls and ceiling dark abyss_stone and cracked_abyss_stone all round (sampled: ash 92, 88, 86 on the floor, 24-38 on the walls and ceiling).
- `review-lush.png` (night vision): moss_block on the ceiling, the walls and the floor's ledges, stone showing between, and clay patches on the floor in front of the eye (the pale blocks at the bottom right, clay's flat colour 160, 166, 180 read in the PNG: the floor, not the near plane).
- `review-dripstone.png` (night vision): a long low cave with dripstone_block floor and ceiling, its layers along the left wall, deepslate showing through them.
- `review-biome-border.png` (night vision): plain cave into the abyss: stone ahead, dark abyss rock and an abyss_shale floor patch at the left.
- `review-bottom-before.png`: the `bottomCavern` pose in a build of 441b67d (`GENERATOR_VERSION` 8): `git archive 441b67d` into a fresh `mktemp -d` directory, `npm ci && npm run build` there, its own `vite preview` on a free port, the same page query and capture steps (night vision, the counts hidden; a scratch copy of the smoke tool, not committed). The cave falls away in deepslate steps down a gully, towards the bedrock plane below the view.
- `review-bottom-after.png`: the same pose today (night vision): the rock floor's mounds and gullies, 1-3-block steps, with dripstone in the ceiling beyond; the hook read the eye at (186, −38.5, −22) on both builds.
- `review-xray-biome.png`: the X-ray at every depth by cave biome from the dripstone pose pitched 20° up (`SP6B_XRAY_PITCH`): dripstone's orange all round, the abyss's violet below at the left and the right, plain cave's grey-blue above; the strip and its five-entry legend.
- `review-hud-strip.png`: the same view with F3's counts: the HUD at the left and the strip at the right of their top container (§9.5), apart.
- `review-slice-biome3d.png`: "Cave slice" by cave biome from the dripstone pose: the Density tab's horizontal Cave biome field at y 0, 256 blocks, plain, dripstone and abyss cave air in the solid.
- `review-map-cave-biome.png`: `?map`'s caveBiome layer over the biome region (`SP6B_MAP`: centre (−1536, 1536), 4 blocks/px) at y −32: lush, dripstone and abyss zones with wavy, speckled edges on plain cave; the shot waits until the view stops changing (two captures 1 s apart equal).

**Reviewed** for straight borders, flat cuts, the floor's relief and the abyss's darkness: no straight or grid-like border in the map layer or the X-ray; no flat cut in the cave shots; the bottom cavern ends on mounds and gullies, not on a plane; the abyss at the light floor is dark. Each biome shot shows its skin (ruling U13): the first run's poses, Task 27's first floor voxel in the grid's index order, lay at their zones' edges (within 12 blocks, a count through the hook in a 25 × 17 × 25 box: lush 106 moss_block and no clay, dripstone 104 dripstone_block, the abyss 2,955 abyss rock, each among 4,534-9,851 deepslate), and U13's poses hold, of the 15,625 voxels within 12 blocks (the rule's own count over the T-stage grid), lush 647 moss_block and 320 clay, dripstone 2,155 dripstone_block, the abyss 15,366 abyss rock. No shot has a near-plane patch: every biome pose's corner has its four voxels open (the rule's 3 × 3), and the one pale area, in `review-lush.png`, is clay.

**The browser smoke** (`npm run build`, then `node test/tools/uiSmoke.ts --skip-build --profile-dir <mktemp -d /tmp/wi10-XXXX> --shots <mktemp -d /tmp/wi10-shots-XXXX>`, its own `vite preview` on a free port and headless Chrome through SwiftShader; 8 min 5 s): 635/635 checks pass, every step of `SMOKE_STEPS` ran (42), 0 page errors; `?selftest=1` `✓ all 65 goldens match (11.8 s)` and `IndexedDB contract: 10/10 passed`. Re-run after U13's poses (8 min 8 s): 635/635, all 42 steps, 0 page errors, the same selftest and IndexedDB lines; Task 55's checks at the new lush pose: the pixel (1200, 820) reads rgb 37, 100, 60 by cave biome (lush) and 97, 54, 28 by cave type; "Nearest lush cave" from (−1679, 220, 1744) finds (−1696, 116, 1738), 105 blocks; the map's caveBiome layer on `CAVE_MAP` (centre (−1678, 1750)) not lush at y 160 and lush at y 48.
